# BIRM — Buildable Spec

**Status: §1–§10 all carry ledger rows.** R4 is satisfiable and the organ and bus statuses in §3 and §4 are lifted accordingly. A section with no ledger rows is not "done", it is "asserted". What §10 licenses is bounded by §10.5, and the two sections must be read together: **§10 can refute; it cannot license** — the claim rests on §2.2's declared priors and §11's ledger of decisions, not on a score.

> **What this document is.** One buildable specification of BIRM: an agent-side controller that receives observation vectors and emits action vectors, with everything benchmark-specific pushed into an adapter. It is written so that a later reading wave can *replace* a filled cell rather than append to it — see §11's `Supersedes` column and §13.
>
> **What it is not.** Not a literature review (that is `wiki/`), not a wish list (that is `wiki/architectural-gaps.md`). A row belongs here only if it is implementable as written or explicitly marked `OPEN-SLOT` in §12.

---

## 0. Reading guide

> **Naming, from wave 18.** Organs were single letters (`M`, `S`, `C`, `X`, `N`, `A0`) through wave 17 and are **brain regions** from wave 18 on. Signals, buses and variables stay symbols. Nothing here is a design decision — this section names what §1–§13 already say and creates no ledger row.

### 0.1 What BIRM is, in five lines

| # | |
|---|---|
| 1 | A **controller**: one observation vector in, one action index out, per environment step. Everything benchmark-specific — pixels, key codes, reward shaping — sits in an **adapter** outside it (§2). |
| 2 | Its bet: the world is a **latent graph**. Which states exist, which actions are edges between them, and where the goal sits are all unobserved. Learning = recovering that graph; acting = navigating it (§1). |
| 3 | The representation is split in two: **`g` = where I am** — an address integrated from actions alone, architecturally forbidden to read the observation — and **`x` = what is here** (content). §6 installs the split, §4 keeps the two on separate wires. |
| 4 | One step (§5.1): integrate `g` → read the fast store at that address → predict the content → admit the observation → settle → **commit** (one winner publishes to every organ at once) → write the store → emit an action. |
| 5 | Everything the *designer* chose rather than the system learned is enumerated in §2.2 and is the **denominator** of every score in §10. |

### 0.2 Organs — the six blocks and their former letters

| Organ | Was | Job in one line | Timescale | Why this brain region | Falsifier |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Cortex** (neocortex) | `M` | the **slow model**: the meta-graph — an action-conditioned predictor of the next latent, holding the code vocabulary and the associative weights over it | slow; written only on the offline transport channel | the consolidated, slowly-written store of what generalises across episodes | `F-Cortex` |
| **Hippocampus** | `S` | the **fast instance store**: one-shot writes of `(Δ, a, r)` conjunctions, addressed by `g`, erased at `done` | one-shot write, scheduled read | one-shot episodic binding filed under a path-integrated (grid-like) address | `F-Hipp` |
| **PFC** (prefrontal cortex) | `C` | the **controller**: holds a task model as sustained state and emits it as *bias* into organs that are already competing; issues the store's write-enable, address and lead time | fast, per step; state persists across steps | task sets, updating and shifting, sustained delay activity | `F-PFC1`, `F-PFC2` |
| **Thalamus** | `X` | the **arbitrator**: sets mode (input-driven vs. internally generated) and which control level is live, via two gains over *edges*; owns the commit gate onto **W** | fast switch, slow set-point | thalamic gating/ignition of a cortex-wide broadcast; routes, never holds content | `F-Thal` |
| **Neuromodulators** (VTA / LC / raphe / basal forebrain) | `N` | the **metaparameter source**: computes gains, precisions and learning rates from the learner's own second-order statistics — eight laws, no shared objective | second-order, slower than what it gates | dopamine / noradrenaline / serotonin / acetylcholine setting `δ`, `β`, `γ`, `α` | `F-NM`, `F-NM2` |
| **Periphery** (adapter) | `A0` | codec + affect channel. **Not part of BIRM** — declared, not tested; its content is §2.2 | fixed | sensory transduction and the body, outside the modelled brain | none — it is the denominator, not a claim |

**Organ or tissue.** A capitalised, code-formatted name (`Hippocampus`, `PFC`) is always BIRM's organ. Lower-case anatomy in a cited result (hippocampus, mPFC, entorhinal) is the biology the row is arguing from, and the two are only as related as the row says.

**Six organs, no seventh** (§3.6): no competence estimator, no detector of conflict/difficulty/error/novelty, no transfer path out of PFC, no feedback into perception.

### 0.3 Buses — still symbols, with what each one is

| Bus | Carries | Read it as |
|---|---|---|
| **O** | the observation `o_t` | the sensory afferent. Denied to `g`, to PFC at every level, and to phase α of the step |
| **Bg** | the structural code `g` — *the context wire* | the "where" stream: an address, relayed, low-passed, content-free |
| **Bx** | the content code `x` — *the direct wire*, with registers `Bx⁻` (predicted) and `Bx⁺` (observed) | the "what" stream: tuned, time-locked, causally dominant |
| **U** | the metaparameter vector `m_t` | the ascending modulatory projection — every organ decodes its own gains from it, and none reads it raw |
| **V** | one scalar `r_t` plus Cortex's residual, **with no address** | the reward/error broadcast. Its address comes from `L_part`, never from the signal |
| **L** | `L_cmd` (address, lead time, erase type) and `L_part` (each organ's licence on its own participation) | the write-mask and the credit path. `L_part` never touches the action path |
| **W** | the published content of one commit — exclusive, plus fields `scope` and `generated` | the global workspace. The payload is a **copy, never a transform**. Note: bold **W** is this bus; `W` in backticks is Cortex's weight matrix — different objects |
| **Act** | the action code, one-hot over `k` | the motor efferent. Read by the adapter only |

### 0.4 Symbols

| Group | Symbols |
|---|---|
| Per step | `o_t` observation · `a_t` action (one-hot over `k`) · `r_t` scalar affect · `done` episode-end bit · `m⁻`/`m⁺` minus/plus-phase belief · `Δ = m⁺ − m⁻`, the written conjunction's content half |
| Codes | `g` structural code / store address · `x` content code · `W_a = f_g(a)` the per-action operator, `f_g` the generator that emits it · `W` Cortex's slow weights · `b` the effect-equivalence bottleneck code |
| Metaparameters (all from Neuromodulators, on **U**) | `m_t` the vector itself · `γ` discount, and it appears **once**, in `δ = r + γV(t) − V(t−1)` · `β` exploration temperature · `α` learning rate · `δ` prediction error · `gain_j` level `j`'s engagement gain · `g_rel`/`g_acq` the two Thalamus gains · `scope`, `generated` the two **W** fields |
| Margins and residuals | `ρ` path-invariance residual (`‖g_int − g_rec‖₂ / ‖g_int‖₂`) · `ρ_tr` conditional action excitation — a property of the **collector** · `γ_rep` the weakest predictable direction — a property of the **environment** · `ε_t(a)` the epistemic bonus (§8) |
| Sizes | `D_o`, `D_g`, `D_x`, `D_u` bus widths · `k` action-code width · `K` module width · `Ŝ` assumed state count · `n_mod = ⌈log_K Ŝ⌉` · `N_slots = c·n_mod` store slots · `θ` skill threshold · `θ_upd`, `θ_shift` PFC's updating and shifting components |

### 0.5 Identifier prefixes

| Prefix | Means | Lives in |
|---|---|---|
| `D<n>` | a **spec decision**, with status `ASSUMED` / `SOURCED` / `MEASURED` / `CONTESTED` | §11 |
| `H1`–`H10` | **declared hardwiring** — what a human fixed. The denominator of every score | §2.2 |
| `O<n>` | an **open slot**: a `?` with an address | §12 |
| `T1`–`T35` | an **acceptance test** | §10 |
| `S1`–`S5` | the **scoring-discipline rules** | §1 |
| `P0`–`P3` | **training-pipeline stages** | §9 |
| `F-<organ>` / `F-<bus>` | that organ's or bus's **falsifier** | §3, §4 → §10 |
| `L1`–`L10` | what §10 **cannot** do | §10.5 |
| `G<n>` | an **architectural gap** in the wiki | `wiki/architectural-gaps.md` |
| `T<n>` above `T35` | an **empirical tension** in the wiki | `wiki/empirical-tensions.md` |
| `I1`–`I25` | a **certification instrument** from the external inventory | §10.0 menu |
| `F1`–`F14` | a **failure mode** an instrument carries with it | §10.0 |
| `C1`–`C5` | a **counterexample class** an instrument does not rule out | §10.0, §10.5 |

**Three letters are overloaded; the section decides which is meant.** `T` — §10 tests below `T35`, wiki tensions above it. `E` — §8's `E1`–`E4` are the four candidate epistemic quantities, while §10's `E1`/`E2` are two protocol pre-commitments (the evoker control and the paired-frame null). `L` — the licensing bus, §10.5's limits, and §13's backlog bands, all at once.

### 0.6 Reading order

| If you want | Read |
|---|---|
| whether the claim is worth anything | §1 (what is being claimed), §2.2 (what was given away), §10.5 (what no test can settle) |
| how it works | §3 (the organs) → §5.1 (one step, line by line) → §6 (why `g` and `x` are two things) |
| how to build it | §3 detail sheets → §4 (wiring) → §5 → §9 (pipeline) → §12 (what is still `?`) |
| why a cell says what it says | §11 only. Body sections state the settled claim and the ledger id; the argument is never in the body (R6) |

## Editing rules

| Rule | Statement |
|---|---|
| R1 | Every design decision gets a ledger id `D<n>` in §11 with a status token: `ASSUMED` (no source, my choice) / `SOURCED` (a wiki page says it) / `MEASURED` (a number exists) / `CONTESTED` (a tension row disputes it). |
| R2 | A decision may only be replaced by a decision that names it in `Supersedes` and says what new evidence forced the change. Deleting a row silently is forbidden. |
| R3 | Any quantity with no number is written `?` and appears in §12. Never write a plausible number. |
| R4 | Every organ (§3) and every bus (§4) must have at least one falsifier in §10 before it is called specified. |
| R5 | Prose is capped: tables, equations, numbered steps. If a paragraph is needed, it is a symptom that a decision is missing. |
| R6 | **§1–§10 state the settled claim only**, plus the bare ledger id. What a decision supersedes, why it changed, and what an earlier wave assumed live in §11 and §13 and nowhere else. A body cell that argues for itself is a defect. |

## 1. Scope

| Slot | Fill |
|---|---|
| Target task set (three-component framing: name it or No-Free-Lunch applies) | The **interactive, transition-sampled slice of the abstract-reasoning AI Set**: an agent emitting actions into an environment family, recovering structure from `(obs, action, obs')` triples. In the latent-variable bit-vector: **edge existence, edge label, path and goal node latent**; node content given by the adapter. **The edge vocabulary's size is not given**: BIRM receives an index into an action code of width `k`, and `k` is the adapter's *encoding* of the repertoire, not a count of the family's edge labels. `k` is charged at `H2` and `H10` and never read as knowledge of the graph. **General rule:** anything solvable by a series of reasoning steps can be adapted into an agent emitting actions into an environment family, with structure recovered from `(obs, action, obs')` triples; how `o` and `a` come to be is the adapter's responsibility. The interactive slice is BIRM's **chosen instantiation**, not a claim about which problems have this shape. Static input→output formats instantiate the rule through an adapter supplying a deterministic `apply(a, o) → o'` plus an authored vocabulary, edge-label-latent becoming path-latent. `D2`, `D129`, `D130` |
| Benchmarks that instantiate it | Primary: `[[wiki/entities/arc-agi-3.md]]` — interactive, action semantics and goal both withheld, and its scoring function `S = min(h/a, 1.15)²` is already the efficiency ratio rather than a side column. Secondary: a **repurposed-skill family** (train on game `X`, evaluate on unpublished variants `X₁…Xₙ` built so `X`'s optimal program is not optimal for `Xᵢ`) — the environment-family identifiability condition turned into an instrument. Control: an **arbitrary sensorimotor mapping** task at `k ≥ 3` alternatives. `D3` |
| Explicitly out of scope — **one kind only** | **(v) Unambiguously-observed environments**: families in which `o_t` identifies the latent state on its own. There, aliasing is absent, `g` has nothing to compensate for, and the belief code this spec is built around is not needed. **Intermediate observation ambiguity is a precondition of the task set, not a hazard of the encoding.** This is the *only* exclusion by problem type; it is `MEASURED` (`D84`) and it is **per-adapter, not per-benchmark**. Static input→output formats (ARC-AGI-1/2, PGM, Raven) are **in scope**: an adapter carrying a deterministic `apply(a, o) → o'` re-encodes them inside the taxonomy, and what that adapter installs is charged as `H10`; a given instance may still fall out under (v), since a fully-rewritten observation *is* the state. Natural language is excluded at §2.1 by the stream contract. The non-embeddable symbolic slice and rewrite-search sit in the non-goals row below. `D84`, `D126` |
| Success statement (one sentence, falsifiable) | On held-out instance-graphs of a **seen** environment family, BIRM reaches skill threshold `θ` in fewer environment steps than a flat baseline of matched parameter count, **and** its advantage survives when performance is plotted against *nodes visited* rather than *edges taken* — the `O(E) → O(V)` gap being the operational definition of "it has the meta-graph". `D5` |
| Non-goals inherited from wiki (things known unbuildable today) | **Constructing the problem representation** — deciding what gets to be a variable (`G73`) — and **choosing a parse** of the input (`G75`): both supplied by the adapter and charged in §2.2. **Deriving the agent/world boundary** (`G92`): drawn by hand, declared, not inferred. **A computable `GD`** (`G31`): approximated in §10, never computed. **In scope by problem type, unmechanised in fact:** the **non-embeddable symbolic slice** (`G11`, `OPEN`, hardness 10) and **rewrite-search** (`T229`) — BIRM has no mechanism for either. `D6`, `D126` |

**Where the narrowing lives** (`D126`). §1 narrows the task set by problem type only under (v); the No-Free-Lunch obligation is discharged in **§2.2, not §1**. An adapter that authors its own action vocabulary, or re-granulates one it was handed (`H10`), carries a strictly larger denominator than one passing the environment's primitives through at the granularity it received them. A broad §1 is read together with an itemised §2.2.

**Scoring discipline forced by the task set** (not optional; `D7`):

**Why these are rules and not preferences.** §1 measures how fast BIRM learns something new, not how well it performs. That target can be faked two ways — **the developer already knew the answer** and built it in, or **the system practised until it memorised the space**. Each rule below blocks one of the two. Formally, the measure is roughly `(generalization) / (priors + experience)`: priors and experience are the denominator, not context around it, so a line that omits them is not a weak number but no number.

| Rule | Statement | Blocks |
|---|---|---|
| S1 | **Report the cost.** A score means nothing without the compute budget that bought it: 80% in ten minutes and 80% in a month are different claims. Cost per task appears on every reported line or the line is void. Where a line is used to argue something about the *representation*, the budget must be **swept, not just reported** (`D187`) — at a single budget, "the structure is there but takes time to amortise" and "the structure is missing" look identical. | practice |
| S2 | **No infinite test problems.** Exclude any benchmark whose evaluation set comes from a generator: unlimited puzzles means unlimited training, and dense sampling reaches threshold without discovering anything. The experience term `E` is capped and reported. | practice |
| S3 | **The evaluation set is consumable.** Every time a score is read and the design is then tweaked, a little of the answer leaks from the evaluation set into the designer and from there into the system — developer knowledge arriving in instalments. So: a scratch set for diagnostic scoring, a disjoint set touched once for final scoring, and a log of how many times each has been read. | prior knowledge |
| S4 | **List what was hardwired before quoting a score.** Everything a human decided for BIRM — observation width, action count, how the world was chopped into chunks — is enumerated in §2.2 (`H1`–`H10`) *before* any number appears. That table is the denominator; without it nobody can tell how much of the "learning" was the designer. This is why `H3` and `H10` sitting at `?` blocks *interpreting* §10, not *building* BIRM. | prior knowledge |
| S5 | **Run the dumb control every round.** The control is an arbitrary sensorimotor mapping at `k ≥ 3`: fixed stimulus→action pairs with no pattern to find, solvable only by memorising them. Run it alongside the real benchmark every evaluation round. A "reasoning" score that does not clearly exceed it measured **binding speed**, not inference. `k = 2` is forbidden because a Repeat-Stay/Change-Shift heuristic — repeat what just worked, otherwise switch — solves a two-alternative control outright, and a control beaten by one line certifies nothing. | neither — it is a floor under the numerator |

---

## 2. Boundary declaration — BIRM vs. adapter

> The agent/world boundary is drawn by hand (`G92`), and whatever is placed in the adapter is the part of the problem that was *not solved*. An undeclared adapter makes every score in §10 uninterpretable.

### 2.1 Adapter contract

| Interface | Spec | Fixed across benchmarks? |
|---|---|---|
| Observation stream(s): count, dimension, rate, dtype, value range | **One** stream. `o_t ∈ ℝ^{D_o}`, float32, range `[0,1]`, exactly one vector per environment step. `D_o` is a per-benchmark constant declared in §2.2, never inferred and never varying within a family. `D8` | Contract fixed; `D_o` **not** fixed — it is hardwiring `H1` |
| Action code: dimension, discrete/continuous, emission rate | **Discrete**, one-hot over `k` primitives, one emission per environment step, no no-op suppression. `k` declared per benchmark. The *semantics* of each primitive are latent to BIRM — the adapter passes indices, never labels, and BIRM is never told what `k` counts (`D129`): the same repertoire admits many widths, so `k` is an encoding width and not the family's branching factor. `D9` | Contract fixed; `k` **not** fixed — hardwiring `H2` |
| Primary affect channel (the "kickstarter") — signal, range, latency | Scalar `r_t ∈ [−1, 1]`, delivered on the **same step** as `o_t`, no shaping, no per-benchmark rescaling. Deliberately *one* scalar with **no address**: it cannot name what it is about. **Two consumers at two orders** (`D70`): `V` reads it per step (addressed by `L_part`, §4), and `Neuromodulators` reads its *running statistics* as a standing disposition — a multiplicative gate on whether a class of computation is run at all, not a term added to the return | Yes |
| Episode/reset signalling | Explicit boolean `done_t` on the same step. BIRM carries **no state across `done`** except slow weights `W` — the fast store `Hippocampus` and the structural code `g` are re-initialised, `g` to a **random phase** so a new environment de-aliases against all previous ones. `D11` | Yes |
| **What BIRM assumes about the stream** — not rules imposed on the adapter | BIRM is written as if: (a) the observation is **unparsed** — no objects, entities or slots are marked in it; (b) **no goal signal** arrives, and nothing beyond `r_t` correlates with distance to one; (c) **no action semantics** arrive — no labels, grouping or mask; (d) `D_o` and `k` **do not vary** within a declared family; (e) **no state crosses `done`**; (f) the stream is **not re-ordered, filtered or denoised**. How an adapter arranges to satisfy these is its own business and outside this spec. A family whose adapter cannot satisfy them is out of scope under `D4`, not a spec failure. `D83`. <br><br>**Natural language, as either input or output, is excluded here rather than in §1** (`D126`, re-siting `D4`(iii)): it is not a claim that language is a different problem type, but that a language channel cannot meet (a) unparsed and (d) fixed `D_o` under `D8`, and imports a prior §2.2 has no way to itemise — so any score quoted over it is uninterpretable under S4. | — |

### 2.2 Declared-hardwiring ledger (mandatory)

Every bit of task knowledge that lives outside the learner, itemised. This is the denominator of every claim in §10.

> **The conservation law that makes this section load-bearing.** Relational content is conserved between *inside a unit* and *between units*: enlarging the units deletes edges by swallowing them, shrinking them multiplies edges without adding information. The choice does **not** converge as granularity is refined. So whatever discretisation the adapter imposes has already absorbed an unmeasured part of the graph BIRM is scored on discovering. `H3` is therefore the denominator of the whole spec, and it is currently unquantified. `D13`

| # | Hardwired thing | Where it lives | Why it cannot be learned | Bits/parameters |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| H1 | Observation dimension `D_o`, dtype, value range | adapter | Fixing the stream's width is prior to any learning over it | `?` — §12 `O1` |
| H2 | Action code **width** `k` (semantics excluded) | adapter | The agent cannot emit an action it has no code for; the code width must precede the first step | `log₂ k` per step, plus `k` declared once. **Not a measurement of the family** (`D129`): `k` is what the adapter's encoding chose, and *which* repertoire it chose at *which* granularity is `H10` |
| H3 | **The discretisation implicit in the encoding** — what counts as one observation, and therefore what counts as one node | adapter | Nothing in the wiki constructs a representation (`G73`) or chooses a parse (`G75`); this spec does not attempt it | `?` — **unquantified and load-bearing**; §12 `O2`. Partially *measurable*: `D71` installs a second, agent-side discretisation whose criterion comes from outside the observation stream, so the residual is the composition of the two rather than one opaque choice |
| H4 | Episode boundaries (`done_t`) | adapter | An episode boundary is a claim about which experiences may bind together; it is not recoverable from the stream at the rate BIRM would need it | 1 bit/step |
| H5 | Existence and sign convention of the affect channel `r_t` | adapter | The agent cannot derive that a scalar is *good* from the scalar itself | 1 scalar/step + sign convention |
| H6 | The **structural code's manifold topology** (which group the action displacements act in) | BIRM | Assumed by the designer, not learned — see §6 | `?` — §12 `O3` |
| H7 | Two-timescale split: which parameters are slow `W` and which are fast `Cortex` | BIRM | The split is a sample-complexity decomposition chosen in advance; nothing in the wiki discovers it | parameter counts, §3 |
| H9 | **Declared state budget `Ŝ`** — an order-of-magnitude estimate of the family's latent state count, declared once per family | BIRM (declared at instantiation) | The true state count is exactly what is latent; nothing can size an organ from a quantity it is trying to discover. `D_o` and `k` are encoding widths and do not track problem scale — a 4-action maze and a 4-action puzzle share `k` and differ by orders of magnitude | one order-of-magnitude figure per family; every organ dimension in §3 is a function of it (`D85`) |
| H10 | **Authorship *and granularity* of the action vocabulary** — which repertoire the `k` codes stand for, and at which grain. Two independent choices: whether the primitives are invented by the adapter or supplied by the environment, and — supplied or not — whether a parameterised primitive is passed as one code or enumerated into many (`D131`) | adapter | Choosing the primitives is choosing the edge set, and `H3`'s conservation law applies unchanged with edges in place of nodes: coarse primitives swallow the search into single moves, fine primitives multiply it without adding information, and the choice does **not** converge as the vocabulary is refined. Nothing in the wiki constructs a representation (`G73`) or chooses a parse (`G75`), and neither does this spec | `?` — **unquantified; blocking for interpreting §10 on any adapter-authored family, not for building**; §12 `O42`. **Never zero** (`D131`): `[[wiki/entities/arc-agi-3.md]]` supplies its primitives, but `ACTION6(x, y)` is 1 code or 4096 depending on the adapter, so its charge is small-but-nonzero; **large and unmeasured** for any format re-encoded under `D126`/`D130`. `D128`, `D131` |
| H8 | **The assumption that the family's actions compose at all** — distinct from `H6`, which is *which* group they compose in | BIRM | No test decides whether an observed action set has closure (`G41`); the precondition is a property of the domain and is checked by nobody. §7's first row makes it an *operational* test rather than a structural one, but the assumption is still made before the first step | 1 bit per family, declared; §7 `D78` |

**No content priors are installed** (`D80`). The wiki's enumerated candidate set — objects, agents, number, geometry, forms, social beings — is *structure*, not encoding, so none of it may be pushed into the adapter: an entry-gated prior sits above perceptual representations and constrains which edges may exist, which is exactly BIRM's job and not Periphery's. BIRM declines the whole inventory: in an arbitrary world any world-specific commitment is a bet that can be wrong, and a wrong prior is worse than no prior (`T211`). `H6` and `H8` are then BIRM's *only* installed structural bets, and they are installed in the one pattern the biology supports — **format installed, resolution learned** (the number system's ratio limit tightens 2.0 → 1.15–1.3 while the ratio-limited form never changes). The price is stated openly: a bet has no withdrawal mechanism here, and nothing in the wiki supplies one.

**Organ sizes are functions, not constants** (`D85`). No dimension in §3 is a fixed number: with `K` the per-module code size, the structural module count is `n_mod = ⌈log_K Ŝ⌉`, the store's carrier is `N_slots = c·n_mod` (linear in module count, by `D17`'s capacity result, while addressable states go as `∏ᵢ Kᵢ ≈ Ŝ`), and the codec-adjacent widths `f_g` and the action head follow `k`, the observation-side encoder follows `D_o`. A benchmark is therefore plugged in by declaring `⟨D_o, k, Ŝ⟩` and re-instantiating, not by re-tuning. The two free scalars left are `K` and `c`; `Ŝ` is the only *new* prior, and it is charged as `H9`. This closes §12 `O12` and opens `O32` (how `Ŝ` is estimated for an unfamiliar family).

**Two discretisations in series, both committed, neither ranked** (`D209`). `H3` is the adapter's and `D71`'s codebook is BIRM's, and each is fixed before the graph exists: `D8`'s contract admits exactly one `o_t` per step, so a second reading of the same step has no port to arrive on, and P0's codebook is frozen in P1 (`D203`). The **carry-several** policy is therefore unrepresentable here rather than rejected, and what a wrong commitment costs is `O2` at the adapter and `O25` at the codebook.

**What §2.2 is *not* claiming.** Every row above is a prior in the sense of `P` in the efficiency ratio. `H3` in particular means BIRM is **not** being scored on latent-graph discovery end-to-end: it is scored on discovery over a vertex set the adapter fixed. Any §10 result must be quoted with that qualification attached. `D14`

## 3. Organ inventory

> **Status token, local to this section.** `SHAPED` = job, buses, timescale and architectural denials are fixed and carry ledger rows; the open slots are named in §12. `SPECIFIED` requires a §10 falsifier (R4). `SPECIFIED-PARTIAL` = the test exists and one input to it does not. Every organ below is `SPECIFIED` except the two whose §12 slot is load-bearing.

| Organ | Job in one line | Inputs (buses) | Outputs (buses) | Objective slot | Learning-rule slot | Architecture slot | Timescale | Falsifier (§10) | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Periphery** (adapter) | codec + affect channel; not part of BIRM | env | **O**, **V**(`r_t`), `done` | n/a | none (fixed) | fixed codec, §2.1 | fixed | none — Periphery is declared, not tested; its content is §2.2 | `DECLARED` |
| **Cortex** (neocortex — slow model) | slow store of the **meta-graph**: an action-conditioned predictor of the next latent, and the vocabulary `Hippocampus`'s bindings are written *over* | **Bg**, **Bx**, **Act**, **L**(transport enable) | **Bg**, **Bx**, prediction residual → **Thalamus** | **Action-conditioned next-content prediction in representation space**, over an environment *family* — with the `x` encoder held to an **isotropic-Gaussian marginal**, one object doing two jobs at once: BIRM's anti-collapse provision and the precondition for identifying *controlled* dynamics (`D86`, `D87`). Never scored by a prediction loss (`D89`) | Slow gradient on **associative weights only**; the input/output codes are frozen during transport (`D23`) | **Hybrid transition, and the hybrid's split is the `g`/`x` split** (`D88`): deterministic carrier = `g` under a fixed group action, stochastic content = `x`. **No decoder on the rollout path**; BIRM's only reconstruction term is P0's effect decoder (`D104`, `CONTESTED` against `T162` — `D135`). Width set by a **rollout-throughput budget** — candidates evaluated per step — not by accuracy per candidate (`D89`) | slow; written **only** on the transport channel, never online | `F-Cortex`: §10 `T1` (reverse ablation + support/query split), scored planner-in-the-loop by `T2`, never by a prediction loss | `SPECIFIED` |
| **Hippocampus** (fast instance store) | fast one-shot instance store, **addressed by `g`**, with a typed access protocol | **Bg** (address), **Bx** (content), **L** (write-enable + erase type + read schedule) | **Bx** (retrieved), occupancy scalar → **Neuromodulators**, `ρ` residual → log | **none** — `Hippocampus` optimises nothing; its *write policy* is `PFC`'s (`D33`) | One-shot Hebbian write at the address; write-**enable** learned by RL with per-slot credit `δ_j = gate_j · δ` | Fixed random `g→Hippocampus` projection + pointwise nonlinearity (§6 `D18`); content slots | one-shot write; typed erase; **read is a scheduled event**, not a pure function of the query | `F-Hipp`: §10 `T3` — score the read **schedule** separately from the contents; a store whose contents probe clean while task accuracy falls is a scheduler failure, not a memory failure | `SPECIFIED` |
| **PFC** (prefrontal cortex — controller) | holds a task model as **sustained state** and emits it as **bias** into organs that keep running their own dynamics | **Bx**, **Bg**, **V**, `Hippocampus`-read, per-level engagement gains from **Thalamus** | **Act**, **L** (write-enable, erase type, read address + lead time), bias into `Cortex`/`Hippocampus` read, **one channel into `Neuromodulators`** | Task return, via **V** only | Reward-gated association among co-active `PFC` units (task model) + RL on the gate/schedule outputs | `k` parallel level-searchers; **one common component + `θ_upd` + `θ_shift`, no inhibition-specific parameters**; typed output channels (`D27`, `D28`) | fast (per step); the control state **persists across steps** and is swapped, not recomputed | `F-PFC1`: §10 `T4` — bifactor fit over a population of trained instances must recover common + updating + shifting and **no inhibition factor**. `F-PFC2`: §10 `T5` — a lesion of `PFC` must produce context-inappropriate but *confidently executed* actions, not chance | `SPECIFIED` |
| **Thalamus** (arbitrator / commit gate) | sets **mode** (input-driven vs internally generated) and **which control level is live**, by two separately controlled gains over *edges* | prediction residual from **Cortex**, per-level output entropies from **PFC**, **V** | `g_rel` (release gain on internal-periphery edges), `g_acq` (acquisition gain on external-reader edges), per-level engagement gains → **PFC** | Commit discipline, §5.4 (`D53`, `D54`, `D56`) | The threshold is not learned — it is the cascade's bifurcation point; what is learned is the **resting distance from it**, set by `U` (`D53`) | Two gains, not one; address space is **edges, not organs** (`D35`) | fast switch, **slow set-point** | `F-Thal`: §10 `T6` — `g_rel` must predict speed only and `g_acq` accuracy only, with the crossed cells empty | `SPECIFIED-PARTIAL` — the test exists; `O18` may make its two axes one parameter by construction |
| **Neuromodulators** (VTA / LC / raphe / basal forebrain) | computes gains, precisions and learning rates from the learner's own second-order statistics | `Hippocampus` occupancy, **V** statistics, **PFC**'s channel into `Neuromodulators` | **U**, per-component gains, learning rates | No objective — six feedback laws with no shared one (`D42`, `O22`) | Control laws on the learner's own second-order statistics: `Var(δ)`, sign-oscillation of `δ`, `V`, `Var_a Q` (`D42`) | **Per-component set-points with an operating point each**, never one global gain (`D37`) | slow-ish | `F-NM`: §10 `T7` — a single-scalar `Neuromodulators` must be beatable by per-component gains on a task pair whose optima differ; `T8` checks each set-point against the statistic that is supposed to drive it | `SPECIFIED-PARTIAL` — the test exists; `O22`'s convergence is not certified by passing it |

**Six organs, no seventh.** A *competence estimator* is deferred to §12 `O14`, not installed. A *conflict/difficulty detector* is refused: only output entropy tracks engagement (`D30`).

### 3.1 Cortex — the slow model

| Slot | Fill |
|---|---|
| State held | Slow weights `W`: the code vocabulary (context codes, event codes, action codes) **and** the associative weights among them, as two separately addressable parameter blocks |
| Parameter count | **Derived, not tuned** (`D85`): `n_mod = ⌈log_K Ŝ⌉` structural modules of width `K`; the content half follows `D_o`; `f_g` emits `k` operators of size `K×K`. `K` and the content-half width are the only free scalars. §12 `O12` closed |
| **Architecturally denied** | (i) Online writes — `Cortex` is written **only** on the transport channel, so an environment step can never move a slow weight. (ii) The observation bus into the `g` half (§6 `D15`). (iii) Any ability to schedule its own transport: the filter and the stopping rule live outside it (§5.3 `D57`, `D58`) |
| Load-bearing commitment | **`Hippocampus` and `Cortex` are two organs, not one model with two learning rates** (`D22`), and what transports between them is the **mapping, not the terms** (`D23`) — `Cortex` already holds the vocabulary at acquisition |
| Prediction space | **Representation, never input.** A pixel/observation reconstruction loss is a *weighting* over what the latent keeps, and the weighting is set by area rather than by task relevance — the object-vanishing failure. Measured at fixed protocol: a representation-space video predictor scores 98/66/62 on violation-of-expectation where a pixel-space predictor with the same predictive form sits **at the untrained null**; latent targets beat pixel targets by 26.2 points with everything else held fixed. `D86` |
| Transition regime | **Hybrid, and the hybrid's two channels are already BIRM's two codes.** The RSSM compromise (deterministic carrier + stochastic content) is not adopted as a compromise: path-invariance *requires* `g` to be deterministic, and a deterministic content channel returns the conditional mean of a plural world — which is neither outcome and merges distinct things (segmentation Average Recall 0.327 → 0.541, mIoU 0.481 → 0.681, on identical probing logic, when the same model is read generatively instead). So determinism is **required** on the structural half and **forbidden** on the content half. `D88` |
| Planner exploitation | The standing hazard of the deterministic cell: a search over a differentiable learned map is an adversary against its own model, and §5.2's rollout is such a search. The defence is the stochastic content channel (`D88`), not a better model — noise makes exploitable ridges disappear under expectation. BIRM's second, cheaper defence is `D53`'s commit failure: a candidate whose support comes from model error does not recruit a coalition and never publishes |
| What identifies it, and what does not | Two margins, both properties of the **data-generating distribution**, neither of the learner. `γ_rep` — the weakest first-order predictable direction against the strongest nonlinear alternative — belongs to the environment and cannot be raised by more data of the same kind; encoder error enters the bound **divided** by it while predictor error enters additively. `ρ_tr = λ_min(E_g[Cov(a\|g)])` — conditional action excitation — belongs to the **collector** and is a design choice; counterfactual error is `δ/ρ_tr`, and at `ρ_tr = 0` the data constrain only the closed-loop map, so `A` and `B` are not separately identifiable while on-policy loss looks fine. Both are computable before training and both are logged in §9. `D90` |
| **Architecturally denied** | (iv) **Being trained on its own converged policy's data.** Every mechanism for improving an actor drives `ρ_tr → 0`, and on-policy prediction error — the only number anyone reports — falls throughout. BIRM therefore has no behaviour-cloning stage and no plan→policy distillation (`D92`), and `Neuromodulators` holds an excitation floor (`D91`) |
| Known hazard | Transport past a finite point *raises* generalization error, and for a relation `Cortex` cannot model the optimal transport is **zero** — so a scheduler that always consolidates is wrong by construction (`D24`) |
| Known hazard | **A read-out can be destroyed while nothing collapses and the loss falls monotonically.** In the one long run where both were measured, the aggregate probe rose for 1M iterations while a *patch-level* probe peaked at ~200k and ended below its own early value. Training horizon is therefore a design variable with a coefficient in nobody's objective, and §9's P1 exit criterion is a read-out peak rather than loss convergence (`D94`, §12 `O36`) |
| **Which organ holds the graph** | **`Cortex`, and neither pole's locus.** The meta-graph is `Cortex`'s action-conditioned transition (`D88`) plus the instance-graph edges `Hippocampus` files under a content-free pointer (`D190`); the fast store is an index and never a map, and `PFC` holds a task model, is denied the observation bus at every level (`D31`) and has no adjacency matrix anywhere in §3.3. So the fast-store pole is refused — the fast store holds *instances*, which is the cortical pole's own premise — while the cortical pole's conclusion is refused with it, because the organ that abstracts the graph is the **slow model and not the controller**. BIRM therefore separates abstraction from control, and consolidation is `D23`'s change of *what is held* — the mapping, not the terms — rather than a change of locus. The row's reconciliation, one coarse graph in cortex and one fine graph in the medial temporal lobe, has **no instance here**: `D66` leaves BIRM with a single fine-scale code and no multi-module bank (`O24`), and `D101` refuses the option library that chunking by *action structure* would need, so BIRM can exhibit neither resolution split and is not evidence on the row. `D205` |
| **Decoupled from the policy, with the affordance argument paid inside the model** | **Decoupled** (`D207`): the objective is action-conditioned next-content prediction over a *family* with no reward term and no task index (§9 P1), and `PFC` is a separate organ whose task model is swapped rather than compiled in. What the entangling pole actually argues — that an action possibility is a property of the agent–environment pair, so factoring it out of the representation throws content away — is installed here **inside the decoupled model** as `D71`'s effect-equivalence typing: content is typed by consequence over the repertoire, not by appearance, so the affordance content is on `Bx` without the policy being in `Cortex`. Two prices, both already booked. (i) That content is repertoire-relative and therefore **body-bound** — the codebook dies with the body (`D102`) while `g`, the associative weights and the `Hippocampus` protocol survive — so BIRM is decoupled across goals and entangled across bodies. (ii) BIRM cannot buy the entangling pole's gain back by compiling: `D92` deletes behaviour cloning and plan→policy distillation outright. The row's second bite — that a task-agnostic representation has no task to be identified against — does not land: `D90` identifies the model by `γ_rep` and `ρ_tr`, properties of the generating distribution and of the *collector*, so identifiability here is bought by excitation and never by a task. What is unscored is the payoff itself: §1's success statement is over held-out instance-graphs of a **seen** family and no §10 row ever changes the objective, so re-goaling is asserted and measured by nothing. §12 `O66` |

### 3.2 Hippocampus — the fast instance store

| Slot | Fill |
|---|---|
| State held | Content slots; a **pointer register** typed differently from the contents (`D32`); per-slot occupancy; per-feature within-slot variance (`D74`); a **per-slot provenance bit**, carrying `W`'s `generated` field as it stood at the write (`D173`) |
| Access protocol | `write(addr=g, content)` · `read(addr, lead_time)` · `replace` · `suppress` · `clear`. **Five primitives, not two** (`D32`) |
| **Query choice** | The address is the integrated position code `g_t` — a **position, not a want**. No goal encoding enters the read (§2.1(b)), no proposer enumerates alternative addresses, and no scorer ranks them; the monitor is `D69`'s read-confidence scalar, with step 9b firing only above threshold. The read's **only choosable variable is the lead time** (`O11`). There is consequently **no matcher at any stage**: no candidate set is proposed and none is scored, so access is structure-addressed and pays nothing for the structure, and the two-stage surface-then-structure retrieval question does not arise here. `D132`, `D159`. **Where a learned similarity lives, by wire** (`D188`): on the **address** wire, nowhere — `D18`'s frozen random projection has no similarity structure, so BIRM takes the code-side pole past the point at which that pole pays, keeping `O(1)` access and forfeiting the geometry that would read a relation off a coordinate test. BIRM's **one** comparator is on the content wire, at §5.1 step 9b, certified by `D69`'s confidence scalar; its form is unspecified and translation-invariant by default, so a relation whose similarity scale depends on where the pair sits is unexpressible there. §12 `O61` |
| What a write contains | The **conjunction** `(Δ = m⁺ − m⁻, a_{t−1}, r_t)`, not the content delta alone — the binder must sit where cue, action and outcome converge, and an efference copy is a requirement rather than a convenience (`D76`) |
| Attractor regime | **Prestructured scaffold, discrete** (Axis 1 / Axis 2): fixed points set by the frozen random `g→Hippocampus` projection, content attached, basins convex and spurious-free, capacity set by the address space, overload degrading **resolution, not identity**. Content-defined (Hopfield) and *learnable*-scaffold variants are denied (`D68`) |
| Read confidence | A **per-read confidence scalar** is emitted alongside the retrieved content, because relaxation supplies no certificate: a correct completion and a confident wrong one are the same state. Two-threshold novelty read; the scalar is a `Neuromodulators` input and a §10 quantity (`D69`) |
| Allocation prior | **Not a hyperparameter.** The allocate-vs-reuse knob is the *fan-out density* of the frozen `g→Hippocampus` projection — the same object `D18` froze for anti-collapse reasons — so `G38`'s separation/completion bias is readable off a weight matrix rather than tuned (`D73`). It is a **frozen constant with no runtime actuator**, taken in a regime — a family of instance-graphs, `g` re-initialised at every `done`, an encoder still under identification — outside the stationary one in which a constant has been shown tolerant. §12 `O51`. `D158` |
| **What a fresh slot is keyed to — allocation yes, identity no** | `Hippocampus` mints a slot on first arrival at an address and caches its conjunction there until `done`, which is allocation-on-demand with within-episode caching and no declared slot set. The key is `g`: a **path-integrated position**, finer than arrival order and still not an identity. Two entities occupying the same latent node are therefore one slot — they do not collide *by overload* (`D68` degrades resolution, not identity) but *by address*, which is a different failure and is not repaired by enlarging `Hippocampus`. `D74`'s per-feature tolerance decides allocate-versus-reuse **within** an address and cannot separate two things at one address. §12 `O60`. `D185` |
| Read tolerance | Per feature, set by that slot's **within-slot variance**, not by similarity: a feature that has historically varied inside a context must not trigger a new allocation (`D74`). This is where BIRM's allocate-versus-reuse decision is made by **inference history**; across episodes it is made instead by the adapter's `done` bit (`D11`, `H4`), so the two answers hold at two timescales and only the within-episode one is BIRM's. §12 `O59`. `D180` |
| **Removal discipline** | Contents leave `Hippocampus` only by a `PFC`-issued typed erase on `L_cmd` — `clear` or `suppress`, never as a side effect of a write (`D32`, `D33`). Nothing leaves by decay and nothing by capacity eviction: overload degrades **resolution, not identity** (`D68`), and the store is wiped whole at `done` rather than reclaimed slot-wise (`D11`). Decay is confined to the commit pool, where an un-committed candidate fades while staying promotable (`D55`). A **clear issued on still-needed contents** is a distinct, loggable failure class alongside the mis-scheduled read. `D167` |
| **A write-enable or the maintenance loop itself — write-enable, and the memoranda are maintained by nothing** | The gating dispute is a gate that flips a reverberating loop and then drops out (four of five reviewed working-memory models, thalamo-cortical or cortico-cortical) against a gate the reverberation runs *through*, with the hyperdirect path supplying the reset (the most recent and most capable of the five, which departs without arguing for the departure). The two differ in what a dual task costs: interference scaling with the **rate of writes** against interference scaling with the **number of items held**. BIRM is the first pole taken past its limit. The gate is `PFC`'s `L_cmd` write-enable (`D32`, `D33`); the memoranda are slots written one-shot into a prestructured scaffold (`D68`) with no recurrence holding them, so holding costs nothing per step and `PFC` is free the moment the write completes. The second cost is not absent but **relocated, and charged to a different budget**: overload degrades resolution rather than identity (`D68`), so load is paid in retrieval *precision* and never in concurrent action selection. The row's unrun dual-task simulation is therefore a crossed prediction BIRM can state — **action-selection interference tracks write rate and not load; retrieval precision tracks load and not write rate; the crossed cells are empty** — added as an arm on `T3`. `PFC`'s sustained task model is the one object BIRM maintains by recurrence, and it is a task model rather than a memorandum: swapped, not accumulated, so it does not supply the items-held axis either. `D200` |
| **Map or index** | **Index, with a payload** (`D190`). The address is a content-free hash of the structural code (`D18`) and carries no predictive content whatsoever; the meta-graph is `Cortex`'s (§3.1). What each slot holds is nonetheless a **transition** — the conjunction `(Δ, a_{t−1}, r_t)` (`D76`) — so `Hippocampus` is instance-graph *edges* filed under a pointer, not a map the agent navigates and not a bare pointer either. Consequence for §5.3, which is the half the dispute is actually about: transport moves `Cortex`'s **mapping, not the terms** (`D23`), so what consolidates is the meta-graph and never the instance store. BIRM runs **one wiring at all times** and refuses the developmental schedule — no phase in which `Hippocampus` is the map and a later one in which it reverts to indexing — so the behavioural onset of generalisation shows here as `Cortex`'s probe error falling (`D58`), never as a change of locus |
| **Address or item — what a sustained code on `Bg` is about** | An **address**, and never the item. The pointer register is typed apart from the contents (`D32`) and rides a different bus from them (`D45`), and `g_t` is a *computed position, not a want* — no goal encoding enters the read, no proposer enumerates addresses and no scorer ranks them (`D132`); the read's only choosable variable is the lead time (`O11`). `Bg` is written every step (§5.1 step 3) while `Bx⁺` is written once per commit, so the address is the **larger sustained signal by construction** — the asymmetry the biology reports, for a reason that is not attention. A §10 probe decoding position off `Bg` and reading it as the stored item is reading the wrong register; `F-Bg`'s crossed cell is what catches this. `D196` |
| **A resource ceiling or a self-imposed margin — a ceiling here, and the margin is in another organ** | The margin pole's mechanism is a **brake on recurrent excitation**, and `Hippocampus` has no recurrence to brake: memoranda are one-shot writes into a prestructured scaffold with nothing holding them (`D200`), nothing decays (`D167`), and capacity is the address space with overload degrading resolution rather than identity (`D68`). On the storage axis BIRM is therefore the ceiling pole **by construction**, and the ceiling is `N_slots = c·n_mod` — a declared number, not a scarce resource the store is straining against. BIRM does hold one thing by recurrence: the commit on `W`, held for a dwell time (`D53`, `D56`). That is where a margin is real, and it is **already a declared register** — the pre-commit resting distance from threshold, set by `U` — so the row's unrun sweep (lower the regulariser until instability, read capacity off the new ceiling) is `O18`'s sweep, and what it measures is commit throughput and rollout depth (`D109`), never item count. Consequence for the row's intervention question, and it is neither of its two options: the bound that binds first is **address collision** (`D185`, `O60`), which is repaired by neither enlarging the store nor loosening a brake. `D204` |
| **Where a never-experienced association is computed — one hop inside, the answer outside, and the composed link written back inside offline** | Online the store returns **one** stored conjunction at `g_t` and nothing recombines (`D76`, `D150`); composition is §5.2's rollout in `Cortex`, scored by `PFC` at every rollout step. Offline the composed link is written **into** the store: §5.3 step 5 offers an edge never traversed, under `D59`'s three acceptance properties. BIRM is therefore the downstream pole at the step timescale and the inside pole at the rest timescale, and which one holds is fixed by which channel is open (`D213`) |
| **Where fast learning physically is — a separate organ at a computed address, and the carrier's repertoire never moves within an episode** | `Cortex` takes no gradient step online (§9 P3) and its codes are frozen even on the transport channel (`D23`), so every within-episode change is a one-shot write into `Hippocampus` at an address `PFC` issues (`D22`, `D76`). Fast adaptation is a **re-addressing**, not a local weight change in the circuit that carries the representation. The forfeit: nothing moves the representation inside an episode — §12 `O67` (`D214`) |
| **What a maintained item is made of — a slot with no carrier, so neither pole's state variable** | Memoranda are one-shot writes into a prestructured scaffold with **no recurrence holding them** (`D68`, `D200`) and **no decay** (`D167`), and their content reaches `Bx` only on a `PFC`-scheduled read (`D76`, `O11`). Maintenance therefore costs nothing per step and the item is unreadable off any bus between commits — the sustained signal is the **address** on `Bg` (`D196`). The one thing BIRM holds by recurrence is `PFC`'s task model, which is a task set and not a memorandum. Failure mode designed against: the mis-scheduled read (`D167`) and address collision (`O60`), never basin escape and never a decaying trace. `T3` gains the delay-length arm — retrieval fidelity **flat** in the retention interval (`D217`) |
| **Instances or a distribution — instances, and the contrastive *form* is taken without the density** | `Hippocampus` writes the conjunction `(Δ, a_{t−1}, r_t)` one-shot at `g_t` (`D76`) into a prestructured scaffold (`D68`), and **nothing in BIRM fits `p(x)`**: no partition function, no free-running phase, no negative term. What is taken from the distribution pole is its *shape* — §5.1 is a minus/plus contrast whose write is `Δ = m⁺ − m⁻` (`D50`) — with the subtracted term supplied by the **structural prediction** rather than by an estimate of the network's own correlations, and the phase switch supplied by an endogenous clock rather than by a wake/sleep regime: single phase, one pass, closed form in what it wrote. The observable the distribution pole is credited with, replay of trajectories never experienced, is produced by **composition of two stored instances** (§5.3 step 5, `D59`) pruned by commit failure (`D134`), never by a model sample; `Cortex`'s stochastic content channel (`D88`) is a conditional predictor and not a density. Forfeit, and it is exactly the term the second phase buys: nothing here is weakened by what BIRM generates spontaneously. §12 `O70`. `D222` |
| **One operation code or a signature spread over the readers — one code, and the readers carry no quotient of it** | The operation lives in `PFC` and ships as a typed struct on `L_cmd`, whose only reader is `Hippocampus` (§4); the store is denied its own erase type (denial (iii)) and only the *effect* arrives downstream. The opcode is therefore loggable at one site, and a removal is verified by re-reading the slot rather than by decoding a pattern — with a `clear` on still-needed contents a distinct failure class (`D167`) and the erase channel carrying no between-instance variance (`D219`). The distributed reading is **unrepresentable rather than refused**: it needs several organs each holding the operation set under its own quotient, and `L_cmd` has one reader, `V` carries no address (`D70`) and `L_part` never touches the action path (§4). Binding on §10: an operation code decodable anywhere but `PFC` and the erase's effect on `Hippocampus` is evidence of an `L_cmd` reader §4 does not authorise, so it falsifies §4's reader list and not the control/storage split — an arm on `T14`. `D223` |
| **Approaching capacity as a failure or as the abstraction mechanism — a failure here, and the bound that binds first is neither** | Capacity is the address space and overload degrades **resolution, not identity** (`D68`), so the store returns the right slot blurred and never a class. The abstraction reading is unavailable rather than declined: it needs traces that overlap **by content similarity** as load rises, and `D18`'s frozen random projection leaves the address wire with no similarity structure at all (`D188`) — what collides under load is an arbitrary pair sharing an address (`D185`, `O60`), never two members of one family. Abstraction is another organ's job on another criterion, `Cortex`'s per-relation ration stopped by that relation's own probe (`D58`(i), `D218`), so loading pressure is not a curriculum knob here and `N_slots` is derived from `Ŝ` rather than swept (`D85`). Prediction owned: loaded past `N_slots`, fidelity for written items degrades in resolution while recall of an *unwritten* member of the same family stays at whatever slot the address lands on, and **the two curves must not converge** — an arm on `T3`. Unpriced: `Hippocampus` reports occupancy to `Neuromodulators` (§3) and **no law consumes it** (`D42`), so BIRM has the quota wire and no controller on it. §12 `O69`. `D226` |
| **Operated on or only stored — only stored, and the reinstatement is forced by wiring rather than read off a signature** | The dispute is whether a memory carried in synaptic state can be **transformed in place** (the wiki's synaptic-store models make maintenance and computation properties of one substrate: a store that reproduces prefrontal cortex's collapse of delay decoding while still performing the task and survives ablation of half its synapses because the memory is not in the trained weights; a store that holds content with no reverberatory trace and expresses it when a probe releases inhibition; and a parameter-sharing argument that activations *are* weights) or **only held**, every manipulation costing a reinstatement into an active, globally broadcast state. BIRM is the storage-only pole **by wiring**: slots have no recurrence holding them (`D200`), nothing decays (`D167`), there is no learned decoder anywhere on the read path (`D18`, `D33`), and nothing in the store recombines (`D150`) — composition is `Cortex`'s rollout (`D213`), reached only through a `PFC`-scheduled read onto `Bx` (`D76`, `O11`) and an exclusive commit (`D53`). The transformability pole is **unavailable rather than declined**: its mechanism needs a substrate whose weights are the thing that computes, and BIRM's store is one-shot writes into a prestructured scaffold at a frozen random address (`D68`, `D18`) with no gradient in it at all. What that pole says no cited experiment separates — a reinstatement that is *necessary* against one that is a correlate of report — is here true by construction, because a manipulation that skips the read has no path. The predicted serial bottleneck is consequently real and already carried on two budgets: items **changed** per unit time is commit throughput (`D109`, `O18`), items **held** is `N_slots` (`D85`, `D204`). `T3` gains the crossed arm — manipulation rate flat in `N_slots`, held-item count flat in commit dwell time, the crossed cells empty. `D229` |
| Size | `N_slots = c·n_mod`, linear in `Cortex`'s structural module count and therefore logarithmic in `Ŝ` — the scaffold's whole point (`D17`, `D85`). Enlarging `Ŝ` by an order of magnitude costs a constant number of slots, not an order of magnitude of them |
| **Architecturally denied** | (i) Choosing what it reads — the address *and the lead time* come from `PFC`. (ii) Surviving `done` (§2.1 `D11`). (iii) Deciding its own erase type — the operation code stays in `PFC` and only the effect ships downstream. (iv) A write that also discards: unlike PBWM's `Go`, write and erase are separate operations here (`D33`) |
| Read-out path | A read returns its slot's stored content onto `Bx` with **no learned decoder anywhere on the path** (`D18` frozen, `D33` one-shot), so `G97`'s dense-read-out interference has no mechanism here. The half that is denied rather than avoided: nothing recombines, so a novel address retrieves whatever slot it lands on rather than a composition of stored elements (`D150`) |
| Load-bearing commitment | The read is a **scheduled, priced event**. `PFC`'s output is an address **plus a time**, and a mis-scheduled read on intact contents is a distinct, loggable failure class |
| Known hazard | Enlarging `Hippocampus` need not raise capacity if the bound is in the *selection* rather than the carrier; the occupancy read-out to `Neuromodulators` exists so this is measurable rather than assumed. Second hazard, accepted: a randomly-addressed store **has no similarity structure** — "give me the stored structures like this one" is unanswerable, and the fix (data-dependent addressing) reintroduces exactly the correlations `D18` removed |

### 3.3 PFC — the controller

**Mechanism.** `PFC` does not route, gate or rewire anything downstream. It holds a task model as sustained state and adds an excitatory bias to organs that are already running a competition; the competition converts a small bias into a decision, so "enhance the relevant" and "suppress the irrelevant" are one operation and there is **no dedicated suppression channel** (`D25`, `D26`).

| Slot | Fill |
|---|---|
| State held | The task model (sustained, swappable, broadcast); `k` per-level engagement gains; the pointer/schedule registers it drives into `Hippocampus` |
| Factorization | Three axes, orthogonal and composed: **by operation** — one common component + `θ_upd` + `θ_shift`, **no inhibition-specific parameters** (`D27`); **by output port** — typed channels whose meaning is fixed by their reader (`D28`); **by policy order** — `k` level-searchers, all live from step 1 (`D29`) |
| Engagement rule | Level `j` is engaged iff the running entropy of **its own output** is non-zero: `gain_j ← σ(H[π_j] − h₀)`. Not error, not difficulty, not conflict, not novelty (`D30`). **One-sided by construction:** the rule lowers `gain_j` as a level's own output entropy *falls*, so a **solved** level disengages and a **vacuous** one — whose output never resolves and whose entropy therefore stays high — holds maximum gain indefinitely. The rule reads entropy's level, never its derivative. §12 `O50`. `D155` |
| Inter-level interface | Level `j+1` receives `(abstract variable, resolved output)` from level `j` and is **denied the observation bus** (`D31`). Non-bypassability is enforced by wiring, not by a loss term |
| **Architecturally denied** | (i) Routing or gating any downstream organ (only bias). (ii) Inhibition-specific parameters. (iii) The observation bus below level 0. (iv) Emitting "which slot" without a lead time and a removal type. (v) Writing to `Cortex` — `PFC` can write to `Neuromodulators` and to `Hippocampus`, never to the slow store |
| No temporal-abstraction layer | `D29`'s `k` level-searchers are the **policy-order** axis; options are the **temporal** axis, and the pair is exhaustive. **No subgoal carries a reward of its own**: `V` cannot take an address (§4, `D70`), per-level shaping is `D30`'s entropy gain, and the per-subtask addressing a pseudo-reward would supply is `L_part`'s job on the credit path (`D182`). BIRM installs no option library, for a measured reason: a correct option set plus a *newly opened shortcut* makes the agent converge on the option route and **ignore the shortcut completely** — not a slowdown but a changed fixed point, because a compiled edge set overlays the graph and hides primitive edges from further discovery. BIRM's option analogue is §5.3 step 5, which **adds** a composed edge to `Cortex` while action selection stays over primitives at every step, so the overlay failure is architecturally unavailable rather than avoided by tuning. The free diagnostic no hierarchical system reports is adopted anyway: a **primitive-only control run** each evaluation round, with a persistent return gap read as a closed-off region of the graph. `D101` |
| What practice does — the automaticity slot | **A level disengages; nothing is transferred out of `PFC`.** `D30`'s rule lowers `gain_j` as the running entropy of level `j`'s *own output* falls, so a mastered behaviour is left to the substrate that was already running the competition and the bias simply stops arriving. No consolidation path runs from `PFC` to any store, and no compiled fast path exists (`D92`, `D101`). §12 `O47`. `D140` |
| Store interface | `Hippocampus` reaches `PFC` on **both** wires and `D45` decides which carries what: retrieved content on `Bx`, level context on the relayed `Bg` edge `D65` resets. The two are separately silenceable, so `T98`'s data-path and gain readings are both instantiated and are dissociated by `T10` at reader `PFC` (`D145`). The reverse direction is **not** its mirror: `PFC`'s write into `Hippocampus` is the direct `L_cmd` struct, while `Hippocampus`'s return runs partly on the relayed `Bg` edge whose destination is a property of the relay and not of the sender (`D45`, `D65`) — so `PFC`'s read port and its write port are different modules (`D171`) |
| **Homogeneous pool or partitioned by content — partitioned, and not on that axis, because the content axis has nothing to cut on** | `PFC` is factorized on three axes and **content is not one of them** (`D27`, `D28`, `D29`). The refusal is not a preference: the partition-by-content position's *own* mechanism is that the partition follows **connectivity to whatever supplies the content**, and `PFC`'s content afferent here is a single wire from a single source (`Bx`, `D45`), with the composition site's fan-in fixed at two by type (`D191`) and `O` denied at every level (`D31`). Afferent diversity into `PFC` is one, so BIRM instantiates the partition position's mechanism and returns the homogeneous position's outcome. What BIRM *does* take is the axis the row's own arbitration adds and neither pole names — factorization by **operation** (`D27`), double-dissociable by lesion site and moved in *opposite* directions by one drug, which pure adaptive coding cannot produce. Consequence for §10: a content-preferring partition appearing in a trained `PFC` is evidence of a **second content afferent §4 does not authorise**, so it falsifies §4's fan-in rather than `PFC`'s factorization — added as an arm on `T4`. The row's `Closes when` — a justified criterion separating 17% strong domain wins from 88% weak ones — is not BIRM's to answer and is not inherited. `D198` |
| **A property of the tissue or of the demand — both, and separable here because engagement is a declared register** | The row's instantiated pole is measured as **participation**: the number of units representing the task at all falls significantly (`p < 0.01`) when retraining leaves only one response tactic valid and returns when both are restored — same animals, same penetrations, accuracy 99–100% — with a downstream area carrying no cue signal in the mixed-tactic task though it is spatially tuned where no tactic choice exists. BIRM is that pole **at the expression layer by construction**: `D30` sets `gain_j` from level `j`'s own output entropy, so a level with nothing to decide is not biased into the competition and its task model is not expressed; and it is the stable pole **at the weight layer**, where `Cortex` is slow and the `g→Hippocampus` projection frozen. The two are separable here and not in the biology because `gain_j` is a **declared register** rather than a quantity inferred from the probe. Binding on §10, and it is the control the row says every interpretability sweep is missing: **no probe of `PFC` may be read as “level `j` does not represent `Y`” unless `gain_j` was non-zero while the probe ran.** The downstream case is covered by a second mechanism rather than the same one — `D31` hands level `j+1` the abstract variable and the resolved output and never the argument they were computed from, so a null at `j+1` is predicted by wiring. `T4` inherits the condition: a population of instances trained under different demand distributions has a factor structure that is partly the distribution's. §10.4 gains the field. `D199` |
| When the task model is written | **Online, during experience.** `PFC`'s task model is fit in P2 by reward-gated association among co-active units (§9), with `Cortex` frozen for the whole stage; no offline channel writes `PFC`. The slow store is the opposite case and the two are not one organ: `Cortex` moves **only** on §5.3's transport channel (§3.1). `D168` |
| **A selector or an error stage below the controller — neither, and the variable that separates them has a register with no rule** | BIRM has no module of that kind and refuses both shapes on decisions taken for other reasons: `PFC` does not route or gate anything downstream (`D25`) and §3.6 refuses a seventh organ, so there is no disinhibitory release on the action path; and `PFC` is denied **O** at every level (`D31`) and emits bias and `L_cmd`, never a content prediction for a level below to cancel, while `V` carries no address (`D70`). The only subtraction in BIRM is the within-organ phase contrast `Δ = m⁺ − m⁻` (`D50`, `D76`). What survives is the discriminating variable — a **ratio** of gains across levels — for which `D30`'s rule reads level `j`'s own entropy only: §12 `O50` (`D211`) |
| **The common component of control — the bias itself, with suppression surviving once, off the action path** | `PFC`'s common component is the sustained task model biasing a substrate already competing (`D25`, `D26`), and the factorization carries `θ_upd` and `θ_shift` with **no inhibition-specific parameters** (`D27`) — so a bifactor fit yielding updating- and shifting-specific factors and no inhibition-specific one is predicted here, and the between-instance variance of inhibition-specific machinery is unaskable because there is none. BIRM's one suppression object is the typed erase `PFC` issues on `L_cmd` (`D32`, `D33`): a fixed operation code, no tunable parameter, and off the action path entirely — a fixed spoke under a varying hub. `T4` gains the variance arm: the erase channel contributes **no** between-instance variance (`D219`) |
| **Does the control layer store knowledge of its own — parameters yes, content no, and the lesion signature is the other pole's** | `PFC`'s task model is its **own parameters**, fit online in P2 by reward-gated association among co-active `PFC` units with `Cortex` frozen (`D168`), so a contentless controller is refused: something in `PFC` was written by experience and survives ablating what supplies its input. What is stored is a **bias pattern over organs already competing** (`D25`, `D26`) and never content — no adjacency matrix (`D205`), no goal index and no plan library (`D101`), `O` denied at every level (`D31`), one content afferent and so no partition by content (`D198`), and the model **swapped rather than accumulated** (`D217`). BIRM therefore predicts the processing pole's lesion signature from the representational pole's learning rule: `T5` requires context-inappropriate but confidently executed actions **and every content store intact**, which is the contrast that separates a knowledge base from an operating space, run inside one system. Unpriced: how many task models `PFC` holds and what a swap costs is the one organ dimension `D85` does not derive. §12 `O71`. `D224` |
| Known hazard | Two task models held at once should produce a **blend** — behaviour consistent with neither rule — which is a different signature from forgetting and is what `F-PFC2` looks for |
| Contested | The bias-only mechanism is disputed: in the biological loop, sustaining the relevant representation and suppressing the irrelevant one are carried by two different populations and removable one at a time. Recorded as `D25` `CONTESTED` against `T275`; §12 `O13` |

### 3.4 Thalamus — the arbitrator

| Slot | Fill |
|---|---|
| State held | Mode variable; two gains (`g_rel`, `g_acq`); `k` engagement gains; a slow set-point for each |
| **Gate or router** | **Router.** The two gains are a per-partner coupling vector over edges; a single "suppress the internal mode" scalar is architecturally unavailable, and the *sign* of each coupling is separately a controlled variable (`D36`) whose rule is §12 `O15`. `D162` |
| Output shape | **Two gains over edges, not one gain over an organ** (`D35`). A single "suppress the internal mode" scalar can produce the release half and cannot produce the acquisition half, because the acquisition half addresses edges to organs that were not in the internal mode to begin with |
| Trigger | **Superseded by `D54`** (§5.4): the trigger is a change in the *attended content*, not a residual against the input, so BIRM emits one boundary per event and none at offset. The threshold is the bifurcation point of the settling cascade (`D53`), not a free parameter |
| **What drives the switch** | **Nothing upstream of both modes, and not the internal mode either — the switch is a bus-occupancy discipline owned by `Thalamus`.** There is no detector organ (§3.6) and no broadcaster: `Thalamus` holds a free-running gate (`D62`), release is content-free and scheduled apart from which-content (`D56`), and internally generated commits are tagged `generated` and denied the `V` path (`D61`). `D153` |
| Trigger typing | **By carrier, not by label.** The error-side residual rides `V` into `Thalamus` and an `Thalamus` commit is the only path to `Cortex` (§5.3); the uncertainty-side scalar is `Hippocampus`'s read confidence (`D69`), which rides into `Neuromodulators` and returns as `β`, a path architecturally incapable of committing since `Neuromodulators` emits gains only. The two update triggers therefore have disjoint consequences and need no arbitration policy, while `U` stays one low-dimensional `m_t` decoded per organ rather than a per-trigger threshold set (`D40`). `D176` |
| **Does the hub hold the content, or only admit it** | **Two objects, and BIRM splits the dispute between them** (`D189`). The `W` bus **holds** the message: occupancy is exclusive and sustained by recurrence for a dwell time (`D53`, `D56`), and the payload is a **copy, never a transform** (`D47`) — so what is published is a representation, not a pointer to one. `Thalamus` **only routes**: it is denied content outright (below, (i)), reads `Bx⁺` as aggregate activity and never as payload (§4), and emits gains. So decodability and perturbability come apart here **by wiring rather than by coding geometry** — a probe at the arbitrator reads a full payload off the bus running through it while perturbing the arbitrator moves only gains, mode and `scope`. That is a mechanism for the dissociation, and it is the one BIRM must own: the un-published case is the failed commit (`D172`), where content exists in the organs and the hub is not holding it |
| Third output | **`scope`** — how many levels a revision is allowed to reach, published as a field on `W` (`D48`). Distinct from the precision gains, which set update *strength* per level and cannot express "revise everything at once" |
| **Architecturally denied** | (i) Carrying content — `Thalamus` emits gains only. (ii) Being addressed by content. (iii) Setting the *sign* of its own coupling permanently: the internal↔control coupling sign is a controlled variable, not wiring (`D36`) |
| **Broadcast channel or internal generator — one bus, both jobs, separated by a field and not by a population** | Internally generated content publishes on **W** like any other commit and is distinguished by the `generated` field (`D61`), never by a mark in the payload (`D47`) and never by a second carrier. The anticorrelation between the two modes is then a **bus-occupancy fact** — occupancy is exclusive (`D53`, `D56`) — and the generator is `D62`'s free-running gate rather than a separate system. The field is consumed downstream: generated content is denied the `V` path and writes to `Hippocampus` only under an explicit `PFC` licence, with a per-slot provenance bit kept at the write (`D173`). What BIRM cannot express is **overlap** — two populations sharing a hub — only alternation (`D210`) |
| Contested | The two-gain commitment is disputed by a one-dimensional measurement of the same axis: every parcel moves on the between-module statistic and none on the within-module one, graded monotonically by demand. Recorded as `D35` `CONTESTED` against `G85`; §12 `O18` (`D148`) |
| Known hazard | Both failure directions are settings of one variable — never-on (no internal mode) and always-on (rumination) — so an implementation must be able to exhibit both, or the gain is not real |

### 3.5 Neuromodulators — the metaparameter source


| Slot | Fill |
|---|---|
| Load-bearing commitment | Every component has an **operating point**, not a monotone gain, and the optima differ between components — so a controller with one temperature/gain hyperparameter is mis-specified by construction (`D37`) |
| Output | **One low-dimensional vector `m_t` on the U-bus**, decoded per organ as `f_k(W_k · m_t)`. Sender-side global, receiver-side addressed — one small matrix per organ instead of a learned destination table (`D40`) |
| **Control laws** — how each set-point is computed from the learner's own second-order statistics (`D42`) | `Var(δ) ↑ ⇒ γ ↓` (a long horizon whose value estimates are high-variance is not feasible; the agent measures its own horizon feasibility). `γ ↑ ⇒ β ↓, α ↓` (predicting far ahead forbids committing early — horizon, learning rate and exploration **cannot be set independently**). `V` very high **or** very low `⇒ β ↑` (exploration scheduled by the value estimate, not by trial count). `Var_a Q(s,a) ↑ ⇒ β ↓` (exploration allocated per state). **Sign-oscillation of `δ` `⇒ α ↓`** (oscillating error means the step is too large). `γ` gates `δ`'s sign: facilitate when `V > 0`, inhibit when `V < 0`. **Seventh law, wave 5: `ρ_tr` below floor `⇒ β ↑`** — a dither injection computed from the agent's own action log with no model, making exploration a requirement of *model estimation* rather than of reward-seeking (`D91`). It is the only law here whose justification is not about return. **Eighth law, wave 14: `pre-commit resting distance ↑ ⇒ commit-pool decay constant ↑`** — the one law whose two ends are both in `Thalamus`, pairing how long BIRM deliberates with how long an un-committed candidate survives, so the deliberating configuration is also the hard-to-clobber one. Its **sign** only is fixed; the coefficient is `?` (§12 `O58`), and the law is declared and ablatable rather than a shared substrate (`D179`) |
| Objective slot | **None, and this is the cost.** These are feedback laws derived from separate arguments, not descent on one objective: nothing guarantees the loop converges, and three of the couplings are mutually inhibitory and could oscillate. The alternative — every gain as a `μ^λ` inferred by descent on one free energy — buys convergence and loses the per-component specificity `D37` requires. BIRM takes the control laws and logs the convergence risk as `O22` |
| Degree vs. value of resolving | **Both exist, in separate registers.** `m_t` carries uncertainty *magnitude*, decoded per organ (`D40`); what resolving it is worth is §8's `ε_t(a)`, computed inside the selector and `U`-gated (`D95`, `D98`). One quantity carrying magnitude already multiplied by worth would delete the `U` bus and `D105`'s separation with it. `D142` |
| `γ` and `β` are separate coordinates, coupled only by a written law | They ride one vector `m_t` but on **different coordinates with different decoders** (`D37`, `D40`) and are computed from **different** second-order statistics: `γ` from `Var(δ)` on the `V` path and nowhere else (`D107`), `β` from four laws that never read `Var(δ)` (`V` extreme, `Var_a Q`, sign-oscillation of `δ`, `ρ_tr` below floor). The one coupling between them — `γ ↑ ⇒ β ↓` — is a **declared equation that can be ablated**, not a shared substrate from which neither is recoverable. `D156` |
