| Closed loop | `PFC`'s channel into `Neuromodulators` carries an **operating-point offset per component, never a gain** — `PFC` may move where a component sits on its inverted U; it may not set the component's output. `Neuromodulators` owns the statistics (`D43`, `G50`) |
| Timescale | `m_t` is second-order: strictly slower than the activity it gates. The order of the dynamics is **derived, not chosen** — only time-dependent expectations minimise the objective itself; everything else minimises its path integral, which is why gains obey slower dynamics and need trace-like variables (`D41`) |
| Second lever on the same law | The excitation floor can be met by **source heterogeneity** instead of by noise: a collector that is a union of unrelated behaviours has high conditional action variance by construction, at no cost in return, where dither costs reward on every step it fires. §9 takes both — P0/P1 buy `ρ_tr` from heterogeneity, P3 holds it with dither (`D91`) |
| Objective slot | **None.** Eight laws with no shared objective and three mutually-inhibitory couplings (`O22`). `Neuromodulators`'s `γ` discounts a *learned* return estimate and is denied the rollout scorer outright, so it never multiplies §8's epistemic term (`D107`) |
| `γ`'s scope, made explicit | `γ` appears **once** in BIRM, in `δ = r + γV(t) − V(t−1)` on the `V` path. It is the estimation discount of a value function being *learned*, and Doya's law is a bias–variance regulator over that estimate: a value function learned with large `γ` has large variance, a smaller `γ` buys a biased but less variable one, and the variance is visible in `δ`. §5.2's rollout has **no `γ`** — it scores against priors over observation trajectories and is terminated by commit failure, which is what deleted the Bellman solve. Two different objects, one letter. `D107` |
| Its timescale, and why it cannot swing inside a rollout | `γ` is re-tuned on **non-stationarity**, not per step. The one measurement of the same parameter at choice time finds it trait-like — a 238× spread across agents, stable across sessions separated by up to six months on a stationary task, and a fitted *population* rate is worse than each agent's own in two of three regions. That does not refute the control law; it names the regime where the law has nothing to do. `D41` already forbids `m_t` from moving faster than what it gates, so this is a restatement rather than a new constraint. `D110` |
| Why there is no coupling gain to sweep, and what one would cost | The integration axis has an **interior optimum**, not a monotone one over the reachable range, and BIRM refuses the knob that would reach the far end: `D37`'s per-component operating points and `D40`'s per-organ decode leave nothing global to raise. If `D148`'s one-dimensional measurement holds and `D35` collapses to one mode scalar, the knob exists and the instrument that would watch it approach does not. §12 `O18`. `D160` |
| **The third tier, and why BIRM declines it** | `Neuromodulators` is BIRM's second tier — it sets the rates at which the first tier changes. There is **no third**: the eight laws are authored, `PFC` may move an operating point and never a law (`D43`), and nothing samples the law set. So the generator of BIRM's own adaptation is hand-written, and that is `O22`'s real content rather than a convergence footnote. What the tier would cost is now specified operator-by-operator, and BIRM already holds **three of its five pieces in other coinage**: the per-rule mutability flag is §4.1's **writability register, set by a third organ and never by the two endpoints** (`G53`); the precedence order is `W`'s `scope` field, which bounds how many levels one revision reaches (`D48`); and the defined outcome for an unresolvable state is **commit failure** — terminal and *abstaining*, where the source's is terminal and *winning* (`D53`, `D172`). The two missing pieces are what would make it a tier: a runtime operator that flips the writability register (nothing writes it during an episode) and a liveness invariant guaranteeing something stays writable. BIRM refuses both, and takes the refusal's benefit explicitly — the objective stays **outside** the rewritable set (§1's success statement, `D5`), which is the clause the source gives up by construction. `D186` |
| **Where the exploration channel is — in these laws, and there is no pathway for it to be in** | The dispute is whether one anatomical channel is an action-specific **veto** (individually addressable suppression, degrading probabilistic learning when removed) or an **explorer** (a third regime that sets the *stochasticity* of selection when the value gradient is absent or too weak to learn from). The veto reading is denied upstream of this section: `PFC` biases a competition and routes nothing (`D25`), there is no dedicated suppression channel (`D26`) and no inhibition-specific parameters (`D27`), so an addressable `NoGo` has no site. The explorer reading's **regime trigger is already written here** — `V` very high *or* very low `⇒ β ↑` is that condition stated in value-gradient terms — so BIRM takes the payoff the row credits to the pathway, annealing falling out of the same modulatory source that sets every other gain rather than being bolted on at a fixed `p`, **without the pathway**: `β` is a coordinate of `m_t` decoded per organ (`D40`), and this organ emits gains only. BIRM is thus the row's *third* position, in which the circuit has no selection-time job at all, while instantiating the second position's function in other coinage. Lesion prediction owned, and it is the explorer pole's: zeroing `β`'s coordinate makes BIRM deterministic and stuck, never impulsive, and the site is `U`'s decoder rather than a selection pathway — an arm on `T7`/`F-U`. The forfeit, unpriced until now: every `β` law here is per-state (`Var_a Q`) or global, so **exploration cannot be allocated per action**, which is the one thing an action-specific channel would buy either pole. §12 `O65`. `D206` |
| **Architecturally denied** | A single global gain shared across components; any organ reading `m_t` raw; `PFC` writing a gain directly |

### 3.6 What §3 does not have

- **No competence estimator.** The biological default self-model is signed-inflated and its calibrator is a separate, lesionable module with an external referent — so adding a self-assessor to `PFC` would install the failure mode along with the function. §12 `O14`.
- **No detector organ.** Conflict, difficulty, error and novelty are all refused as engagement variables (`D30`); nothing in BIRM computes them.
- **No transfer path out of `PFC`.** No operation moves a representation from the control layer into a store that runs it without the controller — `PFC` biases a competition it does not compute (`D25`), so there is nothing in it to move. Automaticity is `D30`'s disengagement, and the claim is unmeasured: §12 `O47`. `D140`
- **No feedback into perception.** No organ writes to the encoder: `g` is denied `O` (`D65`), `O` is denied to phase α of the step (`D50`), and `g`'s correction arrives from `Hippocampus` rather than from the observation. The front end is a single feedforward pass (`D71`), so degraded or occluded observations are the adapter's problem and are charged at `H1`/`H3` unaudited. §12 `O52`. `D164` **Consequence for the sequence parse:** the only prior that parses the stream is `Cortex`'s action-conditioned transition on `g` (`D88`), so depth of top-down channel is not a spendable capacity axis here and the structural/dynamical trade has one runnable arm. §12 `O56`. `D175`
- **No operator-borrowing path.** No organ's update rule may be called on an argument of another organ's type: `D45`'s separate content and context wires *are* the type check, and `Bg` is denied to every reader outside the address/context set (§4). §7's rebinding reuses `Cortex`'s operator on a new index→primitive map, which is the same type. §12 `O45`. `D137`
- **No fan-in axis into the composition site.** There is one composition site — `f_g`'s computed operators (`D63`) — and the number of *distinct sources* feeding it is fixed at two by type, one context wire and one content wire (`D45`), with operator borrowing across types denied (`D137`). "Raise the afferent diversity into an existing operator" is therefore not a sweep here but a new bus, and adding a combinator is a new organ. BIRM's only variable on this axis is **whether an intact component is deployed** — `D70`'s standing disposition and `D30`'s engagement rule. §10.5 `L9`, §12 `O26`. `D191`
- **No gradient between organs.** §4 defines a space by *who may read it*, so the organ set is a partition whose borders are reader lists and typed denials, and §3.6's refusal of a seventh organ closes it. There is no embedding in which the six sit, and therefore no axis along which one shades into another: the continuum reading is **unrepresentable rather than refused**, its mechanism being a similarity gradient over units and BIRM having no similarity over organs to take. The graded axis exists one scale down and is authored there too — `PFC`'s `k` levels ordered by policy order under a continuous `gain_j` (`D29`, `D30`), and `Cortex`'s `n_mod` modules as a factorization of one frame (`D85`). What that buys and what it costs are one fact: the granularity conservation law — relational content moving between nodes and edges as the units are re-cut — is **charged rather than measured** here, since §4's table fixes nodes and edges in the same act. The one node criterion BIRM does *discover* is at the representation scale and not the organ scale: `D71`'s effect-equivalence individuates by intervention repertoire, which is the only label-free criterion on offer and does not apply upward. Consequence for §10: a probe finding a **border** between `PFC` levels `j` and `j+1` is evidence of `D31`'s wiring rather than a discovered unit — an arm on `T4`. `D228`

---

## 4. Bus inventory

> A signal means what it means because of **who is allowed to read it**, not because it converged to a meaning. Fill the *denied* column first.

> **Two structural commitments that precede every row below.**
>
> **(i) Fan-out is asymmetric: filtered in, diffuse out.** The apex reads selectively and writes to every level at once, so a global revision costs one hop rather than `L` relays. Measured on the biological instance: effective input to the apex correlates `r = −0.54` with its own microarchitectural axis (the far end is *insulated*), while effective output has no relation to that axis (`r = −0.18`, `P = 0.064`) and shows the lowest divergence from a spin null of any network — output reaches every level at approximately equal strength. The apex is BIRM's **crossing point**, and its capacity is budgeted: one exclusive commit per step, constant, while `Cortex`'s module count grows as `⌈log_K Ŝ⌉` — sub-isometric on what it serves. Content-addressed retrieval does not pay it, navigating `g`'s metric with local knowledge only (`D18`, `D63`). `D49`, `D147` **Below the apex, everything is a chain**: `PFC`'s levels exchange strictly `j → j+1` and are denied `O` (§3.3 `D31`), and `W` is the only bus with more than one reader-class, so BIRM has exactly one broadcaster and `F-W` is the test that it has not acquired a second. `D174`
>
> **(ii) Content and context ride separate wires with different filter properties.** Every organ pair connected by a content edge is *also* connected by a relayed context edge, and the two carry different variables — not a re-typed copy of the same one. This is the physical form of the `g`/`x` split, and it is what makes the `Denied` column enforceable by wiring rather than by a loss. `D45`
>
> **(iii) The conditioning interface is a design requirement, not an implementation detail.** A model's query language is a first-class property, and the `Denied` column below is where BIRM writes it. `Cortex`'s conditioning path is `(Bg address, Act one-hot)` — a two-register, **localised** intervention (§5.2 step 3), never a global re-denoise, so a localised counterfactual is expressible by construction. `D138`

| Bus | Carries | Encoding | Rate | Writer | Readers | **Architecturally denied to** | Falsifier |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **O** observation | `o_t` | `ℝ^{D_o}` float32, `[0,1]` | 1 / env step | Periphery | `Cortex`'s `x` half only | The `g` half of `Cortex` (§6 `D15`); `PFC` at every level (§3.3 `D31`); `Thalamus`; `Neuromodulators`; `Hippocampus` directly; **and phase α of the online step** (`D50`) | `F-O`: perturb `O` with a content-correlated signal; `ρ`'s distribution (§6 `D19`) must be unchanged |
| **Bg** structural belief (`g`) — *the context wire* | Address / phase code, **plus ≥2 separately addressable state registers at the receiver** (`D46`) | `ℝ^{D_g}`, phase-like | 1 / step, but **low-passed on the edge**: the receiver cannot reconstruct fast content from it | Cortex (path integrator); **Hippocampus (offset-preserving reset, `D65`)** | `Hippocampus` (as address), `PFC` (level context), `Cortex`'s conjunction | Time-locking to `Bx`. The relayed edge is architecturally slow, untuned and high-rate; its **destination is a property of the relay, not of the sender** | `F-Bg`: silence `Bg` into one reader — that reader's *content* responses must be intact and its *context* modulation gone. The crossed cell (silence `Bx`, lose context) must be empty |
| **Bx** content belief (`x`) — *the direct wire* | Content estimate; `Bx⁻` (phase α, predicted) and `Bx⁺` (phase β, observed) are distinct registers | `ℝ^{D_x}`, tuned | 1 / step, time-locked to the step clock | Cortex | `Hippocampus` (content), `PFC`, `Thalamus` (aggregate only, never the payload) | Carrying context. `Thalamus` may read its **aggregate activity** for the threshold test and may not read its content (`D53`) | `F-Bx`: the ablation of `D50` — drive `Bx⁻` from `O` and the write signal `Bx⁺ − Bx⁻` must collapse to zero |
| **U** precision / uncertainty | **A vector `m_t`, not a scalar.** Every organ decodes its own `(gain, learning rate, threshold, sign) = f_k(W_k · m_t)` | `ℝ^{D_u}`, `D_u ≪` organ count | **Second-order — strictly slower than the activity it gates** (`D41`) | Neuromodulators | Every organ, **through its own decoder only** | Reading `m_t` raw. No organ shares another's decoder; no single global gain exists (§3.5 `D37`) | `F-U`: a task pair whose per-component optima differ must be won by per-organ decoders and lost by any single shared decode |
| **V** value / outcome | **One scalar, no address.** Sign supplied by the receiver's type; address supplied by `L_part`, never by the signal | `ℝ`, `[−1,1]` | 1 / step (`r_t`), plus `Cortex`'s residual | Periphery (`r_t`), Cortex (residual) | `Hippocampus` (write gate), `PFC`, `Neuromodulators` (its own second-order statistics), `Cortex` (transport gate) | Carrying an address. **Any organ that consumes `V` without emitting `L_part` inherits spurious credit by construction** (`G19`) | `F-V`: ablate `L_part` and per-organ credit must degrade to co-activity credit — the failure must be visible as spurious potentiation, not as a rate change. **Crossed cell** (`D146`): mask `V` off `PFC`'s input with `L_part` intact — the plasticity role must survive and the state role must not, which is `G57`'s two-role separation run in one system |
| **L** licensing / write-mask | Two sub-channels: `L_cmd` (address, lead time, erase type) and `L_part` (**each organ's licence on its own participation**) | `L_cmd`: typed struct. `L_part`: one gate per organ | 1 / step | `L_cmd`: PFC. `L_part`: **every organ, about itself** (`D44`) | `L_cmd`: Hippocampus. `L_part`: the credit path only | `PFC` may not write `L_part` for another organ; `L_part` may not be read by the action path (it is a credit-side signal) | `F-L`: `δ_j = gate_j · δ` — an organ that did not act must receive no learning signal. Ablating the gate must be the single most damaging manipulation available |
| **W** workspace / commit | The published content of step `k`, **exclusive**, plus two fields: `scope` (how many levels the revision reaches, `D48`) and `generated` (§5.2 `D61`) | Pointer + gate. **The payload is a copy, never a transform** (`D47`) | ≤ 1 commit / step; may fire with no request (`D62`) | Thalamus (the gate) + the winning organ (the payload) | All organs | **Transforming its payload.** A broadcast stage that added information would be detectable as added decodable content, and in the measured case there is none (`BF₀₁ = 1.94 × 10⁴`) | `F-W`: a decoder over the readers plus `W` must not beat a decoder over the readers alone. If `W` adds information, `D47` is false |
| **Act** action code | One-hot over `k` | `{0,1}^k` | 1 / step | PFC | Periphery | Everything else | `F-Act`: §10 `T16` — permute the adapter's index→primitive map between episodes; re-binding must complete within §7's budget **and `Cortex` must not be retrained** |

### 4.1 Interface state per edge

Each edge carries four separable registers, and they are separately manipulable:

| Register | Set by | Timescale | Why it is not foldable into the weight |
|---|---|---|---|
| **weight** | the edge's own learning rule | slow | — |
| **terminal gain** | `U` via the receiver's decoder | seconds-reversible, content-free | A gain register lets a system trade stability margin for capacity without touching what is stored (`G56`) |
| **writability** | a *third* organ, never the two endpoints (`G53`) | event | The licensor is the network's own third node; no node is privileged as gate-setter |
| **operating point** | `Neuromodulators`, per component | slow | Every component has an optimum, not a monotone gain (§3.5 `D37`) |

**A fifth register BIRM does not have: conduction delay.** Every edge is `τ = 0`, so the direction-of-flow field readable off a weight matrix's column sums is unavailable by dynamical class rather than by omission, and the runtime override that field admits — a local time-constant offset — is unavailable with it. `Bg`'s low-pass is a magnitude filter, not a phase lag, and generates no direction. Routing direction is fixed by §4's authored table; the only runtime-variable routing object is `Thalamus`'s per-partner coupling vector and the sign of its entries (`D35`, `D36`, §12 `O15`). `D177`, `D178`. §12 `O57`

**Timescales are per-organ constants below the commit threshold and one system constant above it, and the crossing is a variable BIRM can sweep.** Whether an area's integration timescale is an **intrinsic** property (seven macaque areas ordered by the time constant of spontaneous fluctuations, `50–350 ms`, the ordering predicted by hierarchical position at `r_s = 0.97`, consistent across six datasets) or a **network state** (the same model run under visual and somatosensory drive gives two different dynamical hierarchies; hierarchical position predicts the emergent constant at only `r² = 0.25 / 0.14 / 0.22`; scrambling long-range weights while preserving topology attenuates the separation) is settled here by the threshold the dispute itself names — **perturbation amplitude** — because BIRM has an explicit one. Below `Thalamus`'s commit threshold nothing publishes and every timescale is a **declared per-organ constant**: `Hippocampus` one-shot, `PFC` sustained, `Cortex` transport-only, `Neuromodulators` second-order under `D41`, `Bg` low-passed on the edge, `τ = 0` on every edge. Above it the published commit holds `W` exclusively for a dwell time (`D53`, `D56`), a **system** constant belonging to no organ. BIRM is thus the intrinsic pole in the small-amplitude regime and the emergent pole in the driven one, **by wiring rather than by concession**, and the crossing is not an inference: the pre-commit resting distance is set by `U` (`D53`) and can be swept, which is exactly the experiment the dispute says has never been run. The estimator is already specified and needs no task — across-trial autocorrelation over a fixed epoch, two fitted parameters — so this is an arm on `T6`, not a programme. Two prices. (i) BIRM has exactly **one** input-conditioned timescale below the bus, the commit pool's decay coupled to the resting distance by `Neuromodulators`'s eighth law (`D179`), whose coefficient is `?` (§12 `O58`); everything else is constant by fiat, which is a position taken and not a finding. (ii) The diagnostic half is binding and composes with `D199`: a timescale read under one drive condition is a property of that condition, so §10.4 carries the condition with the number. `D202`

**What survives a step — a declared persistence set, and no re-derivation path.** Four things persist and they are enumerable: `PFC`'s task model, the one object held by recurrence; `Hippocampus`'s slots, held by nothing and unreadable off any bus between commits (`D200`, `D167`, `D217`); the commit on **W** for its dwell time (`D53`, `D56`); and `g` on `Bg`, written every step and content-free (`D196`). What earns persistence is **declared, not learned** — `PFC`'s write-enable on `L_cmd` (`D32`, `D33`). BIRM therefore has the shape the recomputation reading proposes, a small persistence set behind a large transient front end, and supplies the persistence *policy* that reading needs; it does **not** supply the other half, a **re-derivation path**. A dimension neither written to a slot nor on the live commit is gone: **O** is denied to phase α (`D50`) and to `PFC` at every level (`D31`), **W**'s payload is a copy and never a transform (`D47`), and the only sustained code left to re-derive from is an address carrying no content. A late query on such a dimension is answered by **re-observation** — an environment step — or not at all, so the cost appears as a step and never as a latency. Binding on §10: decodability at `t` licenses availability at `t+Δ` only for slot-written content and the live commit; an arm on `T3`. §12 `O72`. `D230`

**Edge classes.** `Bx` edges are direct, time-locked, tuned and causally dominant. `Bg` edges are relayed, low-passed, untuned, high-rate and causally weak on content — **and the relay owns the destination table**, so one organ's context can be delivered to an organ its content does not reach. Two consequences accepted here: an activity-magnitude estimate of "which edge matters" inverts the two classes (the high-rate edge is the causally irrelevant one), so §10 may not infer connectivity from co-activity; and "one global temperature vs. `Neuromodulators` independent ones" is a false choice — the middle option (a small state vector with a routing table over organ pairs) is what `D40` installs. **An address is driver-grade and content-free at once**, which is why the magnitude typing also mistypes `Bg`'s *function*: `g` decides which slot is read and supplies none of what is stored there, so it sets what a step represents without contributing content, and a driver/modulator classification built on axon calibre and bouton size would call it a gain term (`D181`). **The relay is an edge with state and no parameters**: four registers, a typed destination table, and a writability register owned by a third organ — and **no objective anywhere on an edge**, every loss in §3 belonging to an organ (`D183`). **The typing is uniform across destinations and the biology's is not**: the relay's role is postsynaptic-target-dependent, so `O21`'s destination table must carry a *type* per destination and not only an address — and BIRM implements the split as a **low-pass property of the edge** rather than as a labelled line, which is assumed rather than measured (`D163`).

---

## 5. The trace — one step, observation to action

> The load-bearing section. Numbered, no gaps, every line naming the organ, the bus, and the state that persists after it. If a step cannot be written as an assignment, it is not specified.

### 5.1 Online step (mode = input-driven)

> **The step is time-multiplexed inside itself.** One environment step contains two phases driven by an **endogenous clock** — not by `PFC`, not by a mode bit any organ has to supply. Phase α reads, phase β writes, and the two are the *minus* and *plus* halves of a contrastive update, so the fast store learns from its own recall failures with no teacher, no labels and no task boundary. `D50`

| # | Event | Organ | Reads | Writes | State after | Cost |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | observation arrives; `done` checked | Periphery | env | **O**, **V**(`r_t`) | `o_t`, `r_t` live. On `done`: `Hippocampus` cleared, `g` re-seeded to a random phase (§2.1 `D11`) | 1 codec pass |
| 2 | **phase α opens** — `O` is gated *off* the belief path | Thalamus | phase clock | phase bit | α. The gate is a clock, not a decision | 0 |
| 3 | path integration | Cortex | **Bg**, **Act**`(t−1)` | **Bg** | `g_t = f(W g_{t−1} + B a_{t−1})`; `ρ` logged (§6 `D19`) | 1 matvec |
| 4 | scheduled read of `Hippocampus` at address `g_t`, lead time from `PFC` | Hippocampus | **Bg**, `L_cmd` | **Bx⁻** | retrieved transition; a mis-scheduled read on intact contents is a distinct logged failure (§3.2) | 1 read |
| 5 | minus-phase belief settles: the **structural prediction** drives the code | Cortex | **Bg**, **Bx⁻** | **Bx⁻** | `m⁻` — what the system *predicted* this situation contains | settle, capped |
| 6 | **phase β opens** — `O` admitted, the `Hippocampus` read path attenuated; the driving source is handed over *inside* the step | Thalamus | phase clock | phase bit | β. The handover schedule is written at the driver→gate interface by short-term plasticity, i.e. learned from the traversal (`D51`) | 0 |
| 7 | plus-phase belief settles under the clamped observation | Cortex | **O**, **Bg** | **Bx⁺** | `m⁺` — what the situation *actually* contains | settle to commit, capped |
| 8 | **commit** | Thalamus | aggregate activity on **Bx⁺**, **U** | **W** (payload + `scope` + `generated=0`) | one coalition occupies the bus; competitors suppressed; step `k` is now *published* | threshold test; payload copied, not transformed (`D47`) |
| 9 | gated write to `Hippocampus` | Hippocampus | **W**, `L_part`, **V**, **Act**`(t−1)` | `Hippocampus` contents | The **conjunction** `(Δ = m⁺ − m⁻, a_{t−1}, r_t)` written at address `g_t` (`D76`). **An already-predicted episode produces a null write** — the novelty gate falls out of the rule rather than being bolted on | 1 write |
| 9b | `g` corrected by recall | Hippocampus → Cortex | **Bx⁺**, `Hippocampus` contents | **Bg** | Content-addressed read returns previously-written places carrying a similar observation; the returned `g` is folded into `g_t` as a **discrete offset-preserving reset**, not a blend. The residual between the integrated and the returned code **is** `ρ` (`D64`, `D65`). Fires only above the read-confidence threshold (`D69`) | 1 read + 1 compare |
| 10 | action selected | PFC | **W**, **Bx**, **V**, **U** | **Act**, `L_cmd(t+1)`, `L_part` | action emitted as an additive bias into a competing substrate (§3.3 `D25`); task model persists | 1 argmax over biased competition |
| 11 | participation licences latched | all | own output | `L_part` | `gate_j` per organ, consumed only by the credit path | 0 |

**Required decisions, answered inside the step:**

| Decision | Fill |
|---|---|
| Settling vs. feed-forward | **`g` is feed-forward** — one assignment, step 3, no iteration. **`x` settles**, and the settle is terminated by the *commit* (step 8) rather than by a fixed iteration count, with a hard cap as a fallback. Latency is therefore a function of input ambiguity, which is the property a fixed-round scheme cannot express |
| Time-multiplexed internally | **Yes, and it is load-bearing.** Phase α estimates from structure, phase β reads the observation. The informative ablation is negative: **drive `Bx⁻` from `O` and the delta collapses to zero** — a minus phase that can see the answer is not a prediction (`D50`) |
| **Time origin** | **The previous commit.** Every temporal quantity in the step is referenced to the last published event (§5.4 `D54`), a reference `Thalamus` generates and maintains itself — not stimulus onset, not a modeller-supplied oscillator. The one externally stamped origin is `done`, charged at `H4`. `lead_time`'s zero is **unfixed**: §12 `O43`. `D133` |
| Not recomputed this step | `g` (integrated, never recomputed); `PFC`'s task model (sustained, swapped not rebuilt); `U`'s `m_t` (second-order, slower than the step by construction); `Hippocampus` contents; every `Neuromodulators` set-point |

### 5.2 Rollout step (mode = internally generated)

| # | Event | Organ | Reads | Writes | State after | Cost |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `g_rel` raised on internal-periphery edges | Thalamus | **U**, residual | gains | internal mode live (§3.4 `D35`) | 0 |
| 2 | phase α only — there is no phase β, because no observation is clamped | Thalamus | phase clock | phase bit | α held | 0 |
| 3 | `g` advanced by a *proposed* action | Cortex | **Bg**, proposed **Act** | **Bg** | candidate successor code | 1 matvec |
| 4 | successor content predicted | Cortex/Hippocampus | **Bg** | **Bx⁻** | one rollout step | 1 read + settle |
| 5 | commit, **tagged** | Thalamus | aggregate **Bx⁻** | **W** (`generated=1`) | one intermediate state published per rollout step | threshold test |
| 6 | scored | PFC | **W**, **V**, **U** | rollout value | — | 1 |

| Slot | Fill |
|---|---|
| What stops the rollout | **Commit failure** — a candidate that cannot recruit enough support never publishes, and the rollout ends without a verdict. This is a principled abstention rather than a step budget; a step budget is kept only as a fallback cap. It is also BIRM's **rejector**: the rollout is a self-generated trace conditioned on, and it is checked per step rather than run single-trajectory (`D136`). The stopping rule is therefore a **resource criterion, not a reliability comparison** — BIRM maintains no posterior variance per controller to compare (`D144`, `O14`) — and `Cortex`'s width is set by the same currency, candidates evaluated per step rather than accuracy per candidate (`D89`). `D157` |
| **Minimum depth, and why it is a requirement rather than a preference** | The rollout must be able to chain **`H ≥ 2` information-gathering commits** before a pragmatic one. Below that, an epistemic term cannot propagate across steps and degenerates to myopic information gain — which is, independently, the regime in which every neural implementation in the wiki is beaten by its own reward-only ablation, so BIRM's depth floor and `D97`'s audit are load-bearing for each other. Since depth here is set by commit failure and not by a budget, the floor is a claim on `O18`'s stability budget. **The floor is conditional on per-step commit reliability `p` exceeding a crossing `p*`; both are `?` — §12 `O44`.** `D108`, `D109`, `D136` |
| **No discount on this path** | The rollout carries no `γ`. This is not an omission: BIRM has no stationary value function to discount against — §5.2 scores against priors over observation trajectories — and the one place BIRM does need stationarity, the `δ` on the `V` path, keeps the exponential form (`D107`). The split is what lets `O20` stop being a single question: the hyperbolic kernel's time-inconsistency objection bites only where a stationary `V` is required, and that is not here. `D111` |
| What tags generated content | The `generated` **field on `W`, not a mark inside the payload** — forced by `D47`, since a payload that carried its own provenance would be a transform. `D61` |
| What the tag does downstream | Gates `Hippocampus`: generated content may write only under a licence `PFC` must issue explicitly, and is **denied the `V` path entirely** — a rollout may not train the outcome estimate it was scored against |
| Scoring | Rollouts are scored against **prior expectations over observation trajectories**, not against a value function that must be solved for. This deletes the Bellman solve and relocates the difficulty into where the priors come from — which is `O6`/§8, not here |
| **What is scored is a policy, not a plan** | A fixed action sequence systematically **undervalues an action whose worth is what it enables**: if `a₁` leads to either of two successors each needing a different follow-up, both continuations score badly in isolation and `a₁` inherits their cost. The repair is to score `q(a_t \| g_{t−1})` — the action marginalised out of the joint over rollout states — so `a₁` is priced against the responses BIRM *would make* at each successor. This costs BIRM nothing, because `PFC` is already in the loop at **every** rollout step (row 6) rather than only at the end. Measured price of getting it wrong: a plan-based scorer visits the informative cue in 66% of episodes against 100%. `D99` |
| Where the epistemic term enters | Inside the selector, at row 6, **added late and separately gated**. The pragmatic term is computed first and the epistemic term arrives as a `U`-gated addition `Neuromodulators` can zero — which is the one shape that makes the known failure recoverable: every neural agent that computes one joint `G` in a single head is beaten by its own reward-only ablation, and the epistemic term's characteristic failure is *dominating into policy collapse*. Sequential assembly with pragmatic-first is also what the one human recording of the two terms reports. `D95`, `D98` |
| **Which side a failure is on — and BIRM has both knobs separately** | A compositional failure is a *vocabulary* shortfall or a *proposal* shortfall, and they are not the same defect: measured, a codebook whose primitiveness is 1.000 transfers at 0.019–0.038 amortised and at 0.696–0.707 under a 1024-sample majority vote — same weights, ~180× compute, nothing about the representation changed. BIRM's two knobs are already separate objects: the **vocabulary** is the effect-equivalence codebook plus `f_g`'s `k` operators (§6, §7), and the **search** is this section's rollout, whose width is a throughput budget (`D89`) and whose depth is set by commit failure against `O18`. So the two hypotheses are dissociable here by sweeping one at fixed other, which is what makes the row's complaint actionable rather than fatal. The row's open instrument — *what plays the role of primitiveness where no ground-truth vocabulary exists* — has a **candidate and not a solution** in `D72`'s split-recombine test: it scores the code independently of task performance and needs no condition grid and no true primitive set, but it certifies **factorization, not coverage**, and coverage stays `O25`. Consequence for §10, binding: no §10 row may read a low score as evidence about the code unless the sampling budget was **swept**, not merely reported (S1). `D187` |
| **Depth is chaining, not nesting** | A rollout may not contain a rollout: `W`'s occupancy is **exclusive** (`D53`), so two simultaneous queries have no bus to publish on. Depth is the `H ≥ 2` chain of published commits above, each scored by `PFC` at row 6, and what settles a chain is a **relaxation, not a posterior over posteriors**. BIRM holds no other-agent model at all — §1's task set is single-agent — so the recursive-inference-versus-fixed-point dispute has no second instance here. The nesting BIRM declines is priced: one sampled world per level is linear in depth and lossy, `95.0 → 75.0 → 70.0 → 67.5 → 55.0` across orders 0–4, so what is computed deep is a one-sample estimate of the nested query and not the query. `D192` |
| **What the rejector buys — selection, permanently, because the distillation path is denied** | The row disputes whether verifier-driven test-time search buys **reasoning** (the search is distilled back into the policy and the product is a better single forward pass: 12% → **74% at greedy decoding**, self-correction elicited from reward signal alone with no supervised traces) or **selection** (coverage was already in the generator and the verifier picks: 74% greedy → 83% majority@64 → 93% best-of-1000; ~60% pass@1 → 88.9% pass@8192; and the wiki's own 0.019–0.038 → ~0.70 at 1024 samples with a *complete* primitive set already in hand). BIRM is the selection pole **by construction**: `D92` deletes behaviour cloning and plan→policy distillation outright, because every mechanism that trains a policy on its own converged output drives `ρ_tr → 0` and destroys `D90`'s identifiability while on-policy loss falls throughout. The verifier ladder is therefore an **architecture here and never a curriculum**, and BIRM forfeits the greedy-decoding gain the reasoning pole is measured by. Two ways BIRM's rejector is not the surveyed one, both narrowing what may be quoted: it is **per-step commit failure**, a resource criterion with no reliability comparison (`D136`, `D157`), and it is **abstaining rather than selecting** — nothing enumerates `k` completed traces and ranks them, so a best-of-`k` curve is not a measurement BIRM can produce and the row's decisive experiment has an empty arm here. §10.5 `L10`; the size of the forfeited gain is unmeasured, §12 `O64`. `D201` |
| Known hazard | The commit **free-runs** (`D62`): rollouts can start with no request. Rumination is the always-on setting of `g_rel`, and the implementation must be able to exhibit it or the gain is not real (§3.4) |

### 5.3 Offline cycle (rest / replay)

| # | Event | Organ | Reads | Writes | State after | Cost |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | candidate drawn from `Hippocampus` by **uniform coverage** (reservoir), not by reward and not by uncertainty | Hippocampus | tags | candidate | one proposed update `w*` | 1 draw |
| 2 | **recall factor** computed | Hippocampus | `w*`, `Hippocampus` contents | `r_S = w_S · w*` | the fast store's own recall of the proposed update | 1 dot product |
| 3 | gate | Hippocampus→Cortex | `r_S` | `L`(transport enable) | `g(r_S)`: threshold baseline; band-pass if unreliable items can themselves repeat | 1 compare |
| 4 | transport — **the mapping, not the terms** | Cortex | candidate | `Cortex` associative weights (codes frozen, §3.1 `D23`) | one gradient step on the slow store | 1 update |
| 5 | **composition**: two stored transitions sharing an endpoint are composed and the synthetic pair is offered | Hippocampus | `Hippocampus` contents | candidate | an edge the agent never traversed | 1 draw + 1 accept test |
| 6 | probe | Cortex | held-out probe set for that relation | transport counter | marginal generalization gain for this relation | 1 eval |

| Slot | Fill |
|---|---|
| **Sampling filter** | **Uniform-coverage admission + recall-gated write.** Both halves are refusals of the machine standard: reward-proportional sampling is indistinguishable from uniform, and uncertainty-proportional sampling is *worse than not doing continual learning at all* — the two criteria machines actually use are the two that fail. `D57` |
| What is transported | `Cortex`'s associative weights only (§3.1 `D23`). Plus, from step 5, **edges that were never traversed** |
| **Stopping criterion** | Two, both hard. **(i) Per-relation ration**: the optimal number of reactivations is finite and set by that relation's predictability — transport for a relation stops when its probe error stops falling, and is **zero** for a relation `Cortex` cannot model (§3.1 `D24`). **(ii) A per-rest-period event cap**: the biological channel's coupling rate is ~9 coordinated events per 366, so replaying every buffered item runs the channel orders of magnitude harder than the system being copied — which is exactly the over-transport regime where generalization falls below chance. `D58` |
| **Overgeneration pruner** | **Commit failure** — the decoder-free analogue of a well-formedness constraint on the trace. Step 5 supplies the undergeneration half by composing edges never traversed; what refuses a composition is that its intermediate state cannot recruit support and never publishes (§5.2). No MDL length prior is installed, and no decoder is available to test that an intermediate state decodes (`D104`). `D134` |
| Composition operator | Accept the composed pair with probability rising in the terminal state's value, and update the **earlier** item from the later one. Three falsifiable properties, all measured in the biology: it must survive substituting the intermediary, must **not** fire for value-mismatched pairs, and must run reverse to the direction of use. `D59` |
| Schedule | **Two stages.** A cheap scalar tag is written online at encoding, from recency and outcome, and **never refreshed**; the offline distribution is drawn from the tags. Priority is deliberately stale — that is what frees the online phase. `D60` `(tentative)`. The trigger is a clock-and-outcome tag and **not** a measure of how far the new item conflicts with what is stored; `D57`'s recall factor `r_S` is the one online quantity of that kind BIRM computes, and transport is gated **up** by the store's own recall of the candidate rather than by its disagreement with it. §12 `O54`. `D169` |
| Why the uncertainty refusal is not a coincidence | The same uncertainty quantity is a **good thing to act on and a bad thing to rehearse on**, measured on one architecture: as an action bonus it raises 8-task continual performance 0.09 → 0.28 and cuts forgetting 0.37 → 0.13; used to prioritise which stored episodes the model trains on, it performs at the level of the *non-continual* baseline, worse than uniform on every metric. The mechanism is that an exploration bonus is **consumed** the moment it is acted on while a replay priority is **persistent**, so the buffer keeps re-presenting whatever the model cannot predict — disproportionately noise it will never predict. §8's epistemic term and §5.3's sampler therefore read the same statistic and must not share a weight. `D105` |
| Which criterion decides consolidation | **Both, in series, behind a uniform sampler**: admission by coverage and the write gated by recall (`D57`), transport stopped by the relation's own predictability (`D58`). The ordering has a price — a reliably recurring but unmodellable relation passes the gate at full strength and is refused only after a probe cycle has failed to fall. §12 `O53`. `D166` |
| **Incomplete transfer — a refusal, a starved channel, or latency, and which one is readable off a register** | Both mechanisms are installed and are separate objects: `D58`(i) is the per-relation ration, terminal at **zero** for a relation `Cortex` cannot model (`D24`), and `D58`(ii) is the per-rest-period **event cap** — a declared ceiling on the budget the ration spends. A third outcome is `D166`'s ordering price: an unmodellable but reliably recurring relation passes `D57`'s recall gate at full strength and is refused only after a probe cycle fails to fall. So transfer stopped by (i) is correct and must not be repaired; transfer starved by (ii) is a budget; transfer still pending is latency. Never swept: §12 `O53`. `D216` |
| **What decides weights versus a slot** | `D58`(i), per relation, by that relation's own held-out probe (step 6): content `Cortex` cannot model above its probe floor stays in `Hippocampus` permanently. The criterion is **compressibility measured online**, its granularity is the relation and never the entity (`D197`, `O63`), and it routes **migration only** — both organs exist at all times and every step is written to `Hippocampus` first, so allocation is not a decision anything here makes. `D218` |
| **Which direction the store is run in** | Step 5's composition updates the **earlier** item from the later one and must run reverse to the direction of use (`D59`); the carrier itself cannot be run backwards, since `W_a` composes under a monoid and has no inverse (`D63`). Backward credit is therefore bought at the store, never at the integrator. The forward:reverse **mixture** is not a controlled variable — the sampler is uniform and the tag stale (`D57`, `D60`) — so the policy-versus-geometry trade cannot be set here: §12 `O68`. `D215` |
| What this does **not** buy | Recall gating is a **reliability filter, not a causality filter**: a shortcut that recurs stably passes it exactly as easily as structure does. Nothing in §5.3 separates a shortcut from a cause, and reading it as if it did would be the error |

### 5.4 Mode arbitration

| Slot | Fill |
|---|---|
| Quantity the arbitrator compares | Aggregate activity arriving on `Bx⁺` against the commit threshold — **not** a residual against the input. The trigger is a change in the *attended content*, not a change in the input; the two coincide when something new arrives and separate when the incumbent merely loses support. So BIRM emits **one boundary per event and none at offset**. `D54` |
| Where it is computed | `Thalamus`, as the **bifurcation point of the settling cascade** — not a comparator constant. Sensitivity is the fidelity of propagation into `Thalamus`; bias is the *pre-commit resting distance* from threshold, set by `U`. Both are readable before the input arrives, which makes the idle-state gain a legitimate place to spend control effort. `D53` |
| Hysteresis / commit discipline | Occupancy is **exclusive** and held by recurrence for a dwell time. **Release is a content-free event, scheduled separately from which-content** — it dissolves the hold and the next occupant moves in, rather than winning the bus outright (measured: the release event leads the content change by ~209 ms in 86–89% of transitions). Commit and content-selection are therefore two schedulable things, and BIRM keeps them apart. The content-free layer is a **separate object**, not a field-level readout of the content layer's own instability. `D56`, `D154` |
| Sub-threshold case | A **failed commit** must be an expressible state: partial recruitment, logged, distinct from both a completed commit and a blend. In the biology it is 51% site recruitment against 100%, at a burst rate of 0.147 vs 0.17 with a 0.015 floor. A continuous mixture cannot produce it, which is why it is the cheapest test that the gate is real. `D53`. BIRM has **one** publication gate and no second, earlier one: the state in which content is computed and never published is the failed commit itself, logged as partial recruitment rather than inferred from a report. `D172` |
| In-flight rollouts on a switch | **Not discarded.** An un-committed candidate decays but stays *promotable* for a bounded window, so a later signal can publish it — the deferred-commit rule (biological window: ~900 ms). A switch stops feeding the pool; it does not flush it. `D55` |
| Commit vs. update | **The commit is exclusive; the update is not.** Publication on `W` stays winner-take-all (`D53`, `D56`); *memory updating* is responsibility-weighted across every stored structure, `Δ_j ∝ λ_j · k_j · e_j`. These are not in conflict — one is a bus occupancy, the other a plasticity gate — and the argmax alternative has a measured price: a new context whose policy partially matches two stored ones is learned **more slowly** than one matching neither, visible as an excess of exactly one error type. `D75` |
| Alternation between stored structures | **A recency term on the commit gate, not a sample from a posterior.** Occupancy is exclusive (`D53`, `D56`) and an abandoned candidate stays promotable while it decays (`D55`), so repeated switching is what a decaying per-slot bias plus a periodic threshold test produces. BIRM maintains no distribution over which structure is currently active, and §10 may not read alternation as evidence that one is represented. `D144` |
| Where the speed–accuracy point is set | **At the gate.** The threshold is the bifurcation point of the settling cascade and the bias is the pre-commit resting distance set by `U` (`D53`); there is no accumulation to speed up and no coupling gain that is a runtime control (`D41`). BIRM trades speed against accuracy in the one place both measurements of the biology agree it does not. The **paired register** is the commit pool's decay constant, coupled to the resting distance by `Neuromodulators`'s eighth law (`D179`). §12 `O18`, `O58`. `D161` |
| What is graded, by operation | **Allocate: argmax. Express: exclusive. Update: graded.** Allocation is read off the fan-out density of the frozen `g→Hippocampus` projection (`D73`), publication on `W` is winner-take-all (`D75`), and plasticity is responsibility-weighted across every stored structure (`D75`) — hard where the decision is discrete and irreversible, soft where it is continuous and recoverable. The remaining route, a mixture carried as *code overlap* trained to match situation similarity, is architecturally unavailable: `D18`'s frozen random projection has no similarity structure to overlap. `D141` |
| **A set or a point — a point per commit, a sequence of points, never a set** | Publication is exclusive and depth is chaining rather than nesting (`D53`), so two interpretations are never live together and any menu must be re-derived. The recovery half is present and bounded: an un-committed candidate stays promotable for a window (`D55`), which is inhibit-and-resettle's retention step over the commit pool. The answer differs **by operation** (`D141`): allocate argmax, express exclusive, **update graded** — so responsibility-weighted plasticity (`D75`) is the retained-menu behaviour at the layer that has no bus to contend for. §10 may not read alternation as evidence a set is represented (`D144`, `D212`) |
| Failure mode inherited, stated openly | The system **hallucinates by the mechanism it perceives by**: a spontaneous supra-threshold cascade is indistinguishable downstream from a driven one, and in the biology a false alarm is reported exactly like a hit. The only available fix is at the propagation stage, not at the gate |

---

## 6. The factorization — where `g`/`x` is installed and what makes it pay rent

> The wiki's central negative result: no objective is known that prefers a factorized code, and a constant code satisfies path-consistency trivially. So this section is architecture, not loss — and it needs an anti-collapse provision *before* any of it is scored.

| Slot | Fill |
|---|---|
| Mechanism that generates `g` | **Action-accumulated path integration with a per-action operator**: `g_t = ℓ₂(W_{a_t} g_{t−1})`, `W_a = f_g(a)` produced by one small generator from the action index, so an operator is *computed* rather than stored per action. `g` never reads `o_t`; content enters only through the conjunction `p = f(g, x)`. `D15` amended by `D65`; operator form `D63` |
| What makes `g` path-consistent by construction (if anything) | **Path-invariance, not commutativity.** The requirement is that every route *reaching a given state* produce the same `g` — a monoid, not a group: `uncle = father ∘ brother ≠ brother ∘ father` must remain expressible, which matrix composition of `W_a` gives and phase addition does not. The abelian "equal total displacement" reading is the special case and is **withdrawn** (`D63` supersedes `D16`). Still **conditional** on the family's actions composing (`G41`, `H8`); §7's first row decides it operationally, and if it fails this section is void and falls back to §12 `O4` |
| The two constraints that generate the code | *Distinctness* and *path-invariance*, both consequences of `g` being a **memory address** and of nothing spatial. Claimed sufficient to produce grid and band codes with no spatial target in the loss — which is why §6 stays architecture rather than objective |
| How `g`'s drift is corrected — **and it is not by the observation** | The observation bus stays denied. Correction arrives on a **new edge, `S → Bg`**: the content-addressed read of `Hippocampus` returns previously-written places carrying a similar observation, and the returned code is folded into `g_t` as a **discrete, offset-preserving reset** — measured in a fully imaged integrator, where a displaced landmark makes the code *jump while preserving its offset* and cue-conflict is won outright by the cue rather than averaged. Anchoring is therefore implemented as recall, with no landmark machinery and no reset module. Cost accepted: the reset has its own time constant that no model supplies (`O27`), and the anchor is a **recalled place** — neither a landmark nor the self-motion stream (`D143`). `D65` supersedes `D15` |
| What makes a content-contaminated `g` **cost the agent task reward** (rent) | `g` is the **address of the fast store `Hippocampus`** and is used for nothing else. Two requirements then become forced rather than stipulated: *distinctness* (different states must get different codes or their memories collide) and *path-invariance* (the same state must code the same from any direction or the memory is unretrievable); content-invariance follows, because an address must not depend on what is stored at it. Contamination therefore shows up as **retrieval failure**, which is paid in task reward on the same step. Capacity is the second rent: a frozen random projection from `Cortex` structural modules into `Hippocampus` makes all `∏ᵢ Kᵢ` states stable attractors with `N_slots` growing only **linearly in `Cortex`** — and content-shuffled scaffolds with *learned* bidirectional weights lose the exponential scaling entirely. `D17` |
| Anti-collapse provision, and whether it is a design-time property or a runtime monitor | **Both, and the design-time half comes first.** Design-time: `g` is *architecturally denied* the observation bus, so the constant-encoder solution is not reachable by contaminating it with content — and the `g→Hippocampus` projection is **fixed and random** with a pointwise nonlinearity (a linear layer fails), so there is no weight that can collapse it. Runtime: **CCGP and parallelism score** over an enumerable condition grid, logged per evaluation round — **not decoding accuracy**, which in the one dataset reporting both was statistically unchanged while CCGP rose. `D18` |
| Path-**invariance** residual: formula, when logged | `ρ = ‖g_int − g_rec‖₂ / ‖g_int‖₂` — the integrated code against the code returned by the content-certified recall of step 9b. The content channel is the only available certificate: under non-abelian `W_a` "equal total displacement" is undefined, and node identity is latent. Logged on every above-threshold recall, reported as a distribution, never a mean. `D64` |
| The circularity this buys, stated | `ρ` audits the structural code using the content channel, so a content channel that is itself wrong produces a clean `ρ`. This is not removable within the spec; it is `O28`, and it caps what a §10 row built on `ρ` can claim |
| Attractor regimes, named | `g` is a **continuous** attractor (translation-invariant recurrence; the manifold is `H6`); `Hippocampus` is the **prestructured-scaffold discrete** regime. Denied: a content-defined (Hopfield) `Hippocampus`, whose overload is catastrophic rather than graceful; and a *learnable* scaffold — the one case that unfroze it got worse (`FID_p` 43.41 → 47.76), because the codes stand in dynamical relations a per-code gradient does not respect. `D68` |
| What sets the code's **scale**, and why BIRM currently has exactly one | The interval between `Hippocampus` writes. Between writes the integrator must predict that many steps ahead unaided, and the emergent period scales monotonically with the interval. §5.1 writes every step, so BIRM has a **single fine-scale code and no multi-module code** — the same hole every trained system in the wiki has. Several scales require `n_mod` integrators at distinct write intervals, and the effect is **training-time only**: changing the interval at test time leaves the scale untouched. Correlational payoff: grid score predicts 512-step imagination accuracy (`ρ = 0.783, p = 1e-6`), with counterexamples at low grid score and high accuracy, so periodicity is sufficient and demonstrably not necessary. `D66`, `O24` |
| **How many scales, and where the band edges go** | **Not a spectrum and not a bank — an integer and a set of write intervals** (`D184`). BIRM has no eigenvalue measure to partition: `g` advances by `g_t = ℓ₂(W_{a_t} g_{t−1})` under a computed operator (`D63`), so there is no per-mode decay constant, no magnitude axis and **no phase axis** to allocate over. The whole allocation object is `O24`'s pair `⟨n_mod, {write intervals}⟩`, and `n_mod = ⌈log_K Ŝ⌉` is already derived (`D85`) — so the only free half is the interval set. It is **generated, not chosen**: one scalar ratio times the module's index, spanning a range no wider than ~7×, rather than one interval authored per module. Sign only; the ratio is `?` (`O24`). BIRM's other timescales are not in this object at all — they are set **per organ** by §3 (`Hippocampus` one-shot, `PFC` sustained, `Cortex` transport-only, `Neuromodulators` second-order under `D41`), which is where the row's "how many" is actually answered here. `D184` |
| When a periodic `g` is worth having at all | At **intermediate observation ambiguity**. Grid coding peaks there and *collapses* when observations uniquely identify the state — the code is a compensation for aliasing, not a general consequence of integrating actions. This is **not** a coupling to be swept but the scope test of `D84`: `H1`'s informativeness decides whether the family is in scope at all, and an encoding that resolves the state uniquely has moved the family out of §1 rather than broken §6. *Wave 6a:* `D126` reduces §1's exclusions to this one, so this row now carries the **entire** scope test — and it is a test on the *adapter*, not on the benchmark: a family whose adapter fully rewrites the observation each step has excluded itself regardless of which benchmark it came from. `D67`, `D84`, `D126` |
| Manifold topology: assumed or learned; if assumed, stated here as a designer decision | **Assumed — a designer decision, recorded as `H6`.** Which group the displacements act in is chosen per family and not inferred. Two consequences accepted openly: the code cannot compose a joint space from marginals never sampled together, and a family whose true structure is not of the assumed type will be forced onto it silently. `D20` |
| **Metric or topological — both, split by organ, and the metric half has no metric** | `g` is the path-integrated carrier (`D63`) and `Cortex`'s action-conditioned transition plus `Hippocampus`'s stored edges (`D76`) are the graph, so planning is **search** (§5.2), never vector arithmetic toward a goal: no goal encoding enters the read (`D132`, §2.1(b)) and `D18`'s frozen random projection leaves the address wire with **no similarity structure at all** (`D188`), so there is no distance on `g` to do arithmetic in — the same fact §6 states as "equal total displacement is undefined under non-abelian `W_a`". BIRM therefore holds the metric pole's *carrier* without its *coordinate*. Cost taken openly: the admissible-lower-bound-guiding-a-topological-search pairing has **no site here**, so the rollout is uninformed and terminated by commit failure rather than guided. §12 `O62`. `D193` |
| **Hard constraint or strong default — hard at every site, with no override channel and, for one prior, no detector** | `H6`'s group (`D20`) and `D71`'s effect-equivalence codebook are both installed before the graph and never reweighted; the codebook's merges are irreversible (`D203`) and a mis-assumed group is forced onto the family **silently**. The override channel documented everywhere in the literature is an external symbol system, which is `D4`/`D126`'s non-goal, so the defeasible pole is unbuilt rather than refused. Detection is answered by half: `H8`'s composition is tested by `D78` (§7); `H6`'s *which group* has no test and no residual, since `ρ` audits through the content channel and inherits `O28`'s circularity (`D64`). §12 `O3`; `G47` prices the error. `D220` |
| **Whether the code is domain-general is not a question about `g`** | `g` is denied the observation bus (`D15`, `D65`), so no domain variable is in the address and the code cannot be *spatial* or *social* in the content sense at all. What the domain dispute translates to here is `H6` — the assumed group per family (`D20`), priced by §12 `O3`: not "is the map spatial" but "was one group assumed for a family whose structure is not that group". Two consequences. (i) BIRM's map is **always newly learned**: `D11` re-seeds `g` to a random phase at every `done`, so the over-learned regime in which the domain null was measured does not arise. (ii) **Conjunctive coding of an abstract variable with position is denied in the carrier** by `D45`'s separate wires — position on `Bg`, the abstract variable on `Bx` — and survives only at `Hippocampus`'s write, where the slot *is* a conjunction (`D76`). `D195` |
| **Localised or shared — manufactured in one organ, available in three, and the row's two poles are two different questions** | The dispute is whether the abstract format is built by one structure (2,694 human single units across six regions in an uncued serial reversal task; the inference-linked reorganisation in hippocampus **only**, with amygdala (889 units), vmPFC (463), dACC (310) and preSMA (269) all recorded and all negative on shattering dimensionality, context and parity decodability, and no other region holding two variables in abstract format at once) or is what every module in a control loop converges on (the monkey original of the same task: context, value and action in abstract format in **all three** recorded areas, with action abstract in the prefrontal areas and *not* in hippocampus). Both hold here, of different objects, and the row's either/or dissolves on the distinction. **Manufacture is localised absolutely**: one carrier `g_t = ℓ₂(W_{a_t} g_{t−1})` under one assumed group (`D63`, `D20`), one integrator and no second (`D66`, `O24`), and no organ but `Cortex`'s `g` half advances it. **Availability is shared by construction**: `Bg` delivers that one code to `Hippocampus` as an address, to `PFC` as level context and to `Cortex`'s conjunction over a relay whose destination table belongs to the relay (`D45`, `D65`), so `D18`'s CCGP/PS monitor scores abstract format at three readers, none of which built it. The consumer relationship the localised pole infers is therefore **not entailed by its own evidence** — a null at one reader is a statement about that edge's gain, not about where the code is made. The row's action dissociation is **not inherited**: §0.2 makes organ↔region a naming and not a claim, and BIRM's action variable sits inside `Hippocampus`'s stored conjunction (`D76`) and nowhere in `PFC`, which is the opposite assignment. The `Closes when` is a re-analysis of an existing dataset in the pre-stimulus window and is not BIRM's to run. `D227` |
| **One item set inside several relations — one carrier, `k` operators, and no item register anywhere** | The dispute is whether an entity participating in more than one relational structure gets **one fused map** (two 1-D rank orders learned on separate days producing hippocampal/entorhinal dissimilarity that scales with 2-D Euclidean distance in the product space, better than with either order alone; ~90% of evidence-tuned CA1 cells genuinely conjunctive for evidence × position; replicated at `n` = 203 in a different modality, `t₂₀₂` = 2.69) or **`k` parallel maps plus a selector** (link distance and semantic distance over the same 12 objects appearing in non-overlapping clusters, each effect statistically zero in the other's region of interest, `t₂₂` = −0.25 and 0.54). Neither is BIRM's shape. There is **one** carrier `g` (`D63`) under **one** assumed group per family (`H6`, `D20`), so `k` parallel maps are unavailable — and the fusion pole's *operational* content is unavailable too, since fusion is measured as dissimilarity scaling in a product space and `D18`'s frozen projection leaves the address wire with no distance to scale (`D188`, `D193`). What several relations *are* here is `f_g`'s **`k` computed operators over one carrier**: a relation is an operator, not a coordinate axis. So whether two structures fuse into a product space is a **measurable property of the operator set** — do the two relations' `W_a` commute — which is `H8`'s composition assumption tested by `D78`, and not a storage policy anyone chooses. The row's own reconciliation, that the **acquisition profile** (mode, timescale, consolidation stage) decides, has no site: BIRM runs one wiring at all times and refuses the developmental schedule (`D190`), and `D11` re-seeds `g` at every `done`. Cost, and it is the row's real bite: `Hippocampus` is keyed by a **position** (`D185`) and nothing in BIRM holds an item **identity**, so an entity occupying two nodes under two relations has no slot that is *about it* — "what do I know about entity `e`" is unaskable here for the same reason a goal-conditioned read is (`D132`). §12 `O63`. `D197` |
| **One frame or many — one carrier read out in many places, and the multiplicity is the relay's destination table** | There is one `g` (`D63`) under one assumed group per family (`H6`, `D20`), delivered to `Hippocampus` as an address, to `PFC` as level context and to `Cortex`'s conjunction over a **relayed** edge whose destination table belongs to the relay and not to the sender (`D45`, `D65`). `Cortex`'s `n_mod = ⌈log_K Ŝ⌉` modules are a **factorization of that one frame** (`D85`) and never a population of frames: no second integrator (`D66`, `O24`), no arbiter organ (§3.6), no per-frame readout weight — so concurrent frames and voting over them have no site. Registration is not achieved but **unnecessary**, one code being trivially commensurate with itself, and the weighting that arbitration would reduce to is already §4.1's per-edge terminal gain set by `U`. Two consequences: BIRM has one orientation and predicts no dissociation under a conflict design, and `D11` re-seeds `g` to a random phase at every `done` so no relative angle survives an episode either; and a probe recovering two independently oriented codes is evidence of an integrator §3 does not have — an arm on `T10`. `D221` |
| **A multiscale decomposition imposed or discovered — imposed, because discovery's mechanism is denied and imposition's register is not the spectral one** | Discovery needs **depth** — the decomposition appears across a stack's layers — and BIRM has no stack: `g` advances by one recurrence with a computed operator (`D63`), the front end is a single feedforward pass (`D71`, `D164`), and top-down depth is not a spendable axis here (`D175`, `O56`). Imposition is therefore the only available pole and it is imposed in the register BIRM has: `D184`'s pair `⟨n_mod, {write intervals}⟩`, with no eigenvalue spectrum to stratify and no band front end. The two constraints this puts on `O24`'s open half are carried there. `D225` |
| Node set / discretisation: what supplies it | **The adapter, as `H3`** — for the *stream*. Stated here rather than hidden: this is the part of latent-graph discovery the spec does not attempt, and the conservation law means the amount conceded is unmeasured. `D21` |
| What BIRM discretises *on top of* the adapter | **Effect-equivalence.** A binary bottleneck inside an effect predictor — `b = round(σ(f_enc(o)))`, `ê = g_dec(b, a_{t−1})`, `L = ‖ê − (o_t − o_{t−1})‖²` — so `b(x₁) = b(x₂) ⟺ ∀a ∈ A: effect(x₁,a) ≈ effect(x₂,a)`. `Bx`'s content code is then typed **by consequence, not by appearance**, and each code arrives with its own applicability mask (`⟨precondition, action, effect⟩` is a typed edge). This is the only carving criterion in the wiki whose supervision comes from outside the observation stream, and BIRM can compute it because it has a repertoire. `D71` |
| What that does *not* fix | The classes are **repertoire-limited**: two things the action set cannot separate never get two codes, however different they look, and no system reports the shortfall (`O25`). It also does not remove `H3` — it puts a second discretisation *inside* BIRM, so the residual is the composition of the adapter's carve and BIRM's own |
| **How an edge is estimated — by acting, and the observation model is fitted before the graph and never jointly** | Every estimator on the closed ladder — correlation, partial correlation, Granger causality and transfer entropy, model inversion — reads edges off a **co-statistic of a passively observed signal**, and the measured cost is that the observable is a path sum `Σ_k w_ik w_kj` rather than the adjacency matrix: ≈6% precision at 80% recall on a 3%-dense graph, ≈28% even when the generating couplings are exact. BIRM is not on that ladder. An edge is written when it is **traversed**, as the conjunction `(Δ, a_{t−1}, r_t)` at address `g_t` (`D76`), and the transition is fitted action-conditioned (`D88`), so conditioning on the action code breaks the path closure that produces the confound; the excitation that makes this hold is a declared quantity rather than a hope, counterfactual error being `δ/ρ_tr` with a floor held by `Neuromodulators` (`D90`, `D91`). BIRM also **has** the component the row says nothing here has — an estimated observation model: `D71`'s effect-equivalence bottleneck is a fitted encoder–decoder pair. It is fitted **sequentially and never jointly** (P0 trains the codebook, P1 trains `Cortex` with it frozen), which is available only because the codebook's supervision comes from *outside* the observation stream — the action repertoire — so the joint-identifiability question the row asks is sidestepped rather than answered. The price is that the staging is irreversible: a distinction P0's carve merges, `Cortex` can never unmerge. `O25` therefore prices a **sequencing** cost and not only a coverage one. `D203` |
| **Objects: in the architecture or in a read-out — the read-out's criterion compiled into the architecture** | The dispute is entities as a **factor of the latent** (a fixed slot count `K`, soft attention bottlenecks, trained on prediction or contrast, delivering variables a reasoner can bind) against entities as a **post-hoc read-out** (no object variable and no `K`, entities recovered as clusters of the pairwise correlation between an unconstrained model's *counterfactual* responses, beating a supervised segmenter on real imagery where the slot lineage is reported not to scale). BIRM is neither and takes one half of each: `D71` is a **training-time bottleneck** — architectural, in the latent, never a query — whose **individuating criterion is interventional**, `b(x₁) = b(x₂) ⇔ ∀a ∈ A: effect(x₁,a) ≈ effect(x₂,a)`, which is the read-out pole's counterfactual-response criterion written as a loss instead of as a probe. The read-out pole's stated cost is thereby avoided rather than paid: nothing is distilled, so nothing deletes the query, and the `R × T` rollouts are spent once in P0 instead of per segment at test time. What BIRM **forfeits is the architectural pole's payoff**: the codebook is a code over the observation with no `K` and no slots, so "which object" is never a variable and there is nothing for a planner to bind to — the hole `O63` already names from the relational side, reached here from the perceptual side. And the limit the two poles share is BIRM's too: the classes are repertoire-limited (`O25`), exactly as a counterfactual read-out is limited by the intervention set it pokes with. `D208` |
| Anti-collapse monitor #2, and why it is needed | The **split-recombine test**: factorize iff the effect predictor's loss is unchanged when `b` is split and the parts recombined. It needs no enumerable condition grid, so it survives exactly where CCGP/PS cannot be computed — which `O9` flags as likely on the primary benchmark. `D72` |
| Free structural instrument (no data) | Attractor–eigenvector alignment and convergence time against a **degree-preserving permutation of the same weights**: computable from `Hippocampus`'s weight matrix alone. The wiki's only probe that is not label-dependent, and therefore the only §10 candidate that cannot be gamed by the evaluation family. `D82` |

**The allocate-vs-reuse prior is the `g→Hippocampus` projection's fan-out density** — the same projection `D18` froze. Sweeping a context→store projection from fully random toward one-to-one moves the fitted Dirichlet `α` at `r = 0.76, p < 2×10⁻⁴` and moves nothing else. So `G38`'s separation/completion bias is not a hyperparameter BIRM has to tune; it is a fan-out statistic readable off a frozen weight matrix (`D73`).

**What §6 does not have** (`G30`): no objective function is maximised by a path-consistent `g` and minimised by a contaminated one. Everything above is *architecture* and *rent* — the code is built and made to pay, never **trained toward**. The nearest shapes in the wiki are a relations-only Gram penalty (label-free, but no path term and within-input only) and long-range predictability (rewards content-invariance, says nothing about commutativity). Recorded as §12 `O5`.

**`O5` stays open, and the collapse literature is not what closes it.** It supplies no objective preferring a path-consistent `g`; what it supplies is a **derived target for the anti-collapse half** — at fixed total variance, anisotropy raises both the bias and the variance of a linear probe, and among distributions under a scalar covariance constraint the **isotropic Gaussian uniquely minimises** the integrated squared bias of nearest-neighbour and kernel probes. BIRM installs the isotropic-Gaussian marginal on the content encoder (`D87`). It is silent about paths. Three narrowings come with it:

| Narrowing | Consequence for §6 |
|---|---|
| Certifiable in the **target**, not the trajectory | A loss whose minimum is provably non-collapsed still collapses over most of its coefficient space. Stating `D18`'s minimum certifies nothing about the run |
| Certifiable **per read-out**, not globally | Two probes on one encoder moved in opposite directions across 1M iterations of a run in which no term diverged. `D18`'s CCGP/PS monitor must therefore be joined by a probe on whatever read-out §10 will actually use, and both plotted against training time (`D94`) |
| Possibly not certifiable from the loss at all | A supervised objective with **no degenerate minimum** collapses anyway (20.73% top-1, recovering to 72.02% with an isotropy term added), on the reading that the driving term is the optimiser's flux rather than the loss's shape. Every provision is then a **drift control** first |

| **No discardable head exists** | The space BIRM constrains and the space its readers read are one space — §4 defines a space by who may read it, and no projector stands between the `x` encoder and `Cortex`, `Hippocampus` or `PFC`. The literature's cheapest provision, *hosting* a degenerate solution in a head thrown away at read-out, therefore has no site here; `D87`'s isotropy term is paid on the read space itself. §12 `O46`. `D139` |

**The frozen random `g→Hippocampus` projection is kept and the multiplicative conjunction `p = g̃ ⊙ x̃` is declined** (`D17`, `D18`). The frozen projection buys exponentially many convex attractors with `N_slots` linear in the module count; learning those weights destroys the exponential scaling. The conjunction costs `×n_c` units per additional bound region, its transformer rewrite predicts *random* remapping, and a spiking rewrite recovers the grid code only after the conjunction is **deleted**. BIRM's `f` is therefore an **addressed write, not a binding operator** — `g` is the address of `Hippocampus` and the content is what is stored at the slot, which is also what makes `g` structure-determining and content-free in one object (`D181`), and what makes the projection a **fixed converter with no parameters and no loss of its own** (`D183`) — and the payoff the conjunction is credited with, non-random remapping, is **forfeited rather than reproduced**: the projection is random and `g` re-initialises to a random phase at every `done` (`D11`). `D165`

---

## 7. Action-vocabulary rebinding

> The same action code must be able to mean something different in a new environment. Where no relation exists between cue and consequence, no mechanism can be cheaper than one-pair-at-a-time binding — so the design question is *binding speed and where the binding is stored*, not a clever transform.

| Slot | Fill |
|---|---|
| Test that decides whether this domain's actions compose | **Operational, not structural** — no structural test exists (`G41`, and the wiki's own counterexample, a bare social network, has no diagnostic). BIRM runs the two addressing schemes on the same stream and compares **retrieval**: the path-integrated address `g` against a per-observation clone-pool address fitted by expectation–maximisation. Actions compose for this family iff the integrated address wins on re-visit retrieval and on transfer to a held-out instance-graph; if the clones win, §6 is void for that family. The test is therefore a §9 pipeline stage, not a pre-flight check, and it costs one extra store. `D78` |
| Path if they compose | §6 as written: one `g`, `k` operators `W_a = f_g(a)`, `Hippocampus` addressed by `g`, transfer free because all positions are treated alike (a new node needs no new cell and no new synapses, and re-alignment to a same-shaped environment is not a graph-matching problem) |
| Path if they do not | The clone pool becomes the address: de-alias fast, locally, per environment, transferring nothing (`O4`). The merge of the two is named in the wiki as an obvious unbuilt model. BIRM's fallback is the clone pool **plus** the unchanged `Hippocampus` protocol — the store does not care where its address came from, which is the one thing `D17` buys here |
| Where a rebinding is written | **In `Hippocampus`, as an edge, and never in weights.** The represented object is the *pairing*: ~50% of prefrontal cells code the cue×action conjunction against ~30% cue and ~20% action, with reversal (marginals fixed, edge set flipped) as the control, and the tuning is **nonlinear** — activity for one pairing is not predictable from the others. Two consequences BIRM must honour: (i) the write content is the conjunction `(Δ, a_{t−1}, r_t)` (`D76`), because the binder cannot form cue×action×outcome from the sensory stream alone and needs the efference copy; (ii) a binding module fed only by the observation encoder cannot learn what it is for, which is how most memory-augmented architectures in the wiki are wired |
| What is rebound, and what is not | Rebound: the **action→operator generator** `f_g : a ↦ W_a`, `k` operators, plus the `Hippocampus` edges written under it. Preserved **across environments**: `g`'s integrator dynamics, the `g→Hippocampus` projection, `Cortex`'s associative weights, the effect-equivalence codebook. So a rebinding is a re-fit of one small generator, not of the model. `D77`, amended by `D102` |
| What is *not* preserved across **bodies** | The **effect-equivalence codebook**. `D71` defines a class by `∀a ∈ A: effect(x₁,a) ≈ effect(x₂,a)`, quantified over *this* repertoire, so a change of body changes the quantifier and the codebook dies with it. Wave 4 listed it as preserved without distinguishing the two cases; `D102` supersedes `D77` on that cell. The field's own practice agrees in shape and disagrees in emphasis — one interface is routinely fitted above 22 robot types, and in **every** such family a body-specific decoder is still there underneath, so the repertoire-relative part is *relocated, never removed* (`T172`). What survives a body change in BIRM is `g`, `Cortex`'s associative weights and the `Hippocampus` protocol; what dies is `f_g`, the codebook, and the adapter |
| **Size** of the per-body part — `O29` closed | It is not an unreported number after all, because BIRM already derived it: the per-body part is `f_g` plus the adapter, and `D85` makes `f_g` a generator emitting `k` operators of size `K×K`. So the decoder size is a function of `⟨k, K⟩` and nothing new is open.
