The literature's version of this question stays open in the literature — no source reports what its shared interface *cannot express* about any member body — but BIRM does not inherit it. `D102` |
| Second acceptance number, measured on a brain | State-machine reuse with sensory-leaf rebinding: an established latent-state machine relearned for an **unfamiliar cue pair in 147 ± 39 trials against 483 ± 70** for the original — a ~3.3× speedup — with population correlation between old-cue and new-cue trials high **everywhere except the cue region**. That is `D77`'s claim (rebind the leaf, keep the machine) measured, and it is the number §10 scores structure reuse against, distinct from `D79`'s ≤8 trials/cue for arbitrary bindings, which measures binding speed on a *new* leaf rather than reuse of an old machine. `D103` |
| Budget | **≤ 8 trials/cue to <10% error at `k = 3`**; ~3 trials/cue to substantial learning; 24 new mappings/day sustained in an experienced learner. `MEASURED` on the biology and adopted as the acceptance number — no gradient step is available at that timescale, which is why the write is one-shot and lives in `Hippocampus` |
| What licenses a particular binding | **Unsolved, and BIRM inherits it.** Nothing says how the network decides that *this* cue binds to *that* action rather than to the other pairings available in the trial; reinforcement is the only stated selector and is too slow and too coarse for 3 trials/cue. BIRM's cheapest candidate is the responsibility posterior — one quantity controlling creation, expression and updating at once (`D75`) — with the context supplying what no structural relation can. `O30` |
| The strategy floor, and why it is not installed | Repeat-Stay/Change-Shift is a **separately stored object** (it survives fast-store ablation while new bindings fail, and dies with the rule store while the bindings' substrate is untouched) and is worth exactly `E = ⅓·0 + ⅔·0.5 = 33%` error at `k = 3`. BIRM installs none of it: §1's control task exists precisely to detect it, and at `k = 2` it is a *complete* solution, which is why S5 forbids two-alternative scoring. `D79` |
| What an abstraction layer buys here | **A rate multiplier, not an enabler.** Higher-order rules are not necessary to acquire the exemplars — they change the constant in front of `O(n)` by pruning which input dimensions are candidates, and cannot change the exponent, because arbitrary edges have no shared content to factor out. `D29`'s `k` parallel level-searchers must therefore not be expected to buy a sample-complexity *class*; expecting that is expecting something the biology does not deliver |
| Curriculum knob (the one lever that moves which mechanism runs) | **Cue–choice contiguity.** Tasks that *could* be solved by arbitrary binding are instead solved by approach-by-valence when cue and choice are contiguous in time and space; separating them is what recruits the binding path. A benchmark that leaves contiguity uncontrolled does not know which mechanism it is scoring — which is a constraint on §1's secondary family, not a free parameter |
| What is preserved across *bodies* (the body-independent level) | `g`, `Cortex` and the `Hippocampus` protocol; the per-body part is `f_g` plus the adapter. Consistent with the practice of fitting one action interface above many embodiments while keeping a small per-body decoder underneath — the repertoire-relative part is relocated, never removed (`T172`). The **size** of that decoder is **derived, not open**: `f_g` emits `k` operators of size `K×K` (`D85`), so it is a function of `⟨k, K⟩`. §12 `O29` closed by `D102`, which also corrects this row — the effect-equivalence codebook was listed as body-independent and is not |
| Engagement signal, re-confirmed | The acquisition network is driven *harder* by a set with a hidden rule than by a matched ruleless set that is **harder, more uncertain and generates more error feedback** — so engagement is neither error, novelty, uncertainty nor effort. This is an independent confirmation of `D30`'s four refusals, and the discriminating machine run is the same design: train on a structured task and a matched scrambled one, and require an internal quantity to separate them *opposite* to loss |

**What §7 does not have.** No mechanism converts a set of specific bindings into the rule over them, and the extraction cannot be driven by failure to acquire the exemplars, since the rule is not needed to acquire them — so the abstraction layer has **no named error signal** in this section or anywhere in the wiki. Recorded as `O31`.

---

## 8. Exploration vs. exploitation

> Requirement: derived from a quantity the agent already computes, and evaluated *inside* the selector — not an external schedule, not a decayed epsilon.

> **Four incompatible quantities circulate under the name "epistemic value", and picking the wrong one is not a tuning error — it is a different agent.** §8's first job is to choose a row.

| # | Quantity | Over what distribution | Pays for | Status in BIRM |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | `Φ = −Σ_t H(ρ_t^π)` | predictive **state marginal** | *spreading* — coverage of the state space | refused: BIRM's unknown is not which states it will occupy |
| E2 | `I_a(b) = H(b) − E_o[H(b′_o)]` | belief over a **fixed hidden state** | *sharpening* — concentration of the belief | refused as the primary term; it is the one with a derived weight, and it scores the wrong object |
| **E3** | **novelty** `= D_KL[ q(θ\|y,x) ‖ q(θ\|x) ]` | belief over **model parameters** | information gain about the model itself | **adopted** (`D95`) |
| E4 | ambiguity `= E H[q(y\|x,θ)]`, penalised | **observation kernel** given state and parameters | reducing how equivocal the sensor is | inert: BIRM has no action that changes its own sensor (§2.1 `D83`) |

| Slot | Fill |
|---|---|
| Epistemic quantity, with formula | **E3 — information gain about `Cortex`'s associative weights**, the parameters in which the meta-graph is held: `ε_t(a) = D_KL[ q(W \| Bx⁺_{t+1}, g_t, a) ‖ q(W \| g_t) ]`. Chosen by construction rather than by comparison: BIRM's unknown *is* the graph, and a graph enters the generative model as a **parameter**, not as a state. `D95` |
| Which distribution it is defined over (the sign of the term depends on this) | The **parameter** posterior, and the sign is a **bonus for expected belief change** — never a penalty for disagreement between the forward model and the encoder. Both appear in published implementations under one name, and the second collapses the policy. `D97` |
| The audit that separates a real epistemic term from an impostor | **If an agent that freezes its own policy can drive the term to zero, it is a self-consistency penalty, not an information gain.** E1 and E3 pass — an occupancy cannot be made to spread, nor a parameter posterior to move, by refusing to act. The estimator that fails it fails *measurably*: action-prior entropy → 0, the action histogram almost entirely one action, the transition network's variance head low only for that action. §10 carries it as a mandatory row (`D97`) |
| Where it enters the action score | Inside the selector, at §5.2 row 6, **added late and separately gated**: pragmatic term first, epistemic term as a `U`-gated addition `Neuromodulators` can zero. Not a schedule, not an `ε`, not a decayed bonus. `D98` |
| Its weight | **`w = 1` nat**, `ASSUMED`. There *is* a derived weight — expected free energy is exactly planning-plus-information-gain at `w = 1` — but the derivation is over E2 and BIRM uses E3, so BIRM takes the number for its **transfer property** rather than for its derivation: tuned weights vary 5× across four environments and every transferred weight underperforms `w = 1` on at least one target, while `w = 1` sits at the Pareto knee of the success–reward frontier on every environment tested. The honest cost: the success-maximising weight is 20–200× the derived one, so an agent that must be *right* rather than *profitable* has no derivation to appeal to. §12 `O34`. `D98` |
| Its metaparameter, and the second-order statistic that sets it | Two, and only the second is new. (i) `β`, already governed by `D42`'s laws (`V` extreme ⇒ `β ↑`; `Var_a Q ↑ ⇒ β ↓`). (ii) **The excitation floor**: `ρ_tr = λ_min(E_g[Cov(a\|g)])` computed from BIRM's own action log with no model, and `ρ_tr` below floor ⇒ `β ↑`. This is the seventh `Neuromodulators` law (`D91`) and the only one justified by *model estimation* rather than by return — counterfactual error is `δ/ρ_tr`, and every mechanism for improving an actor drives `ρ_tr → 0` while on-policy loss keeps falling |
| Behaviour at each pipeline stage (§9) | **P0** — term inert; the collector is heterogeneous random play, so `ρ_tr` is maximal by construction and there is nothing to buy. **P1** — term **dominant**: `Cortex` is the thing being identified, so E3 is the objective's own gradient rather than a bonus on top of it. **P2** — term **off**; `Cortex` is frozen, so there is no parameter posterior to move and the only live question is control. **P3** — term **on, floored**: `Neuromodulators` holds `ρ_tr` above the floor by dither, and the free-running rollout gate (`D62`) is what a stuck disposition would show up in |
| What makes the term **additive over a rollout** | Per-step novelty decomposes from the global information gain about `W` **only if the parameter belief is held still during the rollout**; let it update inside the rollout and per-probe gains double-count and stop summing. §3.1 already forbids online writes to `Cortex` — it is written only on the transport channel — so BIRM gets additivity by construction and pays nothing for it. The biology separates the same two updates anatomically: state uncertainty valued and updated in orbitofrontal cortex, parameter uncertainty valued in rostral middle frontal gyrus and updated in precentral gyrus. `D96` |
| The term that argues **against** exploration, and where BIRM already has its address | The variational derivation of the plan posterior carries a third term the standard formula omits: `q*(u) = σ(−P(u) − G(u) − C(u))`, where `C(u)` is the **belief-change cost** of the plan — a laziness term with the shape of a description-length penalty. Every expected-free-energy agent in the wiki omits it. BIRM does not have to invent it: `C(u)` is priced as **`scope`** (`D48`), the field on `W` saying how many levels a revision reaches, so `C(u) ∝ scope(u)` and the term is a read of a register that already exists. `D100` |
| No collision with `Neuromodulators`'s horizon law | Two different `γ`s. `Neuromodulators`'s `γ` discounts a *learned return estimate* on the `V` path; the `γ` in the measured reversal is the **planning recursion's**, `V(obs_k) = −c_k + I_k(b) + γ·E_o[max_a V(a, b′_o)]`, over an already-known model. BIRM's rollout has no such term — §5.2 scores against priors over observation trajectories rather than solving for a value function. `D107` |
| Why a discount could not have moved the trade-off anyway | Unrolling that recursion puts **every** term at depth `k` — cost `c_k`, information gain `I_k`, and the commit reward — at weight `γ^k`. A discount is a *uniform weighting over depth*, not a differential weighting between the epistemic and pragmatic terms, so it cannot change their ratio at any fixed depth. The source's own summary agrees and points the other way: `I_k(b)` enters **undiscounted at the current step** while the continuation is discounted, so a lower `γ` produces *more* exploratory behaviour early, and performance is reported **robust across `γ ∈ {0.9, 0.95, 0.99, 1.0}`**. The apparent asymmetry comes entirely from *where the two payoffs sit on the depth axis* — information now, reward at the commit — which is the same observation the hyperbolic-kernel literature makes about the same axis. `D108` |
| What the measured reversal actually measures, and the requirement it forces | **Depth, in probes.** The advantage falls only on **multi-observation** environments and the mechanism is that discounting truncates the effective horizon below the number of probes that must be *chained* before a confident commit; on the single-observation environment the same agent is insensitive to `γ` across the whole range `[0.90, 1.0]`. Independently: at planning horizon `H = 1` the term degenerates to myopic information gain no matter what `γ` is. BIRM's requirement is therefore stated in the units the effect is actually in — **the rollout must be able to chain `H ≥ 2` information-gathering commits before a pragmatic one** — and not as a floor on any discount. `D108` |
| Where that requirement is charged | Not to `Neuromodulators`. BIRM's rollout depth is set by **commit failure** (`D53`, `D62`), so the quantity that decides whether two probes can be chained is the commit gate's *pre-commit resting distance from threshold* — which is `O18`'s stability budget, the knob already known to set take-off, decision speed and the admission threshold together. "How deep can BIRM plan" and "how easily does it commit" are **one knob**, and §8's depth requirement is a second claim on it. `D109` |
| The leak test | The two `γ`s are only separate if nothing wires them together, and the discriminating run is the one the source already published in reverse: on a family where **one** probe suffices, BIRM's score must be **insensitive** to `γ` across `[0.90, 1.0]`; on a family needing `n` chained probes, its score must fall exactly when achievable rollout depth drops below `n` — and not before. Sensitivity on the single-probe family means the estimation discount has leaked into the rollout scorer and `D107` is not implemented. §10 row (i). `D107` |
| What BIRM must not do with this quantity | Reuse it as a **replay priority**. Same statistic, opposite verdict: good to act on, harmful to rehearse on, because a bonus is consumed when acted on and a priority is persistent. §5.3's refusal of uncertainty-proportional sampling (`D57`) and §8's adoption of E3 are consistent only because the two never share a weight. `D105` |
| **Second factor** | `expressed competence ≈ representation × motivation`. The affect channel is **not** a term added to the return: it is a standing dispositional gate on whether a class of computation is run at all, read by `Neuromodulators` at second order (`D70`, closes `O6`). Two within-species manipulations carry it — the same agent succeeds or fails at one cooperation task depending only on whether the reward is dispersed or monopolisable, and social learning is abolished outright by intolerance, with the machinery held fixed in both. No architecture in the wiki has such a state (`G102`); what *sets* the disposition is `O26` |

---

## 9. Training pipeline

> **The pipeline has four stages and one of the template's four is deleted.** `P2 behaviour cloning` is refused outright (`D92`): distilling a planner into a reactive policy fits a *deterministic* `A(s)`, which is precisely the operation that drives `ρ_tr → 0` and makes the data the agent subsequently collects unable to identify the model it plans with — while its on-policy prediction loss falls throughout and reports nothing. The slot is re-used for a controller fit with `Cortex` frozen.

| Stage | Data collector / policy | Objective | What is frozen | What is logged | Exit criterion | Known hazard |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **P0 vocabulary** | **Heterogeneous random play** across the declared family. Excitation is bought from *source heterogeneity*, not from dither — a union of unrelated behaviours has high conditional action variance by construction and costs no return (`D91`) | (i) `D71`'s effect-equivalence bottleneck: `b = round(σ(f_enc(o)))`, `ê = g_dec(b, a_{t−1})`, `L = ‖ê − (o_t − o_{t−1})‖²`. (ii) `D78`'s two-addressing-scheme race — path-integrated `g` against a per-observation clone pool fitted by expectation–maximisation, compared on **re-visit retrieval** | nothing (`Cortex` does not exist yet) | distinct effect-equivalence class count; `ρ_tr`; `γ_rep`; the `D78` verdict; the merged-pair audit (`O25`) | class count plateaus **and** `D78` returns a verdict. `Ŝ` is read off the class count, closing `O32` for this family | The **only reconstruction term in BIRM** lives here (`D104`) and carries the object-vanishing hazard: a pixel-area loss makes small task-critical structure cheap to lose. It is confined to the codebook trainer, predicts a *delta* rather than a frame, and never appears on the rollout path. If `D78` returns "clones win", §6 is void for this family and P1 runs on the clone address instead (`O4`) |
| **P1 pre-training** | The P0 collector **kept running unchanged** — this is the answer to the cross-stage question below, and it is the cheapest implementation of the excitation floor | Action-conditioned next-content prediction in representation space over the **family**, with the `x` encoder held to an isotropic-Gaussian marginal (`D86`, `D87`). No decoder, no reward term, no task index | the `g→Hippocampus` projection (`D18`), the P0 codebook, `f_g`'s form | `γ_rep`, **`ρ_tr`**, `ρ`'s distribution (`D64`), CCGP/PS (`D18`), split–recombine (`D72`), the free structural instrument (`D82`), **and a per-read-out probe plotted against training time** | **The read-out probe's peak, not loss convergence** (`D94`), crossed with the learning-to-learn curve: steps-to-criterion on a *fresh* instance-graph of the same family stops falling | Improving the policy destroys model identifiability (`G63`) — held off here only because the collector never improves. **Partial collapse**: a read-out can be destroyed while no term diverges, no encoder goes constant and the aggregate probe rises monotonically. Training horizon is a design variable with a coefficient in nobody's objective (`O36`) |
| **P2 controller fit** *(replaces behaviour cloning)* | BIRM acting, with `Neuromodulators`'s excitation floor live | `PFC`'s task model by reward-gated association among co-active units; the gate/schedule outputs (`L_cmd`) by reinforcement with per-slot credit `δ_j = gate_j · δ` | **`Cortex` entirely** — associative weights and codes both | `L_part` gate statistics; the read-schedule failure class (§3.2 `F-Hipp`); `ρ_tr` | `PFC`'s level-engagement entropies stabilise (`D30`) | The template's hazard is real and measured elsewhere: a randomly-initialised control interface starts **below** the no-interface baseline and takes ~200 steps to overtake it, so the initialisation transient is a fact rather than a worry. BIRM's answer is the freeze — the transient cannot reach `Cortex` because `Cortex` is not trainable in this stage |
| **P3 deployment** | BIRM. The P0 collector is retired; its **floor is not** — `Neuromodulators` holds `ρ_tr` above threshold by dither (`D91`), paying return for identifiability on purpose | none — no gradient step is taken online. `Cortex` moves only on §5.3's transport channel, `Hippocampus` writes one-shot, `f_g` is re-fit per environment (`D77`) | codes and associative weights of `Cortex` between rest periods; the `g→Hippocampus` projection; the codebook (this body only, `D102`) | §10 in full, plus the **primitive-only control run** (`D101`) and the **freeze-the-policy audit** (`D97`) | none — this is deployment | Rumination: the commit gate free-runs (`D62`), so always-on `g_rel` is a reachable state and must be exhibitable. The self-undermining excitation loop returns the moment the floor is switched off. `D60`'s replay tags are deliberately stale |

**Cross-stage decisions:**

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| Is a random-play collector kept running alongside the improving policy? | **Yes, through P1, and its floor survives into P3 as a dither term.** Not a hedge: `ρ_tr` is a property of the *collector* and not of the architecture or the loss, counterfactual error is `δ/ρ_tr`, and the only number anyone reports — on-policy prediction error — is blind to it. The one system in the wiki whose action model works at scale got its excitation for free from **human teleoperation across many operators including failures**, and nobody framed it that way. `D91`, `D92` |
| What crosses a stage boundary unchanged, and what is re-initialised? | Crosses unchanged: the codebook (P0→P1→P3), the frozen `g→Hippocampus` projection, `Cortex`'s associative weights (P1→P2→P3), `g`'s integrator dynamics. Re-initialised at every `done` regardless of stage: `Hippocampus`, and `g` to a random phase (`D11`). Re-fitted per environment: `f_g` and the `Hippocampus` edges under it (`D77`). Dies with the body: `f_g`, the codebook, the adapter (`D102`) |
| Gain/learning-rate schedule at each boundary (the interface, not the module) | **There is no schedule, because there is no re-tuning.** `D85` makes every dimension a function of `⟨D_o, k, Ŝ⟩`, and `Neuromodulators` computes every gain and learning rate from the learner's own second-order statistics (`D42`), so a stage boundary cannot be a hyperparameter event. What a boundary *is* is a **licensing change**: which `L_part` gates are open and which organs may receive a learning signal at all. P1→P2 closes `Cortex`'s gate; P2→P3 closes every gradient gate and leaves only the transport channel. The interface is the `L` bus, and the schedule is one row of it |
| What P1 must be trained on | An environment **family**, not an environment. Transfer is bought by gradient training across structurally similar worlds and by nothing else: the learning-to-learn curve — many visits per node early, correct on the *second* visit late, regardless of the edge taken — is the acquisition of the structure and the memory policy together, and it is the curve P1's exit criterion reads. The price is stated where it belongs, in §3.1: a genuinely novel structure is not acquirable in one traversal, which is what `O4`'s clone fallback exists for |

---

## 10. Acceptance tests

> One row per organ, per bus, and per claim class. A test whose failure mode is "we would notice" is not a test.

> **§10 is not an invention site.** Twenty-five certification instruments already exist, are numbered `I1`–`I25`, and **none is sufficient**; fourteen failure modes `F1`–`F14` are shared across them rather than belonging to any one. So §10's job is (i) to **select** from that menu, (ii) to **declare which failure modes each selection inherits**, and (iii) to state what the section therefore may not claim. An instrument's cost decides whether it is ever run, so the menu below is ordered cheapest-first and the cheapest rows are the ones made mandatory. `D112`
>
> **No emergence claims.** Which objective a system optimises is not recoverable from its representations, and which representation an objective yields is not recoverable from the objective. BIRM therefore makes **no emergence claims at all**: every structural property in §6 is a *decision* with a ledger row, never a property that fell out. §11 is not documentation of §10 — it is the substitute for the claims §10 is not allowed to license. `D118`

---

### 10.0 The instrument menu — what BIRM buys, and what each purchase costs

| Instrument | What BIRM uses it for | Cost | Failure modes inherited |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I8** support/query split | `F-Cortex`: induce structure on one instance-graph, execute on a second from the same family. The gap between self-fit and transfer *is* the measurement | Nothing beyond the family generator | `F5` (the adapter fixes the representation both halves run in) |
| **I9** consistency score, **restricted to items the system got wrong** | `F-Bg`/`ρ`: identical *incorrect* behaviour across two routes to one latent state cannot both be explained by being right | A declared invariance — BIRM's is path-invariance, `D64` | `O28`'s circularity: the invariance is certified through the content channel |
| **I2** signature limits + resource stress | `F-PFC1`, `F-NM`: certify an installed prior by a constant that survives scale, retraining and family shift, then shrink the budget | Repeated runs only | `F8` — a model with **no** prior also shows an invariant failure profile. Repair applied: perturb the resource the failure could otherwise be charged to |
| **I4** re-goaling | `F-Cortex` second arm: hold the transition structure, swap what `r_t` marks, measure retraining | An adapter switch | `C4` — a multi-task-trained system passes without the abilities sharing one model |
| **I7** linear probing | **Refused as a primary instrument.** Used only as a monitor (§6 read-out probe, `D94`), never as evidence | — | `F4` — probe labels are two guesses deep: the same Othello activations read 75% under one label set and **99.6%** under another |
| **I11**-style declared held-out abstraction | The repurposed-skill family (§1 `D3`): declare which axis of the family is withheld and report every regime against a blind baseline | A family generator with an enumerable axis | `F1`, `F2` — a held-out axis is a depleting stock |
| **I13** accuracy-**profile** correlation | The primary human comparison (`D116`). A system at the human aggregate can correlate **0.06** with the human item-type profile and sit *below chance* on the one item type authored to catch its heuristic | A per-item human profile — ARC-AGI-3 does not publish one (`O40`) | `F10` — the reference system is a protocol, not a number |
| **I22**-style additive ladder | `F-PFC2`, `F-Thal`: environments differing by exactly one named irrelevancy, including an **inert** rung where the added structure is switched off | A parameterised family | Exact-match on a single gold plan assumes plan uniqueness; reported SD can exceed the mean |
| **I25** set-size sweep with polarity split | **A chunk detector for `g`** — a code that has collapsed the relation to an analog scalar gets *better* as the element count rises and breaks asymmetrically on the polarity sitting at zero | Any generator exposing an arity parameter. The cheapest instrument in the inventory | Unrun anywhere in the wiki; no precedent to calibrate against |
| **I5** generalization difficulty `GD` | **Not computed.** ARC-AGI-3 approximates it by *construction* — priors equalised by the core-knowledge-only rule, difficulty equalised by the ≥2-of-10 gate — and measures `E` alone | — | `C2`, `G31`: `GD` still has no approximation, and BIRM inherits the benchmark's authoring rather than a quantity |
| **I15** rule-level evaluation | **Unavailable in kind** — natural language is outside §2.1's stream contract (`D8`, `D83`; formerly cited as §1 `D4`(iii), re-sited by `D126` with the outcome unchanged), so BIRM cannot state a rule. Substituted by `D123`'s plan–trajectory alignment | — | The substitute has no author-declared abstraction to classify against, only the environment's state graph |

**The `F8` null, unconditional:** every per-property effect size in §10 is reported over a null of `n = 20` **randomly-initialised** BIRMs of the same architecture pushed through the identical scoring pipeline — one forward pass per seed. A number quoted without that null quotes an unknown chance level. `D124`

---

### 10.1 Organ tests

| # | What it tests | Protocol | Pass condition | Known counterexample class it does **not** rule out |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **T1** `F-Cortex` | That `Cortex` holds the meta-graph rather than the instance | **Reverse ablation** (`I8`): freeze `Cortex`'s associative weights and train the codes instead; then run the support/query split — induce on instance-graph `A`, execute on `B` from the same family with no weight update | Transport **fails** under the reversed freeze, and the self-fit/transfer gap on the normal configuration is small where the frozen-associator gap is total. A system reading `0.994` self-fit / `0.000` transfer encoded the answer, not the mechanism | A family whose instance-graphs are near-isomorphic, where instance and meta are not separable at all. Does not touch `F5`: both halves run in the adapter's vertex set (`H3`) |
| **T2** `F-Cortex2` | That `Cortex` is scored **planner-in-the-loop**, never by a prediction loss (mandatory row (a)) | `I4` re-goaling: hold the family's transitions fixed, move what `r_t` marks, measure environment steps to threshold `θ`. Report against the same measurement on a matched-parameter flat baseline | Retraining cost is materially below the baseline's, and `Cortex`'s rollout throughput (`D89`) rather than its per-candidate accuracy is what tracks the difference | `C4` — a BIRM trained across many reward placements passes without any of it flowing through one model |
| **T3** `F-Hipp` | That the **read schedule** is scored separately from the contents (mandatory row (c)) — **plus the dual-task arm** (`D200`): that gating capacity is released at the write and load is paid in resolution, not in concurrent selection | Probe `Hippocampus`'s contents (occupancy, retrieval fidelity at a supplied address) and score task accuracy on the same runs. Sweep the lead time (`O11`) with everything else fixed, and sweep the **retention interval** with the lead time fixed. Cross **write rate** against **items held** under a concurrent action-selection load. Load the store past `N_slots` and plot two curves on the same runs: retrieval fidelity for **written** items, and recall of an **unwritten** member of the same parametric family. Run the same probe with the **write path and the read path ablated separately, at each phase** — the store's input link and the store are different objects and give opposite phase signatures, which §4.1's third-organ writability makes separately manipulable (`D170`) | The two curves **dissociate**: contents probing clean while accuracy falls localises the failure to the scheduler. Dual-task arm: **action-selection interference tracks write rate and not load, retrieval precision tracks load and not write rate, and the crossed cells are empty** — a store whose controller stays occupied for the retention interval fills them (`D200`). Delay-length arm: **retrieval fidelity is flat in the retention interval** — a fidelity that falls with delay is a decaying trace and `D167` says BIRM has none (`D217`). Overload arm (`D226`): the two overload curves **do not converge** — written-item fidelity degrades in resolution while unwritten-member recall stays at whatever slot the address lands on. Convergence is the class-learning effect, and it would falsify `D68`'s scaffold regime. And the sweep must move the score materially — `D93` predicts it is the largest single lever, on the order of the measured `15.5 → 54.2` in the spatial analogue | The measured lever is *spatial* block sampling in a static image; BIRM's is a temporal schedule and no source shows they are one knob (`D93`'s own killer) |
| **T4** `F-PFC1` | `PFC`'s factorization: one common component + updating + shifting, and **no inhibition-specific part** — **and no content-preferring partition** (`D198`) | Train a population of BIRM instances **at matched demand distributions** — every level's `gain_j` non-zero over the same schedule, since a factor structure fitted across instances whose live levels differ is partly the distribution's (`D199`); fit a bifactor model over their control-side measures. Score a content-domain partition over `PFC`'s units in the same fit. Run architecture (a) bias-only and architecture (b) bias + dedicated suppression output on the same backbone (`O13`) | (a) yields **no** inhibition-specific factor; (b) yields one. A result where both yield one falsifies the bias-only commitment (`D25`). And **no content-domain factor in either**: one appearing is evidence of a second content afferent §4 does not authorise, so it falsifies §4's fan-in and not `PFC` (`D198`). And the typed erase contributes **no** between-instance variance: an erase issue rate loading on the common factor makes the spoke a second control parameter (`D219`) | `F8`: an invariant factor structure is also produced by systems with no controller at all. Mitigated by the resource-stress arm of `I2` |
| **T5** `F-PFC2` | That `PFC` carries a task model rather than a policy | Lesion `PFC` mid-episode and read the action stream | Actions become **context-inappropriate but confidently executed** — not chance, not uniform. A collapse to chance says `PFC` was the action path, not a bias into it. **Contents-intact arm** (`D224`): under the same lesion every content store probes intact — `Cortex`'s associative weights, `Hippocampus`'s slots, the effect-equivalence codebook — since what `PFC` holds is a bias and never content; stored content degrading with it means `PFC` acquired a content store §4 does not authorise | A substrate whose competitive dynamics collapse to a default winner under any lesion, which mimics the pass. **And a silencing of `Bg` into `PFC`** (`T10`), which produces the identical signature — `T5` must be run with `Bg` intact or it cannot attribute what it sees (`D145`) |
| **T6** `F-Thal` | That `g_rel` and `g_acq` are two gains and not one — **plus the latency-crossing arm** (`D179`): per-item solve latency against item difficulty, on a family whose items are difficulty-ranked — **plus the decode-versus-perturb arm** (`D189`): that `Thalamus` routes and does not constitute — **plus the amplitude-crossing arm** (`D202`): that per-organ timescales are intrinsic below the commit threshold and system-wide above it | 2 × 2 sweep of the two gains, scoring decision **speed** and decision **accuracy** separately. Crossing arm: plot latency against difficulty for high- and low-scoring configurations. Decode/perturb arm: at the same site, (a) train a decoder on `Thalamus`'s inputs for the committed payload and (b) perturb `Thalamus`'s gain outputs, scoring what the step publishes. Amplitude arm: sweep the pre-commit resting distance set by `U` and fit each organ's across-trial autocorrelation over a fixed epoch — two parameters, no task — on both sides of the threshold | `g_rel` predicts speed only, `g_acq` accuracy only, and **the crossed cells are empty**. Any single-gain implementation fills them. Crossing arm: the higher-scoring configuration is **slower on hard items and faster on easy ones**, the two curves crossing rather than one dominating; a fixed inference budget cannot produce a crossing. Decode/perturb arm: **(a) succeeds and (b) changes timing, mode and `scope` while leaving the published content intact** — high decodability with near-zero content-perturbability, from wiring rather than from coding geometry. If perturbing `Thalamus` edits the payload, `D47` and (i) are both false | `O18`: if the ignition window is narrow enough, the two are one parameter by construction and the empty cells are unreachable rather than falsified. `T21` is the test that decides this. Decode/perturb arm: a perturbation whose spatial kernel cannot miss the `Bx⁺` line it sits on, which would make the null a resolution artefact rather than a result. Amplitude arm: an organ whose declared constant already equals the commit dwell, where the two regimes are not separable |
| **T7** `F-NM` | That per-component set-points beat one global gain | A task **pair** whose per-component optima differ (one demanding a high learning rate and a low exploration temperature, one the reverse), run with (a) per-organ decoders of `U` and (b) any single shared decode | (a) wins both members; (b) can win at most one. A tie means `D37` bought nothing and `Neuromodulators` collapses to a scalar | `O22`: nothing guarantees the six control laws converge. A win on the pair is compatible with oscillation on a third task |
| **T8** `F-NM2` | That `Neuromodulators`'s laws are laws and not hyperparameters | Run the closed loop for a full P1 and log each set-point against the second-order statistic that is supposed to drive it (`Var(δ)`, sign-oscillation of `δ`, `V`, `Var_a Q`) | Each set-point tracks its own statistic and no other. A set-point that tracks nothing is a hyperparameter, and §2.2 gains a row for it that round | An oscillating loop whose set-points correlate with their statistics *because* both are oscillating at the loop frequency |

---

### 10.2 Bus tests

Carried from §4's `Falsifier` column, now with protocols and inherited-failure columns. The bus that had none — `Act` — gets one here.

| # | Bus | Protocol | Pass condition | Does **not** rule out |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **T9** | **O** | Perturb `O` with a signal correlated with content but not with structure | `ρ`'s distribution (`D64`) is unchanged | A perturbation the adapter's discretisation absorbs before BIRM sees it (`H3`) |
| **T10** | **Bg** | Silence `Bg` into one reader; separately silence `Bx` into the same reader | Content responses intact and context modulation gone in the first case; **the crossed cell empty** in the second. **Single-orientation arm** (`D221`): estimate the frame orientation of `g` separately at each `Bg` reader — every reader returns **one** orientation, since there is one carrier (`D63`, `D66`); two dissociating orientations mean a second integrator exists | A reader that reconstructs context from content statistics over a few steps |
| **T11** | **Bx** | The `D50` ablation: drive `Bx⁻` from `O` directly | The write signal `Bx⁺ − Bx⁻` collapses to zero | Nothing — this one is a wiring check and passes or fails outright |
| **T12** | **U** | `T7`'s task pair, read on the bus rather than on the organ | Per-organ decoders win; no organ reads `m_t` raw | A vector `m_t` whose components are so correlated that a shared decode approximates all of them |
| **T13** | **V** | Two ablations, crossed (`D146`): (a) ablate `L_part`, hold everything else; (b) mask `V` off `PFC`'s input, `L_part` intact | (a) per-organ credit degrades to **co-activity** credit, and the failure is visible as spurious potentiation rather than as a rate change; (b) plasticity is unaffected and `PFC`'s task model degrades — the two roles of one scalar removable one at a time, which is `G57`'s open requirement | A family in which co-activity and participation nearly coincide, where `G19` has nothing to bite on |
| **T14** | **L** | Ablate the participation gate: set `δ_j = δ` for all `j` | This is the **single most damaging** manipulation in §10. Any manipulation that costs more means `L` is not doing the job claimed. **Operation-code arm** (`D223`): decode the erase/read operation type from every organ's state — only `PFC`, and the post-erase state of `Hippocampus`, may carry it; a third organ carrying it is an `L_cmd` reader §4 does not authorise | — |
| **T15** | **W** | Train a decoder over the readers, and a second over the readers **plus** `W` | The second does **not** beat the first. If `W` adds decodable information, `D47` is false and the commit is transforming its payload | A transform that is information-preserving and still changes downstream use |
| **T16** | **Act** *(new)* | Hold the family and permute the adapter's index→primitive map between episodes without telling BIRM | Re-binding completes within the budget §7 sets (~8 trials/cue, `D78`'s comparator), and the **structural code survives the permutation** — `Cortex` is not retrained, only `f_g` re-fitted (`D102`) | A `k` small enough that random re-binding meets the budget by luck. Barred by `S5`'s `k ≥ 3` and by reporting the random-policy floor |

---

### 10.3 Claim-class tests

| # | Claim | Protocol | Pass condition | Does **not** rule out |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **T17** | `D5` — **the `O(E)→O(V)` gap**, the operational definition of "it has the meta-graph" (mandatory row (d): a learning *trajectory*, not an endpoint) | Use the benchmark's own **directed state graph** per level — nodes hash-identified so trajectories merge, edges valid actions, terminals marked. Plot skill against **nodes visited**, not actions taken, and against the graph **geodesic** for the same level | The curve against nodes visited is materially steeper than the curve against edges taken, and the gap **does not appear** in the matched-parameter flat baseline. The reference point is the wiki's own: 18 steps inferring 42 links | A family whose graph is dense enough that `V` and `E` scale together. And per `T24`, the whole curve must survive the evoker control |
| **T18** | **The exploration/execution decomposition**, which the benchmark computes for humans and for nobody else | Subtract, per level: optimal playthrough (known-mechanics lower bound) from realised trajectory, exactly as the human calibration does with optimal-vs-best-first-run | The score splits into *spent learning* and *spent executing badly*, and the two are reported separately. A single RHAE number sums two failures with different fixes | Nothing about *which* of exploration, modelling, goal-setting or planning broke — the benchmark's four components are authored intuitions, not measured factors |
| **T19** | **`S5`'s control** — that a reasoning score is not a binding-speed score (mandatory row (b)) | The arbitrary sensorimotor mapping task at `k ≥ 3`, every evaluation round. Two-alternative forced choice is forbidden: at `k = 2` a Repeat-Stay/Change-Shift heuristic is complete | The family score **exceeds** the control score. Not exceeding it means the run measured binding speed | A family whose structure is itself learnable at binding speed, where the two are not separable |
| **T20** | **The congruent/incongruent partition** — the strong baseline built into the *trial set* rather than run as a second model | Partition levels by whether a marginal statistic suffices: a frame-change-predicting policy of the published informed-search kind, and the level's exact random-policy `P_win` from its state graph (acceptance threshold: no level solvable by random play more than **1 in 10,000**). Report the two subsets separately, **never their mean** | Above the null on *both* subsets. The measured hazard is that an aggregate is carried entirely by the congruent half: 0.68 congruent against **0.53 (chance)** incongruent under an overall 0.64 | Its precondition: the partition needs the intended structure known in advance, which is exactly what identifiability denies in general (`G16`). Available here only because the benchmark author holds the graph |
| **T21** | `D107`/`D108` — **the `γ` leak test** | Sweep `γ` over `[0.90, 1.0]` on a **single-probe** family and on an **`n`-chained-probe** family | Insensitivity on the first; on the second, a fall beginning exactly where achievable rollout depth drops below `n`. The published signature is `+7.8 pp` at `γ = 1.0`, `+1.0` at `0.95`, `−1.6` at `0.90` on the multi-observation environment and flat on the single-observation one | A BIRM showing the transition on the *single*-probe family has wired its estimation discount into its rollout scorer — that is the failure this row exists to catch, and there is no second reading of it |
| **T22** | `D97` — **the freeze-the-policy audit** | Hold BIRM's policy fixed and measure §8's epistemic term | It **cannot** be driven to zero. If it can, the term is a self-consistency penalty and the implementation is **discarded, not tuned** | The confound the source concedes: all five measured estimators are horizon-1, which is independently where the objective degenerates to myopic information gain. `H ≥ 2` in probes (`D108`) is the standing repair |
| **T23** | `D101` — **the primitive-only control run** | Each evaluation round, a run with composed edges disabled | Any persistent return gap is read as **a region of the graph BIRM's own composed edges closed off** — a changed fixed point, not a slowdown. Costs one extra run | Nothing in the hierarchy literature reports this diagnostic, so there is no external calibration for how large a gap is tolerable |
| **T24** `E1` | **The evoker control** — binding on every learning-curve claim, `T17` included | Re-run the curve with a single unambiguous context-signalling trial inserted | No jump larger and more persistent than a plausible one-trial update. Such a jump is *apparent* learning — the stored states did not change, only the weights over them | Savings, anterograde interference and the consistency effect are all reproduced with learning rates and stored states essentially unchanged; a curve cannot separate a learner from a retriever, and this control only bounds the confusion |
| **T25** `E2` | **The paired-frame null** — binding on every negative result (mandatory row (e)) | No capability absence is recorded until it is re-run under at least one frame in which deploying the capability is advantageous under BIRM's own objective | Both frames reported. A null in one frame only is not a null | The two positions are separable only by a rule nobody has stated (`T291`): re-framing is legitimate when it changes what the agent is *motivated to do*, illegitimate when it changes what the task *asks*, and no benchmark documents which a variation is |
| **T26** | `D90` — **both excitation margins with every learning-curve claim** | Compute `γ_rep` (the environment's, unraisable by more data) and `ρ_tr = λ_min(E_g[Cov(a\|g)])` (the collector's, a design choice) before training; report both | Both quoted alongside the number. A world-model claim without `ρ_tr` is uninterpretable in the same way a score without its §2.2 denominator is | A negative margin does **not** license "this cannot be learned": identification error is measured to change smoothly through `γ_rep = 0`, so a margin is a certificate and not a phase boundary |
| **T27** | `D18`/`D72` — **anti-collapse**, and it must not be scored by decoding accuracy | Two monitors: CCGP/PS over the condition grid where one exists (`O9`), and **split-recombine** — factorize iff effect-prediction loss is unchanged when `b` is split and the parts recombined — where one does not | Both flat or rising through training. Decoding accuracy is **not** admissible: the measured case has decoding flat while CCGP rises | Split-recombine is a *sufficiency* check on separability and says nothing about whether the parts are the right ones. And the field's principal compositionality instrument is maximally fooled by collapse — an all-zero model ties every item and scores 100% where ties resolve to element 0 |
| **T28** | `D82` — **the structural instrument that needs no data** | Attractor–eigenvector alignment and convergence time, against a **degree-preserving permutation** of the same weights | The permutation null is beaten. Cheap enough to run every checkpoint regardless of what else is scheduled | A statistic flat across instances that differ in transfer — a null nothing ever fails is not an instrument |
| **T29** | `D123` — **plan–trajectory alignment**, the substitute for the rule channel §1 excludes | At each commit, log the rollout `W` published and the trajectory actually realised. Score (i) alignment — did the realised path follow the committed rollout — and (ii) classification of the committed rollout against the level's state graph: **intended** (a geodesic or near-geodesic to a terminal), **unintended-but-successful** (reaches the terminal by a route the level was not built around), **wrong** | Alignment high enough that the rollout is a usable proxy for the executed computation — the language-modality analogue reports **93–98%** and is admissible on that basis. Then the two-way distortion is readable: high unintended-successful share means the score **overstates** structure; intended-rollout-with-failed-execution means it **understates** it | The classification has no algorithmic form in its source and BIRM's version substitutes the state graph for a human rater — which is only available where the graph is enumerable, and returns nothing where expansion hit its budget |
| **T30** | `D115` — **the unseen-class arm.** BIRM's own configuration is a harness by the benchmark's definition | Any setting fitted while looking at public environments is re-run on an environment class never seen | The score does not collapse to the no-harness baseline on the unseen class. The measured failure is total: a harness taking a seen class from **0.0% → 97.1%** moves an unseen environment **0.0% → 0.0%** | A §10 pass on a family the spec was written against is *evidence about the spec's author*, not about BIRM. `O39` |
| **T31** | `D116` — **profile parity, not aggregate parity** | Correlate BIRM's per-level (and per-item-type, where the family supplies one) accuracy profile with the human profile, rather than comparing aggregates | The profile correlates. The dissociation this exists to catch is measured: a system **at** the human aggregate (`.90` vs `.91`) correlating **0.06**, and below chance on the one item type authored to catch its heuristic, against `0.55` for a system doing the intended computation | `O40` — ARC-AGI-3 publishes no per-item human profile, only the RHAE denominator, so this row runs at level granularity until a profile exists |
| **T32** | `D118` — **the emergence audit**, run on BIRM's own `g` | The six items, in order: (1) fraction of runs over architectures, activations, optimizers, seeds — not the tuned configuration; (2) compute the **second-order statistics of the training target** and ask whether the structure is already implied by them; (3) does it survive a heterogeneous, biologically ordinary read-out; (4) sweep every suspected hyperparameter and see whether the "predicted" value follows it; (5) check population-level **relational** invariants, not per-unit tuning; (6) compare against **filtered noise**, not against zero | Every item reported, pass or fail, with the conditions under which the structure does and does not appear. **A failure here does not invalidate BIRM** — it converts a §6 row from an emergent property to an installed one, which is where §11 already puts it | Nothing quantifies how much came from the target versus the task (`G44`); the audit is a checklist, not a measure. And item 5 is the only level at which a match is legitimate at all: population geometry is a valid target, per-unit tuning is not |
| **T33** | `D119` — **the stop-gradient disclosure** | State, for every trained component, which parameters the loss is differentiated with respect to and whether the remaining update is the gradient of anything. BIRM has at least two such places: the codes frozen during transport (`D23`) and `Cortex`'s isotropic-Gaussian marginal enforced as a **soft penalty over random 1-D projections** (`D86`, `D87`) | The disclosure exists, and **no minimiser-based guarantee is quoted for a component whose update is not a gradient**. `D90`'s recovery margins are statements about a global minimiser and apply only through an assumption a stop-gradient lineage cannot make | The alternative is not free: a loss with a stated non-collapsed minimum certifies the *target*, not the trajectory, and collapses across most of its coefficient space anyway. Being auditable is worth about one point of performance and is not additive with the stop-gradient design |
| **T34** | **The trajectory lever** — the only thing that breaks the many-to-one degeneracy | Where a reference system's learning trajectory exists, match the **order** of representational change, not the endpoint | The order is reproduced. Endpoints discriminate nothing: three models with different learning rules reach the same orthogonalised endpoint and only one reproduces the order | The price is charged immediately and lands on `H3`: in the one worked case, reordering two symbols in the modeller's own input encoding leaves the learned graph identical and **inverts** the trajectory. A trajectory match is partly a property of the adapter |
| **T35** | `D194` — **the read-out ablation**: whether the payoff is §5.2's rollout or the predictive state §5.1 already carries | Clamp the mode bit and run one trained BIRM on one family under two read-outs: (a) §5.2 enabled, (b) `Thalamus` held in input-driven mode so `PFC` selects from **W**, **Bx**, **V**, **U** with no rollout. Model, data, controller capacity and experience held fixed; both arms reported on a *seen* family and on a held-out instance-graph of it, budget **swept** per S1 | The rollout arm exceeds the reactive arm **only** on the held-out instance-graph, and the gap scales with the chained-commit depth actually used. Equal arms on the re-goaled family refute §5.2's `H ≥ 2` floor | That the gap is **search** rather than features. Both arms read the same `⟨g_t, Bx⁻⟩`, and the rollout arm also writes to `Hippocampus` under `PFC`'s licence, so a gap bought by those writes is not excluded — `D173`'s per-slot provenance bit is what separates them |

---

### 10.4 The reporting block — mandatory with every score

A §10 number is void without all seven fields. `D121`, `D122`

| Field | Content | Why |
|---|---|---|
| **Cost** | Compute and wall-clock per task | `S1`. And it is load-bearing here specifically: RHAE counts only environment-changing actions, so a system with unbounded per-action deliberation is maximally efficient by the metric (`G17`) |
| **§2.2 denominator** | The full hardwiring ledger, `H3` and `H9` included | `S4`. A score without its priors enumerated is uninterpretable |
| **`⟨D_o, k, Ŝ⟩`** | The instantiation triple | `D85` — every organ dimension is a function of it, so a score is a property of the triple |
| **Both margins** | `γ_rep`, `ρ_tr` | `T26` |
| **Facet vector** | Not a scalar: `(family score, control-task score, congruent/incongruent pair, exploration/execution pair, plan-alignment rate, unintended-successful share)` | An honest instrument returns a vector. Reached independently from two sides: `0.92` i.i.d. accuracy coexisting with `0.72` systematicity, `0.50` productivity, `0.54` localism; and a concept-variation score that **rises** on one concept while falling 11 points on another |
| **`n` and `1/n`** *(new)* | The item/level/environment count, and the resolution it implies. **Comparisons below `1/n` are refused** | `F11`: the field routinely quotes a 15-item instrument to a tenth of a point and compares systems 2–3 points apart on it, at ≈3.7× the standard error of a 200-item benchmark. The fix is arithmetic and nobody applies it |
| **Probe condition** *(new)* | The **demand and drive** under which any probe, null or timescale on the line was taken: which levels' `gain_j` were non-zero, and what was driving the network | `D199`, `D202`. A null probe run under a demand that never made the probed decision live measures the evaluation distribution and not BIRM — the control the reversible-participation result shows is missing everywhere. And an integration timescale read under one drive is a different number under another, so a timescale quoted without its drive does not transfer, including to BIRM's own next round |
| **Stock reads** *(new)* | How many times each evaluation set has been read, and by whom | `S3` plus `F1`: developer-blindness is a **depleting stock**, not a property — ~10,000 reported scores absorbed against 100 private tasks. The counter is the only thing that makes the depletion visible |

---

### 10.5 What §10 cannot do

Stated here so no row above is read as more than it is. Each of these is a failure mode of the *inventory*, not of a choice made in the spec.

| # | Limit | Consequence for BIRM's claims |
|---|---|---|
| L1 | **`F1`/`F2` — developer-blindness is a depleting stock, and item-level blindness does not imply distributional blindness.** The two clauses conflict with difficulty calibration and are jointly unfixable | Every §10 score is a joint measurement of BIRM **and of how many times its author has read the family**. The stock counter (`10.4`) makes this visible and does not fix it |
| L2 | **`F5` — the framing axis.** An instrument that supplies the same problem representation to every task measures search *within* that representation | This is `H3` arriving from the instrument side. BIRM is scored on discovery over a vertex set the adapter fixed, and `O2` remains the number nobody has |
| L3 | **`F14` — the detector's null and its worst case share a signature.** Every train/test differencing statistic reads the same for a clean system and one that ingested both splits | BIRM's repurposed-skill family (§1 `D3`) is generated by its author, so this bites on the *primary* benchmark only, where it is bounded by the private-set discipline and not removed |
| L4 | **`O28` — `ρ`'s circularity.** The structural code is audited by the content channel, so a content channel wrong in a way correlated with the structure produces a clean `ρ` | Every row resting on `ρ` (`T9`, `T10`) must quote this. The only escape is an external certificate of state identity, which §1's task set withholds **by construction** |
| L5 | **`F7` — a benchmark hard enough to floor every solver destroys the ordering it exists to produce.** The diagnostic regime is humans-at-ceiling; all reported scores on the primary benchmark are below 1.5% and its metric's discriminating range has never been exercised | Below ~1.5% BIRM's score is not comparable to anything, including itself across rounds. `T20`'s partition and `T18`'s decomposition are the rows that still discriminate down there, which is why they are mandatory rather than optional |
| L6 | **`C5` — the facet vector has no agreed basis.** Five facets, four regimes, a concept inventory and eight perturbation axes exist and nothing relates them | Two vector reports are not comparable, including two of BIRM's own if the family changes. `O38` |
| L7 | **`C4` — nothing certifies that several demonstrated abilities come from *one* model.** `I3` and `I4` are both gameable by multi-task training on the query set | `T2` and `T29` can be passed by a BIRM whose organs each solved their own arm. §10 has no row that closes this and the spec does not claim one |
| L8 | **Morgan's Canon.** *Never attribute to high-level abilities that which can be adequately explained by shortcut learning* — and the intended solution is **not a function of the training data**, so it enters only through inductive bias | Which makes §2.2 and §11, not §10, the place BIRM's claim actually rests. §10 can refute; it cannot license |
| L9 | **The deployment confound.** BIRM has a state in which it holds a capability and does not run it — `D70`'s standing disposition and `D30`'s engagement rule — and **no row above reads that state** | A negative score cannot separate a *missing* operator from an *undeployed* one, which is exactly the confound the comparative literature names when every test for a missing operator was administered in one motivational frame (`T291`). Every negative §10 result must be re-run with `Neuromodulators`'s disposition gate ablated before it is read as a missing part. `O26`, `D191` |

| L10 | **BIRM produces no best-of-`k` curve.** Its rejector is per-step commit failure — abstaining, not selecting (`D136`, `D157`) — so nothing enumerates `k` completed traces and ranks them, and `D92` denies the distillation path that would convert search into a better single pass (`D201`) | The field's whole selection-versus-reasoning axis is measured as `pass@1` against `pass@k`, and BIRM has no `pass@k`. Its scores are not comparable to the sampled-inference literature on that axis in either direction, and §10 may not borrow that literature's numbers as calibration. What the denial forfeits is unmeasured: §12 `O64` |

**And the standing conclusion the inventory supports, which §10 adopts rather than argues with:** certification may be a **maintenance schedule rather than a protocol** — benchmark authorship run as a loop against the field, whose stopping criterion is *"authoring tasks easy for humans and hard for AI becomes impossible"*. If that is right, §10 is a standing expense and not a capital one, and no version of it is ever finished. `D125`
---

| D39 | The output-port factorization is **not visible in unit activity** — any probe of `D28` must be geometric | §3.3, §10 | `MEASURED` | `[[wiki/entities/medial-prefrontal-cortex.md]]` (Lai et al. 2026: no rate difference under any condition; the two channels diverge only in population geometry, sharing a common reference state) | `G52` | — | Nothing — this is a measurement constraint. It kills any §10 row that tries to verify a typed channel by reading a unit |
| D40 | **U is a vector `m_t` with a per-organ decoder** `f_k(W_k·m_t)` — sender-global, receiver-addressed. Not four global scalars and not `Neuromodulators` independent channels | §4, §3.5 | `SOURCED` | `[[wiki/concepts/neuromodulatory-metaparameters.md]]` (Mei et al. 2025: co-release, projection-specific effects, within-region heterogeneity, two transmission modes — the count of control channels is not the count of chemicals); `[[wiki/concepts/transthalamic-context-routing.md]]` (the middle option has a substrate) | `G93`, `T281` | — | A single shared decode matching per-organ decoders on a task pair whose optima differ (`F-U`). Note the predicted cost: with decoders learned rather than assigned, *which* channel does what stops being readable off the wiring |
| D41 | `U` is **second-order — strictly slower than the activity it gates**, and the order of the dynamics is derived rather than chosen | §4 | `SOURCED` | `[[wiki/concepts/precision-weighting.md]]` (states minimise `F`; parameters and precisions minimise its path integral `A = ∫F dt`, hence slower dynamics + trace variables) | `T119` | — | A gain adapting at activity timescale outperforming the separated one — which would mean the timescale split is a biological accident rather than an objective's consequence. `T119` also disputes the frame: if no variance parameter dominates, the precision ratio is a **design knob**, not an approximation error |
| D42 | `Neuromodulators`'s six **control laws** compute `γ, β, α` from `Var(δ)`, sign-oscillation of `δ`, `V` and `Var_a Q` — feedback control on the learner's own statistics, not an outer loop | §3.5 | `SOURCED` | `[[wiki/concepts/neuromodulatory-metaparameters.md]]` (Doya 2002, Fig. 9) | `G61`, `G50` | — | The four-dimensional loop failing to converge, or oscillating on the three mutually-inhibitory couplings — the laws have **no shared objective**, which is the acknowledged price. `O22` |

| D189 | **`T269` is two objects in BIRM and the answer differs between them: the bus constitutes, the arbitrator routes — and BIRM predicts the dissociation from wiring rather than from coding geometry.** The row disputes whether prefrontal cortex constitutes conscious content (A: percept decodable at up to 95% from 987 ventrolateral units on internally generated transitions with no report, cross-condition transfers separately falsifying an input code, an epoch code and an eye-movement code) or only routes it (B: focal stimulation across 1537 sites and 67 patients giving the **lowest** rate of reportable content change in the brain — 0% at the frontal pole against ~67% in unimodal cortex — plus lobectomy nulls and a posterior lesion carrying 214-fold vegetative-state risk). It says the opposite of the arbitrator: `Thalamus` is denied content outright, reads `Bx⁺` as **aggregate activity only**, and emits gains, mode and `scope` (§3.4, §4). So the row's sharpened form — the payload is present at 95% in the tissue whose perturbation changes content 0% of the time — is here a **structural** prediction rather than an anomaly: decoding at the arbitrator reads a full payload off the bus passing through it, while perturbing the arbitrator moves only what `Thalamus` owns. That is B's third survivor (the two results never met) given as wiring instead of anatomy, and it competes with B's own proposed mechanism, a stimulator kernel too coarse for a clustered code, which stays the confound. | D191 | **`T289`'s three positions land on three different objects in BIRM, and only one of them is a knob here: there is no new-part option, no fan-in axis, and exactly the motivational gate the third position needs.** The row disputes whether the human-specific capacity required **one new operator** (A: narrow syntax — recursion plus its two interface mappings — uniquely human, small, discrete, plausibly exapted; | D192 | **`T183` has no other-agent instance in BIRM, and on the axis that does transfer — a query nested inside a query — BIRM is the fixed-point pole by bus occupancy: depth is chaining, never nesting.** The row disputes whether theory of mind is **recursive inference** (A: `query` is definable inside the stochastic λ-calculus, so a planning query nests inside a goal-inference query with well-defined semantics and no separate mechanism is needed — being *definable* is the whole argument) or a **fixed point** (B: two coupled spacetime attractors, each taking the other's inferred plan as its reward input, relax to a mutual best response with no explicit posterior anywhere; and the one *measured* inverse-planning model scores 96.0–99.7% on the Baby Intuitions Benchmark at recursion depth **one**, its tractability coming precisely from refusing nesting). §1's task set is single-agent — an environment family, not a partner — so no organ models another agent and the dispute as posed is not instantiated. | D196 | **`T88`'s two registers are already separate in BIRM and separate *by wire*; BIRM takes the pointer pole's register while denying its semantics, because its pointer is a position, not a want.** The row disputes what prefrontal delay activity is about — **the item held** (A: sustained spatially tuned firing after cue offset is the classic memory trace, inactivation produces localized mnemonic scotomas, and antisaccade recordings find more than twice as many cells coding the *stimulus* location as the movement target) or **the place attention is pointed** (B: with attended and remembered locations dissociated inside one trial, 61% of spatially tuned dorsolateral cells code the attended location against 16% the remembered one, `I_Att` = 1.84 vs `I_Rem` = 1.21, tuning to the attended location is *stronger late* in the delay with sensory input identical in every coordinate frame, and both locations are equally reward-predictive until the trigger). | D197 | **`T51`'s fusion-or-parallel question is not a storage choice in BIRM: there is one carrier and `k` operators, and the row's real bite is that no organ holds an item identity at all.** The row disputes whether an item set embedded in several relational structures is held as **one conjunctive map** (two 1-D rank orders over 16 people, learned on separate days and never co-presented, giving hippocampal/entorhinal dissimilarity that scales with 2-D Euclidean distance in the product space better than with either order alone, plus a grid-like code over inferred vectors; ~90% of evidence-tuned dorsal-CA1 cells genuinely conjunctive for evidence × position rather than tuned to either alone; the whole result replicated at `n` = 203 in medial prefrontal cortex on an attack × defence map, `t₂₀₂` = 2.69) or as **parallel, anatomically separate maps** (link distance → bilateral entorhinal and semantic distance → bilateral hippocampus over the same 12 objects, in non-overlapping clusters, each effect statistically zero in the other's region of interest, `t₂₂` = −0.25 and 0.54, with the authors concluding the hippocampal formation "does not construct conjunctive maps"). | D198 | **`T96`'s content partition is refused in BIRM, and refused by the partition position's own mechanism: `PFC`'s content fan-in is one, so there is nothing to partition on.** The row disputes whether the controller is a **homogeneous pool** (prefrontal neurons adaptively code whatever the task requires; orbitofrontal and dorsolateral differences are balances of response strength rather than category-distinct modules; "no modular discretely localized forms") or **partitioned by content class**, with the partition predicted to follow **connectivity** to the posterior or subcortical region supplying the content (275 imaging studies showing consistent localization differences; social impairment after ventromedial damage against mechanistic impairment after dorsolateral; emotion-specific neurons in human ventral prefrontal cortex). | D199 | **`T111` is BIRM's position at the expression layer and its opposite at the weight layer, and the two are separable here only because engagement is a declared register rather than an inferred one — which turns the row into a binding §10 prohibition.** The row disputes whether a control representation is a **stable property of the tissue** that task conditions modulate (the reading every representational claim in the wiki relies on, and what licenses probing a trained model once and reporting what it contains) or **instantiated by the demand** (in monkey posterior medial prefrontal cortex the *number of neurons representing the task at all* falls significantly, `p < 0.01`, when retraining leaves only one response tactic valid, and returns to its original size when both are restored — same animals, same penetrations, actions still varying, accuracy 99–100%, no change in pre-SMA or SMA recorded in parallel; earlier studies found no representation in this territory because their tasks required one tactic, and a downstream area carries no cue-position signal in the mixed-tactic task though it is spatially tuned where no tactic choice exists). The biology cannot separate the two because participation is what it measures; BIRM can, because `gain_j` is declared. What the row buys is therefore not an architectural change but a **standing prohibition**, and it is the control the row says every interpretability sweep in the wiki lacks: no probe of `PFC` may be read as "level `j` does not represent `Y`" unless `gain_j` was non-zero while the probe ran. | D200 | **`T130` is answered by organ split: BIRM's gate releases at the write and its memoranda are maintained by nothing, which is position A past the limit the row says nobody has represented — and it makes the unrun dual-task simulation a crossed prediction here.** The row disputes whether the gating module in a gated working memory is a **write-enable that then drops out** — releasing a thalamo-cortical or a cortico-cortical reverberation, four of the five reviewed models, with an argument behind the cortico-cortical variant (thalamus would need as many dedicated cells as prefrontal cortex to carry maintenance) — or **the maintenance loop itself**, reverberation running through the direct pathway with the hyperdirect pathway supplying the reset, which is the most recent and most capable of the five and departs without arguing for the departure. The items-held cost is **relocated rather than absent**, and this is the part BIRM must own: `D68` degrades resolution and not identity, so load is paid in retrieval precision and never in concurrent selection. The crossed prediction is then stateable and testable in one design — action-selection interference tracks write rate and not load, retrieval precision tracks load and not write rate, crossed cells empty — added as an arm on `T3`. `PFC`'s sustained task model is BIRM's one recurrently maintained object and it is swapped rather than accumulated (`D168`), so it does not supply the items-held axis either | §3.2, §10.1 | `SOURCED` | `[[wiki/entities/basal-ganglia.md]]` (the three-way model census and what each option predicts about the controller after the write); `[[wiki/concepts/working-memory.md]]` (the five models and the missing separating datum); `[[wiki/entities/pbwm.md]]` (the gate-a-cortico-cortical-loop option BIRM's `L_cmd` is the analogue of) | `T130` | — | A `T3` dual-task run in which concurrent action selection degrades with the number of occupied slots at a fixed write rate — the gate then being consumed for the retention interval after all, and `D33`'s separation of write from maintenance being nominal |
| D201 | **`T180` is settled for BIRM before the evidence is weighed, because `D92` deletes the mechanism position B needs: the rejector is run-time architecture here and can never become a curriculum.** The row disputes whether verifier-driven test-time search buys **selection** (the generator's coverage is already there and the verifier picks: 74% greedy → 83% majority@64 → 93% best-of-1000; ~60% pass@1 → 88.9% pass@8192; the wiki's own theorizer 0.019–0.038 → ~0.70 at 1024 samples with a *complete* primitive set in hand, so pass@1 measures the policy and everything above it measures the selector) or **reasoning** (reinforcement learning from verifiable rewards distils the search back into the policy: 12% → 74% at greedy decoding, and self-correction elicited in an open-weights base model from reward alone with no supervised traces — the verifier being training-time scaffolding whose product is a better single forward pass). So `external-verification`'s ladder is an **architecture here and never a curriculum**, and every performance gain BIRM can buy comes from the vocabulary, the model or more search, never from compiling search into the policy. | D202 | **`T319` is dissolved by BIRM's commit threshold, which is the very variable the dispute names as discriminating and never sweeps: per-organ constants below it, one system constant above it, and `U` moves the crossing.** The row disputes whether an area's integration timescale is **intrinsic** (seven macaque areas ordered by the time constant of spontaneous fluctuations, `50–350 ms`, the ordering predicted by hierarchical position at `r_s = 0.97`, `P = 0.002`, consistent across six datasets at `r_s = 0.89`, mean firing rate excluded as a confound — the form every timescale bank in the wiki assumes) or **emergent** (the same network run under visual and somatosensory drive yields two different dynamical hierarchies, undriven visual areas expressing the slow distributed state rather than their own fast one; hierarchical position predicts the emergent constant at only `r² = 0.25 / 0.14 / 0.22`; scrambling long-range weights while preserving topology and the local gradient strongly attenuates the separation). The row's own status names the threshold that reconciles them — **perturbation amplitude**, with small fluctuations around baseline reflecting isolated-area properties and large or sustained input expressing whole-system constants — and says it has never been swept. BIRM has that threshold as an object rather than as a regime: below `Thalamus`'s commit threshold nothing publishes and every timescale is a declared per-organ constant (`Hippocampus` one-shot, `PFC` sustained, `Cortex` transport-only, `Neuromodulators` second-order under `D41`, `Bg` low-passed on the edge, `τ = 0` on every edge, `D177`); above it the published commit holds `W` exclusively for a dwell time (`D53`, `D56`), which belongs to no organ. | D210 | **`T267`'s untested third position is BIRM's architecture: one bus does both jobs and the two contents are separated by a declared field, not by a population.** The row disputes whether the midline hubs **broadcast** current content (anterior and posterior cingulate and precuneus listed alongside dorsolateral prefrontal and inferior parietal cortex as workspace nodes amplifying and globally distributing whatever is currently ignited, external or internal) or **generate** internal content (the same hubs assigned to remembering, prospecting, mentalizing and self-reference, and defined by their *deactivation* under externally directed tasks — the opposite of a bus that must serve external content). | D211 | **`T303`'s two poles land on two different BIRM objects, neither of which is the object the row names — and the variable that would separate them has a register here with no rule to read it.** The row disputes whether the striatum is an **action selector** (it reads cortical ensembles and releases one program by focused disinhibition of its thalamic target, opponent D₁/D₂ populations pushing a tonically inhibitory output in both directions — the reading every architecture in the wiki takes) or a **prediction-error stage below prefrontal cortex** (output to thalamus places it lower in the hierarchy, the densest corticostriatal projection originates in layer V — the deep-pyramidal source predictive coding assigns to descending predictions — spiny neurons then occupy the error slot with D₁/D₂ as its precision gain, and focused disinhibition is demoted to a consequence of winnerless competition on hidden causes). The error stage is denied by wiring: `PFC` is denied **O** at every level (`D31`) and emits bias and `L_cmd`, never a content prediction for a level below to cancel, and `V` carries no address at all (`D70`), so a residual here cannot be *about* a level.
