| D215 | **`T80` is split by channel and BIRM is a different pole on each: the carrier cannot be run backwards at all, and the store's reverse pass never runs a sequence.** The row disputes whether a population can traverse a stored sequence in reverse — **yes** (hippocampal place-cell sequences replay forward *and* reverse after experience, CA3's symmetric spike-timing kernel letting one weight matrix generate both directions, with the forward:reverse mixture used as the knob that sets whether the consolidated map stores the policy or the geometry) or **no** (macaques driving a cursor from ~90 M1 units fail, across sessions and under a corridor constraint that makes it the only rewarded solution, to emit an existing neural trajectory time-reversed; the two cursor directions turn out to use *distinct* trajectories rather than one path traversed both ways). Online BIRM is the second pole, and not on the evidence: `g_t = ℓ₂(W_{a_t} g_{t−1})` composes under a **monoid, not a group** (`D63`), so no inverse operator exists and no action index could name one — `f_g` computes `W_a` from a one-hot code of width `k` and the repertoire is the adapter's (`H2`). Reverse traversal of the carrier is **unrepresentable rather than refused**, and B's finding that two directions use two trajectories is the same statement as two operators. Offline BIRM takes the first pole's payoff without its mechanism: §5.3 step 5 composes two stored transitions sharing an endpoint, and `D59` requires the composition to **run reverse to the direction of use** — but what runs is a uniform-coverage draw from `Hippocampus` (`D57`) updating the *earlier* item from the later one, never a sequence generator played backwards. Backward credit is therefore bought at the store and never at the integrator, which is the row's own "reverse traversal needs its own machinery" with `D59` naming the machinery. The forfeit is the knob: the sampler is uniform and the priority tag is deliberately stale (`D57`, `D60`), so the forward:reverse **mixture** is not a controlled variable here and the policy-versus-geometry trade the wiki reads off it can be neither set nor swept. §12 `O68` | §6 `D63`, §5.3 `D59` | `SOURCED` | `[[wiki/concepts/offline-replay.md]]` (bidirectional replay, the symmetric kernel, and the mixture-as-knob); `raw/talk-nd-brain-learning-limits.txt` **(tentative — talk only)** (the failed volitional reversal and the two distinct trajectories) | `T80` | — | A family in which backward credit is demanded *inside* a step, where `D59`'s offline pass cannot reach — the monoid would then be a cost rather than a free denial, and `g` would need an inverse operator `f_g` cannot emit |
| D216 | **`T82`'s two poles are two separate registers in BIRM, composed in exactly the order the row's own Status proposes: a per-relation ration inside a per-period cap.** The row disputes whether permanent fast-store dependence is an optimal **refusal** (consolidation regulated by predictability, so for a relation the slow learner cannot model the optimal transfer is zero and the absence of cortical involvement is the correct output of a working system) or a **transport failure** (the same observable — persistent hippocampal activation at delayed retrieval plus weak hippocampal–prefrontal coupling — produced by a degraded channel: in older adults it tracks reduced prefrontal slow-wave activity, itself mediated by medial prefrontal grey-matter atrophy, with hippocampal volume and every non-slow-wave sleep variable predicting nothing). BIRM installs both, as two objects that are not interchangeable. `D58`(i) is the refusal: transport for a relation stops when its probe error stops falling and is **zero** for a relation `Cortex` cannot model (`D24`) — A's stopping rule as a terminal state rather than a delay. `D58`(ii) is the channel: a per-rest-period **event cap**, taken because replaying every buffered item runs the copying channel orders of magnitude harder than the system being copied, which is the over-transport regime where generalization falls below chance. B's mechanism is therefore present as a **declared ceiling on the budget A rations** — the composition the row offers as a possibility, here as wiring rather than as a hypothesis. Two consequences. (i) The row's design question — what a system should do on observing incomplete transfer — is answered **by register** and not by inference: transfer stopped at `D58`(i) is correct and must not be repaired, transfer starved by `D58`(ii) is a budget and may be. (ii) BIRM has a third outcome neither pole names, and it is `D166`'s ordering price: a reliably recurring but *unmodellable* relation passes `D57`'s recall gate at full strength and is refused only after a probe cycle has failed to fall, so incomplete transfer here can also be **latency**. The confound the row calls unresolvable in the literature is separable here only because both gates are declared constants rather than inferred tissue, and the row's own manipulation — vary material predictability while stimulating the channel — is `O53`'s unrun sweep with its two axes named | §5.3 `D58`, §3.1 `D24` | `SOURCED` | `[[wiki/concepts/generalization-optimized-consolidation.md]]` (predictability-regulated transfer and the zero-transfer optimum); `[[wiki/concepts/complementary-learning-systems.md]]` (the slow-wave channel, its atrophy mediation, and the null on every other sleep variable) | `T82`, `T81` | — | A relation whose probe error stops falling and which a later, larger `Cortex` then models — a false zero would make `D24`'s terminal state a capacity artefact, and the refusal would be indistinguishable from the failure after all |
| D217 | **`T86` is answered by object rather than by mechanism: BIRM's memoranda are position B taken past short-term plasticity — no spiking, no decay, and no open read-out path — while the one thing held by recurrence is a task model and not a memorandum.** The row disputes whether a working memory is held in **persistent spiking** (sustained delay activity, one attractor per item, distractors survived by basin recapture — what every attractor account in the wiki assumes) or in **synaptic state that spiking only reads** (sample identity at chance in the macaque prefrontal population ~1 s into a 4 s delay while the animal performs at ~99%, with activity returned not to baseline but to a state carrying no sample information; trained short-term-plasticity networks reproducing exactly that profile — spike decoding falls, synaptic-weight decoding holds for the whole delay — and more brain-like than fixed-synapse networks across the whole sweep). BIRM's memoranda are the second pole in a stronger form: a one-shot write into a prestructured scaffold with **no recurrence holding it** (`D68`, `D200`), **no decay of any kind** (`D167`), and content that reaches `Bx` only on a `PFC`-scheduled read (`D76`, `O11`). The delay-period profile B measures is therefore what BIRM's buses show by construction — nothing on `Bx` between commits, and the sustained signal is the **address** on `Bg`, written every step (`D196`) — and the item is invisible until probed because the read-out path is closed, not because the trace has weakened. Position A survives on exactly one object: `PFC`'s task model, held by recurrence and *swapped rather than accumulated* (`D200`), which is a task set and supplies no items-held axis. Two things follow. (i) The failure mode to design against is neither basin escape nor `τ_f` decay but a **mis-scheduled read on intact contents** (`D167`) and address collision (`D185`, `O60`). (ii) BIRM is separable from the synaptic account by a **delay-length sweep**: a synaptic trace degrades with the retention interval and a slot does not, so retrieval fidelity flat in delay is BIRM's prediction and the short-term-plasticity network's falsifier — added as an arm on `T3`. The row's task confound does not transfer: withholding a known motor response is not a maintenance operation here, since there is no suppression channel on the action path (`D25`, `D26`), so BIRM's only maintenance is "hold information for later computation" | §3.2 `D200`, §3.3, §10 `T3` | `SOURCED` | `[[wiki/entities/stsp-working-memory-rnn.md]]` (the decoding dissociation and the hyperparameter sweep); `[[wiki/entities/stp-flickering-cann.md]]` (the hippocampal existence proof); `[[wiki/concepts/attractor-dynamics.md]]`, `[[wiki/entities/rolls-treves-hippocampal-model.md]]` (the persistent-activity pole); `[[wiki/concepts/working-memory.md]]` (the hold/use cost split) | `T86`, `T2`, `T87` | — | Retrieval fidelity falling with the retention interval in a BIRM instance — a decay `D167` says does not exist would put the memorandum back into a state variable and make `Hippocampus` a synaptic store after all |

| D219 | **`T103` was settled by `D27` before the row was read, and settled as position B — and BIRM additionally instantiates the third position the row states and neither side takes.** The row disputes what the common component of control is: **inhibition** (a higher-order factor over inhibition, updating and shifting predicts the inhibition factor almost perfectly, and a bifactor reparameterization yields updating-specific and shifting-specific factors but **no inhibition-specific factor**, so the variance shared across stopping tasks *is* the variance shared across all control tasks) or **goal maintenance and biasing** (the same statistics follow from a controller whose only primitive is an actively held goal that biases processing — inhibition tasks load hardest because a lapsed goal lets the prepotent response win by default — and the several "inhibitions" dissociate, some not being neural inhibition at all). BIRM is B by construction: `PFC` holds a task model as sustained state and adds excitatory bias into organs already running a competition, so enhance-the-relevant and suppress-the-irrelevant are one operation and there is **no dedicated suppression channel** (`D25`, `D26`); the factorization is one common component plus `θ_upd` and `θ_shift`, with **no inhibition-specific parameters** (`D27`). The bifactor result — including the *absence* it reports — is therefore a prediction of the architecture rather than a fit to it, and the row's discriminating measurement, the between-subject variance of inhibition-specific machinery, is **unaskable** here rather than unmade: there is no such machinery to carry a variance. The row's unstated third position, *a hub with a fixed spoke*, is BIRM's shape on a path the row does not look at: the action path has no suppression channel at all, while the **store** path has a typed erase — `clear` and `suppress` issued on `L_cmd`, an operation code with no tunable parameter, no runtime gain and no learned component (`D32`, `D33`). Suppression exists here exactly once, off the action path, as a fixed spoke under a varying hub. Consequence for §10, and `T4` already carries the fit it belongs in: across a population of trained instances, control-side variance loads on the bias and the erase channel contributes **none** — added as an arm on `T4` | §3.3 `D25`, `D27`, §3.2 `D32` | `SOURCED` | `[[wiki/concepts/control-unity-and-diversity.md]]` (the bifactor reparameterization and the dissociating "inhibitions"); `[[wiki/concepts/cognitive-control.md]]` (bias into a competitive substrate); `[[wiki/concepts/inhibitory-control-of-coding.md]]` (the dedicated-channel alternative BIRM refuses) | `T103`, `T275` | — | An erase channel whose issue rate varies across trained instances and loads on the common factor — a spoke that varies is a second control parameter, and `D27`'s three-axis factorization would then be incomplete rather than exhaustive |
| D220 | **BIRM's installed priors are hard at every site, the row's override channel is on the non-goal list, and one of the two priors is wrong undetectably.** The row — the wiki tension, not §10's **Bx** test — disputes whether an installed prior is a **hard constraint** (core principles operate all-or-none and *delete* candidate structures rather than reweighting them; an entity violating cohesion is never admitted to the object system, and non-object substances never enter it) or a **strong default** (the same principles are false at the smallest and largest scales and are demonstrably overridden — by physics and non-Euclidean geometry in adults, by learning to count in preschoolers, by learning about eating and breathing in children's agent concepts). BIRM is the hard pole at every site and is not free to be otherwise. `H6`'s group is chosen per family and never inferred, and `D20` states the hard pole's own price: a family whose true structure is not of the assumed type is **forced onto it silently**. `D71`'s effect-equivalence codebook is the second prior and has the same shape — fitted in P0, frozen in P1, and irreversible in one direction, since what P0 merges `Cortex` can never unmerge (`D203`). The override channel the row finds in every documented case is an **external symbol system**, which is precisely `D4`/`D126`'s non-goal: the non-embeddable symbolic slice (`G11`) and rewrite-search (`T229`) have no mechanism here, so the defeasible pole is unbuilt rather than rejected. What the row's *mechanism-of-override* question becomes here is a detector question, and BIRM answers only half of it: `H8`'s composition assumption is tested operationally by `D78` (§7), so "do these actions compose at all" is falsifiable, while `H6`'s **which group** has no test and no residual that reports it — `ρ` audits path-invariance *through* the content channel and inherits `O28`'s circularity (`D64`), so a consistently wrong group produces a clean `ρ`. §12 `O3` prices the choice and `G47` prices getting it wrong. An override is therefore blocked twice: nothing to override with, and, for one of the two priors, no signal that an override is due | §6 `D20`, §2.2 `H6`, §7 | `SOURCED` | `[[wiki/concepts/core-knowledge.md]]` (both poles, the all-or-none admission rule, and the documented overrides running through an external symbol system) | `T11`, `G41`, `G47` | — | A residual computable inside BIRM that rises under a mis-assumed group and stays flat under a correct one — a detector would make `H6` defeasible in principle and turn `D20`'s silence into an engineering gap rather than an architectural commitment |
| D221 | **`T47` is answered by wiring rather than by evidence: BIRM is the one-code pole, and the many places the code is read out are the relay's destination table.** The row disputes whether concurrent structural codes are **independent frames** (grid, place and head-direction cells in primary somatosensory and secondary visual cortex arising from a generalized path-integration principle on each area's own self-motion signal, "complementary yet functionally independent" of the entorhinal integrator, and in the columnar version thousands of object-anchored frames voting on a pose) or **registered, or singular** (the only task in which two regions' frame orientations were estimated together finds them *aligned* — entorhinal and ventromedial-prefrontal hexagonal codes over one conceptual space sharing a grid angle, `t21 = 2.18`, each stable across sessions more than a week apart, later extended from a pair to a five-region network all tested against the entorhinal angle — which is equally consistent with one entorhinal code propagated outward). BIRM has **one** carrier `g` (`D63`) under one assumed group per family (`H6`, `D20`), relayed to `Hippocampus` as an address, `PFC` as level context and `Cortex`'s conjunction, with the **destination table owned by the relay and not by the sender** (`D45`, `D65`). `Cortex`'s `n_mod = ⌈log_K Ŝ⌉` modules are a factorization of that one frame (`D85`) and not a population of frames — `D66` leaves BIRM a single fine-scale code with no multi-module bank (`O24`), §3.6 refuses a seventh organ, and no per-frame readout weight exists — so position A's voting has no site and `G43`'s arbitration question does not instantiate. Registration is **unnecessary** rather than achieved: one code is commensurate with itself, and the weighting the registered reading reduces arbitration to is already §4.1's per-edge terminal gain set by `U`. Two consequences. (i) The row's conflict design has one answer here by construction — one orientation, no dissociation — and `D11` re-seeds `g` to a random phase at every `done`, so not even a relative angle survives an episode. (ii) The row's own instrument problem (single units for independence, BOLD for alignment, neither able to address the other) does not transfer: BIRM's frames are declared, so the count is read off §3 and never estimated. | D222 | **`T62` is answered by taking the distribution pole's *form* and refusing its *density*: one contrastive phase pair whose subtracted term is a structural prediction, and the novel-replay observable bought by composition instead.** The row disputes whether a fast associative store holds **instances written in one shot** (the Hebbian outer product closed-form in the patterns, behavioural-timescale plasticity writing a receptive field from a single traversal, and the standing argument that 10⁴–10⁶ exposures cannot be one episode) or **models a distribution learned iteratively** (recall of what was shown is not generation of what was not: fitting `p(x)` forces a `−log Z` term whose gradient is obtainable only by free-running the network, hence two phases, many passes and no closed form). BIRM's store is instances outright — one-shot writes of `(Δ, a_{t−1}, r_t)` at `g_t` (`D76`) into a prestructured scaffold (`D68`), nothing recombining online (`D150`) — and no organ anywhere fits `p(x)`: there is no partition function, no free-running estimate of the model's own correlations and no negative term. What BIRM takes from B is its *shape*, and this is the row's own nearest partial answer instantiated: §5.1's minus/plus phases write `Δ = m⁺ − m⁻` (`D50`), a subtracted term supplied by the **structural prediction** rather than by a sampled expectation, with the wake/sleep switch the two-phase rule cannot pay for replaced by an **endogenous clock**. So BIRM is a single-phase local rule at A's cost, differing from B exactly in what is subtracted — and it therefore does *not* fit a density, because a structural prediction of this step's content is not an estimate of the store's correlations. The observable B is credited with is produced by a different mechanism: §5.3 step 5 composes two stored transitions sharing an endpoint and offers an edge never traversed (`D59`), pruned by commit failure (`D134`) — replay of the never-experienced from stored instances, not from a model sample. `Cortex` does carry a stochastic content channel (`D88`) and it is a conditional predictor, so the row's "one substrate or two write rules" question is dissolved by `D22`: the two writes are in two organs. The forfeit is the term B buys with its second phase — nothing here is weakened by what BIRM generates spontaneously, so the unlearning half of the negative phase has no site: §12 `O70` | §3.2 `D76`, §5.1 `D50`, §5.3 `D59` | `SOURCED` | `[[wiki/entities/boltzmann-machine.md]]` (the `−log Z` argument, the two-phase cost and the single-phase alternative); `[[wiki/entities/hopfield-network.md]]`, `[[wiki/concepts/synaptic-plasticity.md]]`, `[[wiki/concepts/complementary-learning-systems.md]]` (the one-shot pole); `[[wiki/concepts/offline-replay.md]]` (replay of never-experienced trajectories); `[[wiki/entities/fcann.md]]` (the single-phase rule that predicts its own correlations) | `T62` | — | A family whose rollouts need *samples in the style of what was stored* rather than compositions of it — `D59`'s composition covers edges between stored endpoints and nothing else, and a density would then be required rather than declined |
| D223 | **`T90` is position A by construction and B is unrepresentable: one operation code in `PFC`, one reader, and only the effect downstream.** The row disputes whether a working-memory operation is issued by **one controller** (the frontoparietal network is the only one of four cortical communities whose patterns separate all four operations — maintain / replace / suppress / clear at 0.968–0.999 pairwise against 0.53–0.99 elsewhere — with the visual, somatomotor and default-mode patterns read as downstream consequences) or identified by the **conjunction** of several subsystems each carrying a different quotient of the operation set (visual cortex held-vs-not-held, somatomotor cortex singling out `clear`, the default-mode network adding suppress ≠ clear — and not redundantly: somatomotor cortex separates `clear` from `suppress` at 0.762 where visual cortex manages 0.610, so a supposedly driven subsystem carries a distinction its driver's other targets do not). B is unrepresentable rather than refused: it needs several organs each holding the operation set under its own quotient, and `L_cmd` has one reader, `V` carries no address (`D70`) and `L_part` is barred from the action path (§4). Two consequences. (i) The row's bite — where a removal can be verified — is answered by register, and its warning about probing one layer for the control variable applies here to the *store*: a probe reading the operation off `Hippocampus`'s state is reading the wrong register, the same error `D196` catches on `Bg`. (ii) The row's discriminating test, predicting removal success from each community separately and jointly, has no BIRM analogue because there are no communities; what replaces it is a reader audit, and it is `D198`'s move on a second channel — an operation code decodable anywhere but `PFC` and the erase's effect falsifies §4's reader list, not the control/storage split. | D224 | **`T95` is answered by separating parameters from content: `PFC` stores its own parameters and no content, so BIRM predicts the processing pole's lesion signature from the representational pole's learning rule.** The row disputes whether the control layer stores knowledge of its own — **representational** (prefrontal cortex stores goal-oriented event sequences with categories, social rules, boundaries and grammars, localized by which posterior region supplies their content; lateral prefrontal cortex argued to descend from motor areas that *store motor programs*, so no gradual path to a contentless module exists; a 275-study survey finding consistent regional differentiation between attention, episodic memory, working memory, language and semantic memory) or **processing** (a general algorithm independent of what it processes: adaptive coding making prefrontal neurons a temporary task-specific operating space with little regional specialization, and a supervisory attentional system that is priority-setting machinery with no knowledge base). BIRM refuses the contentless reading on a decision already taken: `PFC`'s task model is **its own parameters**, fit online in P2 by reward-gated association among co-active `PFC` units with `Cortex` frozen throughout (`D168`), so experience writes something into `PFC` that survives ablating the organ supplying its input — which is the row's `Closes when` satisfied *by construction* for parameters. But what is stored is a **bias pattern over organs already competing** (`D25`, `D26`) and never content: no adjacency matrix (`D205`), no goal-indexed plan library and no option set (`D101`), `O` denied at every level (`D31`), a single content afferent and therefore no partition by content class (`D198`), and a model **swapped rather than accumulated** (`D200`, `D217`). So the row's dichotomy is mis-cut for BIRM and the answer is *both, on different objects*: representational at the weight layer, processing at the content layer. The signature this predicts is a crossed one and it is already `T5`'s: a `PFC` lesion yields context-inappropriate but confidently executed actions **while every content store probes intact** — position A's storage claim and position B's lesion prediction in one system, which no source in the row reports together. Two further consequences. (i) The row's evolutionary criterion has no purchase here — `PFC` is authored, not descended — so the argument that forbids a contentless module is not inherited. (ii) The reduction that a process *is* a set of representations held active is instantiated exactly once, and not in `PFC`'s favour: the one thing BIRM holds by recurrence is the task model, and the memoranda are held by nothing (`D217`). Unpriced, and it is the row's own "nothing measures how much is stored": `PFC`'s task-model capacity, swap cost and acquisition rate are the one organ dimension `D85` does not derive — §12 `O71` | §3.3 `D168`, `D25`, §10 `T5` | `SOURCED` | `[[wiki/concepts/controller-knowledge-vs-process.md]]` (both poles, the five criteria, and the discriminating observation); `[[wiki/concepts/cognitive-control.md]]` (bias into a competitive substrate); `[[wiki/concepts/control-unity-and-diversity.md]]` (the operation axis BIRM factorizes on instead) | `T95`, `G51` | — | A `PFC` lesion in a BIRM instance that also degrades stored content — a controller holding content would make `D25`'s bias-only mechanism and `D31`'s denial of the observation bus jointly false |
| D225 | **`T178` is imposed, and not by preference: the discovering pole's mechanism is denied and the imposing pole's register here is the write interval rather than the spectrum.** The row disputes whether a multiscale temporal decomposition should be **discovered** (one initialisation interval on one linear time-invariant recurrence, stacked twenty deep, and the decomposition appears anyway — first-layer kernels covering a few steps while last-layer kernels span all 16,384 positions at varying frequencies, state of the art on all six Long Range Arena tasks and the only model above chance on Path-X, with nothing designed in) or **imposed** (a learned undecimated wavelet cascade into `S+2` bands, one state-space model per band with eigenvalues initialised into disjoint intervals and an input-dependent mixer, at matched parameters and matched total state, gaining 3.4 points on ListOps and the best sCIFAR, ImageNet-1K and PTB-XL numbers in its tables — with band selection alone worth 24.8 points over per-position gating). BIRM cannot take the discovery pole at all: `g` advances by a **single** recurrence with a computed operator (`D63`), the front end is one feedforward pass with no feedback into perception (`D71`, `D164`), and depth of top-down channel is not a spendable capacity axis here (`D175`, `O56`) — there is no stack across whose layers a hierarchy could appear. Cheapest close is a measurement, not a channel: run a family in which one action is known-bad and the rest ambiguous, and score BIRM against an agent given a per-action bonus at matched total exploration. | O66 | **Re-goaling is BIRM's stated payoff for decoupling and no §10 row measures it** | §3.1, §10 | `T142`, `G28` | No — but it is the unscored half of a decision already taken | §1's success statement is over held-out instance-graphs of a **seen** family, so every acceptance test holds the objective fixed and the decoupled model's whole advantage — the same transition structure serving a new objective at query time — is asserted. Cheapest close: change the reward on a trained family, re-fit `PFC` only with `Cortex` frozen, and report steps-to-criterion against a control retrained end-to-end; S1's budget sweep applies, since at one budget "the structure transfers" and "the controller relearned it" look identical. `D207` |
| O67 | **Nothing can move BIRM's representation inside an episode** — off-manifold learning is deferred to §5.3 and the deferral is unmeasured | §3.1 `D23`, §9 P3 | `T79`, `G14` | No — a forfeit with a stated boundary | The redesign is refused: an online gradient path into `Cortex` reintroduces exactly the interference `D22`'s two-organ split buys off, and `D23` freezes the codes even on the one channel that does write. So the cheapest close is a **measurement of the size of the forfeit**, not a mechanism: run a family containing instances whose solution needs a distinction `Cortex`'s codes do not carry alongside instances that need none, with the rest period withheld in both, and report the gap. `D214` |
| O68 | **The replay direction mixture is not a knob here** — the wiki uses the forward:reverse ratio to set whether the consolidated map stores the policy or the geometry, and BIRM has no actuator for it | §5.3 `D57`, `D59`, `D60` | `T80` | No | Add a direction weight to step 5's draw and sweep it on one family, scoring the policy arm (return under a fixed goal) against the geometry arm (`D5`'s re-goaled transfer). Uniform coverage and a stale tag (`D57`, `D60`) make the ratio an emergent statistic today rather than a setting, so the sweep needs a register that does not exist. `D215` |
| O69 | **The store's quota wire has no law** — `Hippocampus` reports occupancy to `Neuromodulators` (§3) and none of the eight laws reads it (`D42`), so load is measurable and uncontrolled | §3.2, §3.5 `D42` | `G42`, `G32` | No — a wire with no consumer | Write a ninth law on it and sweep the sign. The candidate with a stated mechanism is *occupancy high ⇒ transport ration up* (`D58`(i)), which spends the store rather than resizing it; the sizing route is barred, `N_slots` being derived from `Ŝ` (`D85`). `D226` |
| O70 | **No negative phase anywhere** — nothing in BIRM is weakened by what BIRM generates spontaneously, so the density pole's one term has no site | §5.1 `D50`, §5.3, §3.1 `D88` | `T62` | No — a forfeit with a stated boundary | Route commits tagged `generated` (`D61`, `D173`) back through §5.3 step 4 with a negative sign and measure `Cortex`'s probe error. What blocks it as written: transport is a positive gradient step on a *drawn* candidate (`D57`), and `D173`'s provenance bit stops at `Hippocampus` (`O55`), so the sign has nothing to key on downstream. `D222` |
| O71 | **`PFC`'s task-model store has no capacity** — how many task models are held, what a swap costs, and what the acquisition rate is, is the one organ dimension `D85` does not derive | §3.3 `D168`, `D217` | `G51`, `T95` | No — for interpreting `T5`, not for building | Train one instance across `n` task sets, swap between them, and read the blend rate (`T5`'s hazard) against `n`. The number is BIRM's answer to "nothing measures how much the controller stores", and until it exists the representational half of `D224` is asserted at one item. `D224` |
| O72 | **BIRM has no re-derivation path** — a dimension neither written to a `Hippocampus` slot nor on the live commit is unrecoverable inside the episode, so late availability is bought only by re-observation | §4.1 `D230`, §3.2 | `T274`, `G12` | No — a forfeit with a stated boundary | The redesign is barred: re-deriving a lapsed dimension needs either a sustained content code (there is none — `Bg` is content-free, `D196`) or a transform on **W** (`D47` forbids it). So the cheapest close is a measurement of the size of the forfeit, not a mechanism: run a family whose task queries a stimulus dimension `k` steps after it was last committed, and report the extra environment steps BIRM spends re-observing against an agent given a persistent latent for that dimension. If the gap is inside §1's budget the forfeit is an accounting entry; if it is not, the missing object is a second persistence tier and `D32`'s write-enable is under-issued rather than the architecture wrong. `D230` |
